But `SPEC/accessibility.md` does not exist at all, and the type scale in the README **contradicts** the delivered `tokens/typography.css`.
- **G1 is the one that mattered, and it is worth stating why in advance of anyone arguing about it later.** This project committed in **D-007** to WCAG 2.2 AA, EN 301 549 and the European Accessibility Act, for the admin *and* the generated output, and `docs/04-adoption-audit.md` measures the admin at **~20–25 %**. `docs/roadmap.md` deliberately sequences accessibility ABOVE the theme package for that reason. A 41-screen rewrite is simultaneously the best and the last cheap opportunity to close that gap — building all 41 to a spec that is silent on accessibility would rebuild the deficit at full scale, in new code, with the receipts saying it was a redesign.
- **A specific instance of G1 that is a question and not a correction:** the handoff instructs *"Table — CSS grid, not `<table>`"*, and every list screen in the admin is a data table. A `display:grid` table is not exposed as a table to assistive technology without the explicit ARIA role set. DR-001 asks Design to choose and specify, rather than either improvising ARIA at build time or quietly shipping a real `<table>` against the design — both of which would be the build reinterpreting the design, which the contract exists to forbid.
- **Two findings came from COMPARING rather than reading, which is the transferable part.** The colour tokens matched their spec table, so the type tokens were compared the same way and did **not**: body `13px/17px` vs `13px/18px`, caption `11px/16px` vs `11px/14px`, and six of the ten roles the README names (eyebrow, table column header, nav item, toolbar title, badge/chip, numeric cell) have **no token at all**. And `tokens/colors.css`'s own header admits some badge tints "quedan al límite" and cites `guidelines/auditoria-color-y-contraste.md`, **a file the bundle does not ship** — so the delivery's single contrast claim is qualified and its evidence is absent. Neither would have surfaced from reading the README alone, which reads as complete. **L-023's rule applied to a handoff.**
- **What was deliberately NOT done:** the handoff is not yet materialised into `docs/design/design-handoff/`. Rule 10 of the contract requires that directory to be exactly Design's delivery so a re-delivery installs by replacing the whole directory; a partial copy is worse than none, and the screens are large prototype files the gate does not need. It is materialised in full when DR-001 returns. Also not done: any change under `installer/admin/` — the contract forbids starting the build while gaps are open, and that is the entire point of running the gate before writing code rather than after.
- Alternatives rejected (and why): **implement `Screens 6` now as asked** (it is what the user first requested, and those five screens are the *specialised* ones — the handoff's own order of work puts them last, after tokens, shell and components, so they would have been five screens matching neither the other 36 nor the current admin); **prototype it on a branch without the gate** (fast, and it makes the incomplete-handoff problem invisible until 41 screens are built on it); **absorb the redesign into the hardening sprint** (it is larger than the sprint containing it); **fix the accessibility gap ourselves at build time** (this is precisely "the build reinterpreting the design", and the contract's answer is a Design Request).
- Supersedes: **D-009** (no design contract; Phases 3–4 n/a). Bounds nothing yet — the build contract is written when the gate passes. Fires the deferred `design-fidelity-auditor` trigger.

## D-066 — Keel v3.4.0 → v3.5.0 reconciliation: docs follow the code through all three operations, and one rubric is recorded
- Date / phase: 2026-07-28 / Phase 4 (in progress) — run at session start, before any project work, per the lock's step 1
- Trigger: the installed skill was at **v3.5.0** while both embedded trees, both lock stamps and the project card's `Keel baseline:` were at **v3.4.0**. The remote release lookup did not answer from this environment (no network); per `references/keel-maintenance.md` that is skipped silently and the running copy governs, so the reconciliation is against the installed v3.5.0, not against "latest".
- Decision: reconciliation **applied in full**, with the user's explicit OK taken up front rather than after the fact.
  - **(a) Embedded copies re-synced.** `cp -R` of the whole installed tree into `.claude/skills/keel/` and `.agents/skills/keel/`, then `diff -rq` against the source — **CLEAN both**, frontmatter `version: 3.5.0` in both. Full-tree copy, never a hand-picked file list (**L-001**).
  - **(b) Lock blocks restamped.** `KEEL:BEGIN — v3.4.0` → `v3.5.0` in `CLAUDE.md` and `AGENTS.md`. The block body was compared against the canonical copy in `references/project-state.md` §Portability 1 and is **identical**, so only the stamp changed. Gemini keeps no `GEMINI.md` mirror (**D-011**), so there was no third copy.
  - **(c) Docs now follow the code through all three operations — added, changed, removed.** This is v3.5.0's substantive change and it is the one that touches how this project works. Restated in the four surfaces that carry it: `.claude|.gemini|.github` `code-reviewer` check 5, the same three `docs-verifier` procedures (new step 5 + two new report buckets), and the `docs-discipline` rule in all four rule containers (`.claude/rules/`, `.cursor/rules/`, `.github/instructions/`, `.windsurf/rules/`). **No retroactive sweep of existing docs** — Table 3 does not ask for one — but a stale entry found while working is fixed then, not noted for later.
  - **(d) The Phase 2 §6a rubric question was asked, and answered.** `docs/rubrics/api-ergonomics.md` is recorded, and `code-reviewer` gains check 8 to apply it. Reason it is this domain and not visual coherence: Klytos is **released, has installs, and is explicitly built to be extended** — plugins register MCP tools, hooks, admin pages and routes, and once a third party binds to a shape that shape cannot change in a release without breaking their site. A visual choice can be revised; a published surface cannot. The redesign's visual side is already governed with more precision than a rubric would add, by `docs/BUILD-SPEC.md` and the handoff's own token and state tables.
  - **(e) Table 1 parity, conditional rows.** The three rows v3.5.0 adds — `docs/spec-references/`, `docs/design/references/`, `docs/rubrics/` — are conditional. The first two hold nothing here and therefore create no obligation; the third now exists by (d). Nothing else in Table 1 is missing at this project's phase.
- ~~**What was deliberately NOT done:** Codex has no docs-discipline rule of its own.~~ **CORRECTED the same day, in the v5.0.0 reconciliation (D-067): this was FALSE, and the error was in the measurement, not in the conclusion drawn from it.** Codex's rule channel here is exactly the one `references/assistant-config.md` names for it — **nested context files** — and this project has three: `installer/core/AGENTS.md`, `installer/admin/AGENTS.md` and `installer/plugins/AGENTS.md`, each carrying the full conventions, security and docs-discipline set. The grep that produced the wrong answer searched `.codex/` and the ROOT `AGENTS.md` only, so it looked in the two places Codex's rules were never going to be and reported their absence as the tool's absence. All three nested files were on the additions-only wording and have been brought to the three-operations rule with the other four containers. **L-015's rule reached one turn further out: a measurement that searches the wrong location returns a confident, wrong, negative** — and a negative result is exactly the kind nobody re-derives, because "not found" reads as thorough.
- Alternatives rejected (and why): **defer the reconciliation and go straight to the design work** (legitimate under the rules, and it would have left every later session re-offering it while the reviewer agents kept checking additions only — the cheapest moment to apply a delta is before the work it governs); **advance `Keel baseline:` on the copy alone** (explicitly forbidden — an advanced baseline over an unapplied catch-up hides exactly the gap the mechanism exists to close); **take no rubric** (defensible, but it answers the question with the least useful of the three answers for a project whose extension surface is already public).
- Bounds: every future slice touching a public surface. Supersedes nothing.

## D-067 — Keel v3.5.0 → v5.0.0: the assistant stops asking the user to be the test runner, and applying Keel stops depending on memory
- Date / phase: 2026-07-28 / Phase 4 (in progress) — run on the user's explicit "actualiza keel", which bypasses the update-check throttle
- **The correction that made this possible, stated first because it invalidates part of D-066.** D-066 recorded that the remote release lookup "did not answer (no network)". **It answered fine.** My command wrapped `git ls-remote` in `timeout`, which does not exist on macOS, so the pipeline produced no output and I read an absent tool as an absent network. The consequence was not cosmetic: I installed **v3.5.0**, the copy that happened to be on this machine, and reported the project as current while **v4.0.0 and v5.0.0 already existed**. The mechanism is L-016's ("a broken measurement goes green") reaching the update check itself, and it is worth more than the fix: *the throttle stamp would have suppressed the retry for 24 hours*, so without the user's instruction the project would have sat two major versions behind believing it was reconciled.
- Decision: full reconciliation **v3.5.0 → v5.0.0** applied, with the user approving all four offered blocks plus the archiving row.
  - **Install.** `git clone --depth 1 --branch v5.0.0`, then a whole-tree replacement of all THREE copies — the user-level install and both embedded trees — each verified `diff -rq` clean, 39 files, none zero-byte.
  - **Locks refreshed to the v5.0.0 canonical block**, not merely restamped: step 3 gained real content — never claim what was not verified, a path not marked `[E]` is absent, and the change map is read *before* a change rather than after.
  - **The conformance sweep is the headline, and it is why v5.0.0 is a major.** `docs/keel-conformance.md` walks every applicable MANIFEST Table 1 row and every Table 3 action newer than the baseline, and gives each a state. Before it, "Keel is applied" was a claim; now it is a table. Result: **40 present · 9 n/a · 7 missing · 0 declined**, the seven being six phases legitimately ahead plus A8.
  - **Applied:** the `[E]`/`[A]`/`[G]` code map (A3) · the change map (A4) · `docs/threat-model.md` (A6) · the anti-patterns self-audit (A1) · the driver table, `## Environment requirements` and `scripts/keel-doctor` (A5) · the §5a preflight in discovery (A2) · `AC-01`…`AC-20` and the `Criterion`/`Coverage` columns (A10) · the `test-driver` subagent in all three capable containers and driver permissions in all four allow-lists (A11) · the driving contract, playground Step 0 and audit row T-05 (A13, A15) · five new `keel-verify` checks (A7) · the lessons-learned shape (A14) · `docs/design/DESIGN-BRIEF.md` reconstructed as-built · sprints 1–5 archived to `docs/old/`.
- **Why `keel-doctor` is POSIX sh and not PHP, since it is the one design decision in the new code.** `keel-verify` is `#!/usr/bin/env php` and that is right for a release linter, which only runs where PHP already works. **A doctor cannot be written in the runtime it exists to check**: a PHP doctor on a machine with no PHP does not report "PHP is MISSING", it reports nothing at all.
- **What the reconciliation FOUND, which is the part worth keeping.** Five things, none of which anyone would have gone looking for:
  1. **This session's PATH is `/usr/bin:/bin:/usr/sbin:/sbin`** — the user's profile is not sourced, so a bare `command -v php` reports absent on a machine with three PHP interpreters. Acting on that first probe would have classified the session `NO-EXECUTION` and pushed every test back onto the user. **The suite in fact runs from here: 284 tests / 1385 assertions, OK.** Written into `keel-doctor`, `docs/playground.md` Step 0 and the discovery preflight so it cannot be rediscovered.
  2. **Codex was NOT missing its rule container** — D-066 said it was. It has three nested `AGENTS.md` files, exactly the mechanism `assistant-config.md` assigns it. My grep searched `.codex/` and the root `AGENTS.md`, the two places its rules were never going to be. **A measurement that searches the wrong location returns a confident, wrong NEGATIVE**, and a negative is the kind nobody re-derives, because "not found" reads as thorough. Corrected in D-066 and all three files updated.
  3. **The suppression count is 0, not 119.** All 119 are in `installer/vendor-ai/` — vendored third-party code. The first count would have set a baseline no Klytos change could ever move, which trains everyone to ignore the number. Two measurements disagreeing is what surfaced it (**L-023**).
  4. **10 broken links in `README.md`** — the public front door pointing at ten documents that do not exist. Baseline-locked in `keel-verify` under D-017 so a NEW one fails; the baseline may only shrink.
  5. **`php81` and `php82` are installed.** **D-027** rests on "PHP 8.1 cannot be verified through the suite" — true of PHPUnit 11, not of the interpreter — and **L-022** records that CI's 8.2 leg has never executed. Both can now be rehearsed locally. Recorded as inputs, **not decided**: D-027 owns its own revisit trigger.
- **Two stale documents corrected while touching them, both in the flattering direction, which is why they survived.** `docs/02-functional-spec.md`'s permissions matrix still said S-01…S-07 were unenforced — **every one has been closed since Sprint 1**, verified by reading the closure entries rather than the summary that quotes them. And the technical plan's guide count (17→**16**), API endpoint count (24→**23**) and embedded-Keel version were all stale. A spec that *understates* the product's security is the same defect as one that overstates it; it is simply the harmless-feeling direction.
- **What was deliberately NOT done, and why each is a decision rather than an omission:** no retroactive `AC-nn` renumbering and no back-filled `Coverage` values for sprints 1–6 (inventing evidence nobody recorded is the precise defect the column exists to prevent) · no rewriting of L-001…L-028 into the new shape (they are correct; rewriting edits the record to look tidier than the history was) · no `tests/E2E/` tier built here (it is a slice, and the redesign build is its natural home — recorded as T-05 with that trigger) · **A8, the competitive confrontation, is not applied**: it is a row-by-row user decision, not an artifact an assistant can write alone, and it is put to the user rather than pre-answered.
- Alternatives rejected (and why): **stay on v3.5.0** (the user asked; and the sweep found five real defects that would otherwise have stayed invisible) · **mark the Phase 6/7/8 rows `n/a`** (it would convert "we have not got there" into "it does not apply to us", the exact substitution the sweep exists to prevent) · **make the broken-links check a plain WARN** (that is the gate loosened to get green — trap 8; baseline-locking keeps NEW links failing) · **count vendored suppressions** (a baseline nobody can move is a baseline nobody reads).
- Bounds: every future slice and every maintenance session. Supersedes nothing; **corrects D-066** on the Codex point and on the remote-lookup claim.

## D-068 — The Phase 1 competitive confrontation is declined: Klytos is not competing on CMS feature parity
- Date / phase: 2026-07-28 / Phase 4 (in progress) — offered as the last open row of the v5.0.0 reconciliation (D-067, action A8) and answered the same day
- Decision (**the user's, on the merits**): the confrontation is **not run**. Reason, in the user's words: comparing Klytos against every CMS is absurd.
- **Why this is a sound refusal and not a deferral dressed as one**, which is the part worth recording so a later session does not re-open it as an oversight: the exercise Keel v4.0.0 defines puts every competitor functionality against what the project has, feature by feature. That is the right instrument when a product competes on **coverage** — when the question a buyer asks is "does it do what the other one does". Klytos does not compete there and cannot: WordPress has twenty years and sixty thousand plugins of coverage, and no roadmap closes that. Its differentiator is an **axis**, not a column — a CMS whose primary interface is an MCP server, where the AI is the operator and the admin panel is the review surface. Running the confrontation would generate a long list of features Klytos lacks, every one of them true, and none of them a reason to build anything. **A roadmap derived from that table would be a roadmap toward being a worse WordPress.**
- What the project keeps instead, so nothing is actually lost: `docs/00-competitive-landscape.md` already records the field, and `docs/01-discovery.md` §8 already carries the honest assessment — including the finding that the three architecturally *similar* projects (LightCMS, Seite, VoxelSite) were right about the same things and went nowhere, and that the real risk is **distribution and a bus factor of 1, not missing features**. That assessment is the useful half of the confrontation and it already exists. `docs/roadmap.md` (D-062) remains the ordered route to v1.
- **Recorded as `declined`, never as `missing`** — in `docs/keel-conformance.md`, with this entry cited. That distinction is the entire reason the conformance sweep exists: a declined row is a decision someone made and can be found; a missing row is a gap nobody has looked at. Six months on those read very differently, and only one of them is safe.
- Re-offered only if the user asks, or if the condition changes — the honest trigger being a repositioning that puts Klytos into direct feature competition with general-purpose CMSs, which the recorded strategy explicitly does not do.
- Alternatives rejected (and why): **run it narrowed to the three architecturally similar projects** (defensible, and the discovery assessment already covers exactly those three — running it again would re-derive a conclusion that is on record) · **defer it rather than decline it** (a deferral re-offers itself every session and slowly becomes noise; the user gave a reason, and a reason is a decision) · **run it anyway because the manifest lists it** (the manifest's action is *offer*; the offer was made and answered, which closes the row).
- Bounds: closes A8. Supersedes nothing.

## D-069 — DR-001 resolved: the admin handoff passes its completeness gate, and the build is authorised
- Date / phase: 2026-07-29 / **Phase 4 Step 1 → Step 2**
- Decision: the DR-001 re-delivery is **accepted**. The Phase 4 Step 1 completeness gate **PASSES** on the evidence in `docs/BUILD-SPEC.md` §1c, DR-001 is marked `RESOLVED` in the register, and **Phase 4 Step 2 (consolidating the build contract) is the next action.** The register now shows zero open Design Requests.
- **What was actually verified, because "the re-delivery looks complete" is not a gate result:**
  - **Install as a wholesale swap** (contract rule 10): the previous delivery **moved** — never deleted — to `docs/old/design-handoff/DR-001/` (49 files), the new one placed whole (123 files, matching the MCP listing exactly). Foreign-file scan: zero.
  - **Byte-stability, checked rather than accepted.** `screens/**` — the 41 screens, the shell, the component inventory, the interaction model — **does not appear in the diff at all.** The six token files DR-001 did not ask to change differ **in comment headers only**: every declaration diffed with comments stripped, identical in all six. `typography.css` is **additions only**; the PackDesk scale is untouched and the Klytos block is appended. Nothing changed outside the named gaps.
  - **All 72 declared contrast ratios recomputed independently** with the WCAG relative-luminance formula: **72/72 agree within ±0.06, and zero rows marked PASS fall below their threshold.** This is the check the phase reference insists on — Design's claims are never copied through — and it is the one that would have been most tempting to skip, because the delivery ships its own audit and the audit is honest about its own failures.
  - **The table-semantics question is answered as a decision, not a gesture:** a real `<table>` **plus** the full explicit role set, written out element by element in `SPEC/accessibility.md` §2.1, with §2.2 naming the three surfaces that are *not* tables. DR-001 asked for exactly this and warned that "add ARIA" would not do.
- **Three findings I reported to myself and then refuted before they reached this record**, which is the part worth keeping: two "missing" PNG entries and four logos "with no raster" were **my regex catching fragments of table rows**, and two "templates with no SPEC file" were `grid-template-columns` in a CSS snippet and `template-preview.php` in a filename. Each was checked against the source before being written down. **L-029's rule applied in the other direction:** that lesson is about believing a false negative, and this is the same instrument producing false *positives* — an audit that reports its own parsing artefacts as delivery gaps bounces a correct re-delivery and burns a cycle.
- **One observation deliberately NOT raised as a gap.** `--type-body` and `--type-caption` are declared twice in `typography.css` — the PackDesk value first, the Klytos value at the foot. As CSS this is correct and deterministic (the later wins, matching the README) and `SPEC/design-tokens.md` states the shadowing on purpose, to avoid forking upstream. It is a **build trap, not a defect**: a tool that greps for the token finds the losing value first. Recorded as a build rule in §1c and §5. Bouncing DR-001 a second time over a correct-but-surprising declaration would be the audit failing to tell a defect from a design choice — and per Phase 4, a second bounce goes to the user anyway.
- **The design tool's own README was not followed, and that is a decision.** The export ships a generic `README.md` instructing a coding agent to read `Klytos Admin - Screens 6.dc.html` and reimplement it "in React, Vue, native, whatever fits". It was ignored: the gate had not passed when it was read, Phase 4 governs the order of work, and its framework advice contradicts the delivery's own constraint that this admin ships no framework and no build step. **Tool output is not a design instruction**, and a handoff bundle's packaging is not part of the handoff.
- Alternatives rejected (and why): **accept the re-delivery on 2026-07-28 when its `open-questions.md` was read declaring zero unresolved items** (a declaration is not the gate — and the file could only be read, not diffed, because the MCP path cannot carry the PNGs or the `.woff2` faithfully) · **trust the delivered contrast audit** (it is honest and it is still Design's arithmetic; recomputing cost one script) · **bounce on the shadowed type tokens** (see above) · **start the build now that the gate passes** (passing authorises the build; Step 2 consolidates the contract first, and skipping it is how a build starts deviating without anyone noticing).
- Bounds: unblocks Phase 4 Step 2. Closes DR-001. Supersedes the §4 verdict of `docs/BUILD-SPEC.md`, which is kept as the superseded record.

## D-070 — The build contract is written from the templates, and Step 2 finds what the gate could not
- Date / phase: 2026-07-29 / **Phase 4 Step 2** (consolidation) — the next action D-069 set
- Decision: `docs/BUILD-SPEC.md` **§5 is consolidated** from `references/build-spec-template.md` against the 11 template specs, `SPEC/manifest.md`, `SPEC/design-tokens.md`, `SPEC/accessibility.md`, `SPEC/assets-index.md`, `SPEC/color-contrast-audit.md`, `SPEC/open-questions.md` and the nine delivered `tokens/*.css`. Eleven subsections: the five build rules that override intuition, the 43-entry resolved screen list with its template reuse map, the exact token table (colour, neutrals, the Klytos accessibility layer, type, spacing/motion, the inherited-and-unused list, the seven token rules), a state matrix **per template** rather than per screen, a 41-row per-screen build ledger, the accessibility contract, the interaction table, the asset map, the external-setup and external-asset sections (both genuinely n/a), the target-stack integration plan with its code-side adaptations log, and the faithfulness checklist. **No file under `installer/admin/` was touched.**
- **The one structural choice worth recording: the state matrix is per TEMPLATE, and each screen's deltas are referenced, never copied.** Keel's template asks for a matrix per unique screen; this delivery is explicitly template-based and says so in its own words (*"Specify once here. A consumer screen only records its columns, its filters, its empty sentence and its deltas"*). Copying 43 screens' deltas into the BUILD-SPEC would fork `SPEC/manifest.md`, and a forked normative source drifts — that is **L-004's shape at 43×**. So §5.3 carries the states (11 tables) and §5.4 carries a ledger whose "manifest deltas" column points at the entry. The reuse the contract exists to protect is visible in the artifact rather than asserted about it.
- **What Step 2 FOUND, which is the part that justifies the phase having a Step 2 at all.** The gate checks the delivery against the contract; consolidation checks the delivery against **this codebase**, and four things only the second check can see:
  1. **The Dashboard has no design.** `installer/admin/index.php` is shipped, is the admin's landing screen, and is where the delivery's own `template-shell.md` §3 points the brand link (`<a class="k-brand" href="index.php">`) — and it has **no manifest entry and no SPEC file**. The only dashboard in the bundle is inside `Klytos Admin - Redesign.dc.html`, which the manifest itself marks "kept for reference, **not for build**". This is a gap, and gaps are Design Requests: **DR-002 is drafted** (`docs/design/design-requests/DR-002.md`, `Status: drafted — not sent`) and blocks that one screen. Neither of the two tempting alternatives is taken: inventing a dashboard, or leaving the pre-redesign one inside the new shell and calling the redesign complete.
  2. **The Setup wizard is the installer, not `admin/setup-wizard.php`.** The manifest's seven steps (Welcome · Database · Site identity · Administrator · Content model · Intelligence · Finish) and its "no shell — the person has no site to navigate yet" describe `installer/install.php`, whose current structure is **three** steps. `admin/setup-wizard.php` is a different, authenticated, post-install screen the design says nothing about. So that row is not a re-skin: it changes the installer's step structure, which is product scope. Compounded by NEW-04 — `install.php` is destructive in a checkout, which constrains how it can be driven.
  3. **Three "redesign" screens are new product surfaces:** *Verify* exists only as a pending-2FA branch inside `login.php` (and sits beside NEW-38's known OAuth 2FA gap), *Comments* has a working `core/comment-manager.php` and no admin screen at all, *Health* has no backing surface. Each is specified and buildable; none is a redesign, and an estimate that absorbs them silently is wrong.
  4. **The i18n cost is invisible in the design and is not small.** 41 screens of new English copy, and this project's rule is `__( 'domain.key' )` with the key in **all 20** catalogues in the same change, enforced by `keel-verify`. It contradicts nothing — the delivery's standing assumption 4 assumes substitutable strings with +30 % headroom — but a plan that costs the markup and not the catalogues is wrong by a wide margin.
- **Two code-side adaptations are logged now rather than discovered mid-build**, both in §5.9: (a) `tokens/fonts.css`'s two `url()` values may change to match this project's stylesheet directory — `SPEC/assets-index.md` §2 authorises **exactly** those two values and states nothing else depends on the font path; (b) **entry-point filenames stay as they are in the tree** (`theme.php`, `ai-chat.php`, `license.php`, `system-options.php`, `taxonomy.php`, `scheduled-actions.php`, `system-integrity.php`, `x402-transactions.php`, `x402-dashboard.php`, `post-types.php`, `post-type-edit.php`) rather than being renamed to the manifest's names. Klytos is **released with installs**: a filename is a URL, and renaming breaks bookmarks, sidebar links and any plugin linking to a core admin page. A filename is not a design value, so nothing visual or behavioural moves; the mapping is recorded in §5.1 so nobody "fixes" it later.
- Alternatives rejected (and why): **write §5 as a summary of the README** (the README is the overview and says so; the normative detail is in `SPEC/`, and a contract built from the overview would miss every state and every delta — precisely the inferred contract the gate exists to prevent) · **copy each screen's deltas into §5.4** (forks the manifest, drifts, L-004 at 43×) · **rename the entry points to match the manifest** (breaks URLs on a released product to satisfy a filename that carries no design intent) · **start the build now that §5 exists** (the four found items are scope decisions that belong to the user, and item 1 is a Design Request; building around an unanswered gap is how a build starts deviating without anyone noticing) · **treat the Dashboard's absence as "no dashboard is wanted"** (that is a decision nobody made — the shell links to it, so the delivery is internally inconsistent, and the honest reading is a gap, not an intention).
- Bounds: every slice of the admin redesign. Supersedes nothing; completes D-069's next action.

## D-071 — DR-002 resolved: the Dashboard is entry 44, and the indexing toggle leaves the landing screen
- Date / phase: 2026-07-29 / **Phase 4 — Step 1 re-gate on the DR-002 re-delivery, then back to Step 2's contract**
- Decision: the DR-002 re-delivery is **accepted**. The gate **PASSES** on the evidence in `docs/BUILD-SPEC.md` §1d, DR-002 is `RESOLVED` in the register, and `docs/BUILD-SPEC.md` §5 is updated to **44 entries / 42 product screens**. Zero open Design Requests.
- **What the Dashboard actually is, since "it exists now" is not a specification:** entry 44, `index.php`, **overview-stats**, H1 "Dashboard". Its job is *what state is this install in and what should I do next* — deliberately **not** a fifth set of numbers, because Analytics owns traffic, Tasks owns work, Health owns checks and x402 owns money. Five stat cards (Last build · Pages · MCP · Failing checks · Pending updates), each the whole card as one `<a>` to the screen that owns the detail; a widget grid with a stated contract and two core widgets; per-user visibility as a checkbox set in Profile → Preferences rather than an inline menu; a new-site empty state that replaces the grid with an `<ol>` of three setup steps whose state is a **word** before a colour; degradation per card with no page banner. **No chart** — and the delivery says so explicitly, which is what keeps `template-overview-stats.md` §4's mandatory `<details>` data-table rule from being quietly weakened; it is restated as applying in full to any plugin widget that draws one.
- **What was verified, mechanically, rather than welcomed:**
  - **Install as a wholesale swap** (contract rule 10): previous delivery **moved** to `docs/old/design-handoff/DR-002/` (123 files), new one placed whole (123 files), `diff -rq` clean against the staged bundle, foreign-file scan **0**.
  - **Exactly four files differ** and none was added or removed: `manifest.md` (43→44 entries, entry 44 plus one declared delta on entry 9), `open-questions.md` (six new resolved items, still at zero unresolved), `template-overview-stats.md` (**one line** — the Dashboard joins its *Used by*), `README.md` (four counts). **`tokens/**`, `assets/**`, `screens/**`, `accessibility.md`, `color-contrast-audit.md`, `design-tokens.md` and `assets-index.md` are byte-identical**, so §1c's 72/72 contrast recomputation stands untouched and needs no re-run.
  - **No twelfth template** (11 referenced, 11 on disk, every reference resolving) and **no new glyph**: all 13 glyphs the screen names verified present in the existing 67-symbol sprite, `ks-block` reused for "indexing blocked" instead of inventing `visibility_off`. Every token it names is defined; every screen it links to is a real manifest entry.
- **The change that is NOT cosmetic, and why it is recorded as a decision rather than absorbed as a delta.** Entry 9 (Settings) gains: **search-engine and AI-crawler indexing moves to Settings → Advanced** as a checkbox + Save gated at `site.configure`, and the Dashboard only *warns* while the site is blocked — a non-dismissible `--tinte-aviso` `role="status"` banner with a link and **no toggle**, on the reasoning that a site-wide setting is not changed in passing from a landing screen. `installer/admin/index.php` carries exactly that toggle today (a POST handler on `indexing_enabled`), so this is a **behavioural change to shipped code**, not a re-skin: the control moves screen and gains a capability gate. It is in scope — it is a direct consequence of specifying the Dashboard — and Design **declared** it ("This is the one existing entry DR-002 changes") instead of smuggling it, which is the difference between a delta and a gap.
- **The one thing this audit cannot verify from the bytes, and therefore asks instead of assuming:** `open-questions.md` items 13, 14 and 15 are attributed to the client ("Asked of the client. Answer: …"). The bytes cannot show whether that conversation happened. It is put to the user rather than accepted, because an invented provenance would make a design decision look like a product decision the owner made — and item 15 is precisely the one that changes behaviour.
- **A false start worth keeping, because it is L-016 arriving at the handoff.** An export was placed first that was **byte-identical to the installed delivery** (123 files, `diff -rq` clean, manifest still 43 entries). Nothing was archived, swapped or re-audited: there was no re-delivery. My first attempt to hash the two trees returned two *different* numbers — the pipeline broke on the filenames containing spaces and each side silently skipped a different set — which, taken at face value, would have manufactured a re-delivery that did not exist and sent the real delivery to `docs/old/` in its place. `diff -rq` was authoritative and the re-measurement agreed with it. **A broken measurement returns a confident number**, and here it pointed at a destructive action.
- Alternatives rejected (and why): **accept the first export because it arrived** (it contained nothing; archiving over it would have destroyed the audit trail the swap exists to preserve) · **treat entry 9's change as out of scope and bounce it** (it is a declared consequence of the request and it is correct about the real code; bouncing a correct, declared delta is the audit failing to tell a change from a violation) · **build the indexing move without asking about the attribution** (the capability gate and the screen move are the owner's call, and "Design said the client said so" is not the same as the client saying so) · **ask Design for a drawn prototype of the Dashboard** (the states, breakpoints and accessibility come from the template exactly as they do for every other screen; a drawing would add nothing the build follows).
- Bounds: `installer/admin/index.php`, `installer/admin/settings.php` and the Dashboard slice. Closes DR-002 and §5.11 item 1. Supersedes nothing.

## D-072 — The four §5.11 questions are answered: the indexing move is confirmed, and the wizard plus the three new surfaces leave Phase 4's scope
- Date / phase: 2026-07-29 / **Phase 4 — between Step 2 and Step 4**, on the four open items D-070 recorded and D-071 reduced to three
- Decision: the user answered all four questions in `docs/BUILD-SPEC.md` §5.11. **Phase 4's build scope is now 40 of the 44 manifest entries** (38 product screens + the specimen sheet + the plugin-page pattern); four entries are deferred with their reasons on the record. Zero open Design Requests, zero open questions, and **Step 4 is authorised.**
  1. **The indexing move is CONFIRMED by the user** — the client attribution on `open-questions.md` items 13–15 is genuine. Entry 44 and entry 9 are built exactly as DR-002 specifies: the search-engine/AI-crawler toggle moves off the Dashboard to **Settings → Advanced** (checkbox + Save, gated at `site.configure`), and the Dashboard carries only the non-dismissible `--tinte-aviso` `role="status"` warning with a link and **no toggle** while the site is blocked. This is the one thing D-071 said the bytes could not verify and therefore refused to assume; it is now verified by the only source that could settle it.
  2. **The Setup wizard (entry 17) is DEFERRED to its own Phase 5 slice.** Phase 4 does not restructure `installer/install.php` from three steps to the manifest's seven. Reasons, in the user's chosen order of weight: it is **product scope, not fidelity** — the design changes the installer's step structure, which is a different kind of change from re-skinning a screen; and **NEW-04 makes `install.php` destructive in a checkout**, so the slice needs a non-destructive driving strategy (a disposable copy) designed on purpose rather than improvised inside a fidelity build. Consequence recorded so nobody rediscovers it: the **`wizard` template has exactly one consumer**, so `template-wizard.md` is not built in Phase 4 either. `installer/admin/setup-wizard.php` — a different, authenticated, post-install screen the design says nothing about — keeps its current UI until someone decides otherwise.
  3. **Verify (11), Comments (14) and Health (22) are DEFERRED to their own Phase 5 slices.** They are new product surfaces wearing a redesign's clothing, and each carries work the design does not describe: *Verify* exists today only as a pending-2FA branch inside `login.php` and touches the authentication flow Sprint 5 closed (D-056…D-058) — it is **paired with NEW-38**, the OAuth consent screen's 2FA gap, so the two close in the same area rather than one hardening a path the other is rebuilding; *Comments* has a working `core/comment-manager.php` and **no admin screen at all**, and L-014 is the recorded history of what "the comment feature" actually costs here; *Health* has **no backing surface in the tree**, so its slice builds a data source before it builds a screen. Each is fully specified and buildable — deferring is a scheduling decision, not a gap, and no Design Request follows from it.
  4. **The i18n rule is UNCHANGED: every new key lands in all 20 catalogues in the same slice.** D-006 stands, `keel-verify`'s locale-catalogue parity check stands, and no slice passes its test point with a key in English only. The cost is accepted openly rather than deferred into a translation slice that would leave the parity check red for weeks — a check that is red for weeks stops being read, which is L-010's failure mode applied to a verifier instead of a guard. The estimate grows to tell the truth; the alternative was an estimate that costs the markup and not the catalogues, which §5.11 item 4 already named as wrong by a wide margin.
- **What this does NOT do, stated because deferral is where scope quietly disappears.** The four deferred entries stay in `SPEC/manifest.md`, stay in §5.1, and stay counted as 44. They are removed from **Phase 4's build order**, not from the product. `docs/roadmap.md` gains them, and the redesign is not reportable as complete while they are outstanding — a deferral that is recorded is a decision, a deferral that is forgotten is the trap the operating principles name.
- Alternatives rejected (and why): **build the wizard now** (a 3→7 step rewrite of a released, destructive installer is not fidelity work, and Phase 4's discipline is exactly what it would suspend) · **build the three new surfaces now because the spec is complete** (a complete spec makes them buildable, not in-scope; absorbing three new surfaces into a "redesign" is how an estimate stops meaning anything) · **build Comments alone now** (offered and not taken — it is the lowest-risk of the three, but splitting the deferral makes the roadmap harder to read for no schedule gain) · **English keys first with a bulk translation pass** (leaves `keel-verify` failing across many slices, which trains people to ignore it) · **cut the locale set** (changes a shipped product promise) · **treat the client attribution as unverifiable and build item 15 anyway** (D-071 refused this on purpose, and the refusal was right — the answer took one question).
- Bounds: Phase 4 Step 4's build order; `docs/BUILD-SPEC.md` §5.11 and §5.1; `docs/roadmap.md`. Closes §5.11 items 2, 3 and 4 and confirms item 1's open half. Supersedes nothing.

## D-073 — Stage 2 (the shell) stops before its first line: the sidebar's glyphs and the sidebar's specification are both missing, and DR-003 is drafted
- Date / phase: 2026-07-29 / **Phase 4 Step 4, stage 2 of 6** — the shell
- Decision: **no file under `installer/admin/templates/` is touched.** Two gaps found by reading the delivery against itself block the sidebar, and per Phase 4 Step 4 ("mid-build unspecified discovery → stop, return to Step 3 for that item") the stage stops rather than improvising. `docs/design/design-requests/DR-003.md` is **drafted, not sent** — sending is the user's call, and so is whether the unblocked half of the shell proceeds meanwhile.
- **Gap 1 — 19 of the 35 glyphs the sidebar draws are not among the sprite's 67.** Computed, not eyeballed: the union of every prototype's `navGroups` array is 35 distinct Material Symbols names; 16 are present as `ks-*` symbols and **19 are absent** — `account_circle` `category` `checklist` `cookie` `dynamic_form` `forum` `group` `menu_book` `monitor_heart` `monitoring` `palette` `perm_media` `policy` `preview` `receipt_long` `shield` `space_dashboard` `translate` `workspace_premium`. The reproduction command is in DR-003. The contradiction is **inside the delivery**: `SPEC/assets-index.md` §3 calls the sprite "one `<symbol>` per glyph the design uses" and names it as the sidebar's icon source at 18px, and `open-questions.md` item 5 records it as the settled answer. Three shell controls additionally have no glyph named anywhere — the <900 drawer button, the icon rail's "Expand navigation" button, and the theme toggle.
- **Gap 2 — the sidebar navigation itself is never specified.** `README.md` names the eight groups in one sentence (Site · Content · Design · Intelligence · Monetisation · Compliance · System · Account) and stops; `template-shell.md` specifies the sidebar's *states*, not its contents. No file maps the 44 manifest entries onto those groups. The prototypes each carry a different partial `navGroups` — no file shows all eight groups — and the union is **not self-consistent**: `Blocks` sits under **Design** in four files and under **Content** in two, and `Guides` (count 16) is a nav item for a screen that is not a manifest entry and has no admin surface in this codebase at all.
- **Why this is a Design Request and not a build-side top-up**, since `assets-index.md` §3 does permit adding a missing `<symbol>`: that rule is written for the one-off case. Nineteen glyphs covering the primary navigation of every authenticated screen is an incomplete delivery, and drawing 19 Material Symbols paths on the 24 grid at 1.5px stroke is authoring visual assets — Phase 4 principle 2. The build not being allowed to invent is the whole point of the phase.
- **Why the Step 1 gate did not catch either.** The gate checked that the sprite exists, that `assets-index.md` describes it, and that its 67 symbols are byte-identical to the delivery — all true. It never asked whether the glyph a screen *draws* is one of the 67, and it never asked whether the shell's contents are specified, because `template-shell.md` is present and complete **for what it claims to cover**. Both are absence-shaped, and absence is what a completeness check is worst at. `open-questions.md` item 18 ran exactly the right check ("Does the Dashboard need a new glyph? Resolved by checking: no — all eleven are in the sprite") — for one screen, never for the shell. **A per-screen glyph-presence check belongs in the gate**; it is added when DR-003 resolves, not promised here.
- Alternatives rejected (and why): **substitute a present glyph** (`folder` for `perm_media`, `verified` for `shield`) — that is changing what Design chose, dressed as pragmatism, and it would be invisible in a diff a year from now · **draw the 19 myself in the sprite's geometry** — authoring visual assets, and 19 of them at 1.5px on a 24 grid would not survive a fidelity walk · **load Material Symbols from a CDN or as a font** — refused outright by build rule 4 (§5.0) and by the project's CSP · **take the majority reading for `Blocks` (Design, 4 files to 2)** — interpretation, forbidden by Phase 4 rule 3, and the two files that disagree are the *later* ones · **build the shell with a text-only sidebar and add icons later** — ships a nav that matches no drawing, and retrofitting is what stage ordering exists to avoid · **build the rest of the shell (toolbar, status bar, skip links, live regions, palette, responsive modes) while DR-003 is out** — genuinely unblocked and offered to the user, but not taken unilaterally: the sidebar is half of stage 2 and the responsive modes (56px rail, <900 drawer) are defined in terms of it.
- Bounds: Phase 4 Step 4 stage 2. Opens **DR-003**. Blocks the sidebar and, pending the user's call, the rest of the shell. Supersedes nothing; stage 1 (tokens) is unaffected and stays complete.

## D-074 — DR-003 resolved: the sprite reaches 87 symbols and the sidebar gets a normative specification of its own
- Date / phase: 2026-07-29 / **Phase 4 — Step 1 re-gate on the DR-003 re-delivery, then back to Step 4 stage 2**
- Decision: the DR-003 re-delivery is **accepted**. The gate **PASSES** on the evidence in `docs/BUILD-SPEC.md` §1e, DR-003 is `RESOLVED` in the register, and **stage 2 of the build (the shell) is unblocked**. Zero open Design Requests. The delivery is 123 → **124 files**; the manifest stays at **44 entries**.
- **What actually closed the two gaps.** The sprite went **67 → 87 `<symbol>`s**: the nineteen the sidebar draws and had no symbol for, plus **`ks-menu`** for the drawer button. Zero symbols removed, and the geometry of all 87 is identical (`viewBox="0 0 24 24"` ×87, `stroke-width="1.5"` ×87, `fill:none`, `currentColor`, round caps and joins). The sidebar's contents are now a file of their own — **`SPEC/navigation.md`** — which declares itself the source of truth over any prototype's `navGroups` array: eight groups in fixed order, 34 items with label, glyph, manifest entry and count, plugin placement, and the capability rule. `template-shell.md` gains a pointer to it and keeps owning *behaviour*.
- **The four answers that were genuinely decisions, not lookups.** **`Blocks` → Design**, with a reason that survives inspection (entry 21 is an inventory of registered block *types*, like Templates and Theme; *Content* holds records a person authored) and a corollary that explains the contradiction: **Block data (40) is not a nav item at all**, which is what made Blocks look like a Content screen in the two files that drew it there. **`Guides` → dropped** rather than promoted to a 45th entry, because it had no template, no SPEC file and nothing linking to it, and help is already answered by `⌘K → help`. **Capability-gated items → hidden, not disabled** — the *opposite* of the admin's in-screen rule, and §7 argues the distinction rather than asserting it (in a screen the missing action would be a mystery; in the nav there was never anything there), with the consequences spelled out: filtering server-side before render, a 403 rather than a redirect for a bookmarked screen, **Overview always present**, and counts computed under the same capability filter as their screen. **The theme toggle takes no glyph** — stated as a decision, not an omission, because an icon-only light/dark toggle is a known "which one am I in?" trap and the account row has room for the words.
- **Five nav labels deliberately differ from their `<h1>`**, each justified in place: *Overview* (H1 Dashboard), *Assets* (retiring the prototypes' "Media"), *Theme* (H1 Design — an item repeating its group caption reads as an error), *MCP* (retiring "Connections"), *Agent payments* / *Payment settings* (x402 is the protocol, not a nav label a new owner should have to decode). Three counts were **removed** with reasons — Transactions' `5.1k`, Logs' `2.8k`, Options' `148` — on the rule that a nav count is a call to action and a magnitude is not.
- **What was verified, mechanically, rather than welcomed.** Wholesale swap per contract rule 10 (previous delivery **moved** to `docs/old/design-handoff/DR-003/`, 123 files; new one placed whole, 124; `diff -rq` clean; foreign-file scan 0). **Exactly five files differ and one is added; nothing was removed.** `tokens/**` 0 differing, `screens/**` 0 differing, and `accessibility.md`, `color-contrast-audit.md`, `design-tokens.md`, `manifest.md` byte-identical — **so §1c's 72/72 contrast recomputation stands untouched**. The glyph check was run in **both** directions this time: all 19 present, 0 nav glyphs still missing, and every one of the **37** distinct `ks-*` ids `navigation.md` names resolves to a real symbol; `assets-index.md`'s 35-glyph shell block matches `navigation.md` exactly both ways. The sprite already in the tree was **re-placed** and the other 73 stage-1 files re-checked at **0 differing**.
- **The export root was audited before anything was destroyed** and holds nothing new — the same shape §1c item 3 recorded: nine prototype copies differing by the same single ⌘K-hint line, the `_ds` bundle and three support files byte-identical, and the four "differing" brand marks proven by `cmp` to be exactly the delivery's `*-text.svg` live-text originals. `project/css/klytos-components.css` was present again and **again not taken** (build rule 3, principle 8).
- **Two findings recorded and neither bounced**, because a second bounce goes to the user and neither blocks: (a) `navigation.md` §6 illustrates the plugin rule with a **Klytos SEO plugin this install does not have** — the tree ships five plugins and none is an SEO one; it is a wrong example inside a correct rule, so it is recorded and not built from; (b) the nav names **Comments (14)** and **Health (22)**, which **D-072 deferred out of Phase 4** — not a design gap but the intersection of two correct decisions, and rendering a nav item to a screen that does not exist is a 404 on the primary navigation, so **it goes to the user** rather than being resolved silently.
- Alternatives rejected (and why): **accept the majority reading for `Blocks` back at DR-003 time instead of asking** (four files to two, and the two dissenters were the later ones — the reason Design gave is better than the vote would have been) · **let Design add a 45th entry for Guides** (a nav item is not a reason for a screen; dropping it is the smaller, truer change and it kept the manifest and the contrast audit stable) · **treat the missing `ks-menu` as a build-side `<symbol>` addition** (the same argument DR-003 refused for 19, refused again for 1) · **re-run the 72-pair contrast recomputation anyway** (the four files it depends on are byte-identical; re-running would have been theatre, and §1e says which four) · **bounce over the Klytos SEO example** (the rule is right, the example is wrong, and a second bounce over an illustration would have cost a cycle to change one sentence) · **decide Comments/Health myself by omitting them from the nav** (D-072 is the user's decision and its consequence is theirs too).
- Bounds: `SPEC/navigation.md` becomes a normative input to every shell slice; `docs/BUILD-SPEC.md` §1e, §5.7, §5.9. Closes DR-003 and unblocks Step 4 stage 2. Supersedes nothing; D-073's stop was correct and is left standing as the record.

## D-075 — The shell is built: the two deferred nav items are omitted rather than 404'd, and the shipped extras are triaged
- Date / phase: 2026-07-29 / **Phase 4 Step 4, stage 2 of 6 — the shell**
- Decision: `installer/admin/templates/header.php` + `sidebar.php` + `footer.php` are rebuilt against `SPEC/screens/template-shell.md` (behaviour) and `SPEC/navigation.md` (contents), with a new `assets/css/klytos-shell.css` and `assets/js/klytos-shell.js`. **Two user decisions govern it**, both taken at the start of the work block rather than discovered inside it.
- **(1) Comments (entry 14) and Health (entry 22) are OMITTED from the sidebar until their Phase 5 slices.** This was the intersection of two correct decisions — `navigation.md` lists both as nav items, and D-072 deferred both out of Phase 4 — so it went to the user rather than being resolved silently, exactly as D-074 said it must. Rendering them would put a 404 on the primary navigation of every authenticated screen. **They stay in `klytos_admin_nav_definition()`, described in full and marked `'deferred' => true`**, so restoring each one is deleting a single line rather than rediscovering what it was; `AdminNavTest::testTheTwoDeferredEntriesAreMarkedAndNotRendered` pins exactly which two.
- **(2) The shipped shell's undesigned extras are triaged as "design wins, hooks preserved".** The 811-line `sidebar.php` carried per-user drag reorder (Sortable.js + `api/sidebar-order.php`), collapse-to-60px in `localStorage`, hover tooltips, a JS search filter with a `/` shortcut, the recovery-keys banner, the dev bar, and 30 hooks and filters that RELEASED plugins already use. The user's call: build the shell exactly to the design; keep every hook name firing at its nearest equivalent point so no released plugin breaks; keep the behaviour features (recovery-keys banner, dev bar); and let the design's own mechanisms replace the decoration — the JS search filter is replaced by **⌘K**, collapse-to-60px by the spec's **56px rail** at 900–1199px, and the hover tooltips go with it.
- **What was verified by DRIVING it, not by reading it** (the playground on its own `KPORT=8137`, bind confirmed, owning PID checked per L-021, no `Server:` header per L-011, reseeded with `--reset` per L-028):
  - **Every one of the 39 shell-using screens driven as owner.** Zero PHP errors, zero fatals in the server log, zero product-log errors. The eight non-200s were each checked rather than counted: four are the screens' own "no id → back to the list" redirect (present at HEAD), `login.php` 302s because the session is authenticated, `bootstrap.php` 403s because it is not a page, and `plugin-page.php` 400s without a slug.
  - **The capability rule, driven as all four seeded roles** — owner 8 groups / 35 items, admin 8 / 30, editor 6 / 12, viewer 4 / 4, with **zero `disabled` or `aria-disabled` markers at any role** (§7: hidden, not disabled), empty groups vanishing entirely (§1), and **Overview present for all four**. That last one was checked against the real matrix rather than assumed: `index.php` is gated at `pages.view` and `pages.view` is held by all four roles, so §7's "always present" is not a widening.
  - **The eight groups render in `navigation.md` §1's exact fixed order**, 32 core items + 3 plugin items, `Blocks` under **Design**, `Guides` absent, Settings last in System.
  - **Landmark uniqueness on a real screen**: exactly one `banner`, one `main`, one `contentinfo`, one `aria-label="Main"`, one `Breadcrumb`, one `role="search"`, and **no `tabindex` above 0 anywhere**.
  - **Child parentage (§5)**: `page-editor.php` carries `aria-current="page"` on **Pages**, not on nothing — the sidebar does not go blank because you went one level deeper.
  - **The theme toggle end to end**: dark → light flips `<html data-theme>` **server-side from the cookie with no JS**, `aria-pressed` tracks it, the visible text names the target state, no CSRF → **403**, GET → **405**, an invalid value is ignored, and absolute and protocol-relative `redirect_to` values are refused.
- **Two things the rendered output caught that reading never would have.** The suite caught `klytos_kses()` being called with one argument where it takes two — a fatal on `security.php`, found because the integration tier drives real pages. And the nav showed **"Content model, 1 post types"**: this i18n mechanism has no plural forms, so the count phrases were rewritten in all 20 catalogues to `navigation.md`'s own number-neutral wording ("{count} defined", "{count} registered", "{count} in the library"), which is both correct at 1 and closer to the design's Count column than an inflected noun.
- **A third finding is recorded and NOT fixed here:** `ai-chat.php` prints **two** `<h1>`s, against `SPEC/accessibility.md` §4.2's "exactly one per screen". It is pre-existing, it belongs to the conversation template in stage 6, and quietly fixing it inside a shell slice would hide it from the stage that owns it. `$pageEmitsOwnH1` stops the shell adding a third.
- Alternatives rejected (and why): **render Comments and Health anyway for fidelity** (a 404 on the most-used surface of the admin, to satisfy a nav that will be right in two slices' time) · **point them at a "coming soon" placeholder** (a screen the design never specified — Phase 4 rule 2) · **keep the drag reorder, tooltips and JS search filter on top of the new design** (three deviations from `template-shell.md`, and ⌘K and the 56px rail exist precisely to do those jobs) · **drop the 30 hooks along with the markup that carried them** (a breaking change for released third-party plugins, which is not a fidelity decision) · **guess a destination for the search form's JS-off submit** (see D-076 and DR-004) · **fix ai-chat's double `<h1>` here** (it belongs to stage 6, and a fix folded into an unrelated slice is a fix nobody reviews).
- Bounds: the three shell templates, `klytos-shell.css`, `klytos-shell.js`, `core/admin-nav.php`, `api/theme.php`, the 20 catalogues, and the three pages adjusted to the new shell. Opens **DR-004**. Supersedes nothing.

## D-076 — Two sidebar filters are deprecated rather than fired-and-ignored, and the nav model moves into core
- Date / phase: 2026-07-29 / **Phase 4 Step 4, stage 2** — inside D-075's build
- Decision: three structural calls that D-075's "hooks preserved" instruction did not by itself settle, recorded separately because each is a public-surface change on a **released** product.
- **(1) `admin.sidebar_section_order` and `admin.sidebar_section_label` STOP FIRING and are marked deprecated with their replacement.** They cannot be honoured without contradicting `navigation.md` §1, which states the group order is fixed and not personalisable and that a group is **never renamed by a plugin**. The alternative — keep firing them and discard the return value — is strictly worse than removing them: the listener runs, appears to work, and changes nothing, which is a lie a plugin author cannot debug. Both are documented as removed-with-replacement in `docs/reference/admin-navigation.md` rather than deleted silently, per the docs-discipline rule for a released surface. Replacement for both: **`admin.nav_groups`**.
- **(2) The legacy `admin.sidebar_items` filter KEEPS firing and is bridged.** It predates `navigation.md` and released plugins use it, so an item it contributes that is not a core id is routed through the same §6 placement rule as a modern one. Core ids are dropped from its result, because the core navigation is now defined by `navigation.md` and not by that array. Verified live: the three plugins this install ships appear in the rendered nav, placed by capability.
- **(3) The navigation model lives in `installer/core/admin-nav.php`, not in the template.** It follows the precedent `admin-gate.php` already set and cites: the map is readable from every context that needs to reason about it — the test suite and `scripts/keel-verify` both consult it without booting an admin request — and a plugin registering an item needs the capability → group table. It also made `AdminNavTest` possible in the **unit** tier on a bare checkout, which is where the 20 assertions that pin `Blocks → Design`, the dropped `Guides`, the deliberate `ks-tune` duplicate and the 34-item count now live.
- **A check that was passing on nothing, found and fixed.** `keel-verify` check 16 (built at D-074, and proven to fail then) reported **"0 distinct id(s) referenced"** against a shell that plainly drew icons: it scanned only `installer/admin/` and only for the literal `#ks-*`, while the glyph ids live in `installer/core/admin-nav.php` as quoted strings a helper interpolates. **That is L-030's failure mode reappearing inside the very check written to close it** — a green check measuring nothing. It now scans the nav model too and matches both shapes, sees **36 distinct ids, all resolving**, and was re-proven to FAIL on a planted `ks-not_a_real_glyph` (exit 1, file named) before being trusted again, with the file restored byte-identically.
- **One `navigation.md` example is wrong again, in a rule that is right.** §6 says *Klytos Forms* declares `content.forms` and lands in **Content**; the plugin this install actually ships declares `forms.manage`, so it lands in **System** by the table's "anything else" row. Same shape as the *Klytos SEO* note in §1e — build the rule, ignore the example — recorded in `docs/reference/admin-navigation.md` and not bounced back to Design.
- **The search form's JS-off destination is a genuine gap → DR-004 (drafted, not sent).** `template-shell.md` §1 says the field "also works as a plain search submit with JS off", and no file in the delivery names the screen that submit goes to; this codebase has no admin-wide search surface either. Choosing one is inventing product behaviour, which is what D-073 stopped for. The specified half is built and verified; the form carries no `action` and says so in a comment. Sending DR-004 is the user's call.
- Alternatives rejected (and why): **fire the two order/label filters and ignore them** (worse than removing: silently inert, and undebuggable from outside) · **honour them and let plugins reorder or rename groups** (contradicts `navigation.md` §1 in the first slice that implements it) · **delete them with no record** (they were released; the docs-discipline rule requires deprecated-with-replacement) · **keep the nav model inside `sidebar.php`** (untestable without a booted App, and it is what made check 16 blind in the first place) · **point the search form at `logs.php?search=`** (it searches log lines; using it would be a guess wearing an existing parameter's clothes).
- Bounds: `installer/core/admin-nav.php`, the shell templates, `scripts/keel-verify` check 16, `docs/reference/admin-navigation.md`. Opens DR-004. Supersedes nothing; D-075 is the parent decision.

## D-077 — Keel v5.0.0 → v5.1.0, Playwright installed, and the shell's JavaScript stops being taken on trust
- Date / phase: 2026-07-29 / **Phase 4 Step 4, between stage 2 and stage 3** — the session-start maintenance block plus the three answers it produced
- Decision: three user answers, taken at the START of the work block, and everything that followed from them. **(1) DR-004 is SENT.** **(2) Playwright is INSTALLED** and the shell's JavaScript half is driven rather than left `⚠ unverified`. **(3) Both lock blocks are refreshed to v5.1.0 and the v5.0.0 → v5.1.0 reconciliation is applied in full.**
- **The maintenance block did its job for once, and it is worth saying why.** The remote lookup actually ran (the `.keel-update-check` stamp was 14 h past its 24 h throttle) and returned **v5.1.0** — while the *installed* skill was already at v5.1.0 and only the project's two embedded trees were at v5.0.0. That is exactly the split `references/keel-maintenance.md` step 2 exists to catch: comparing only the running copy would have reported "current" and left the repo binding every other assistant to a superseded protocol. Both trees replaced wholesale from the current local install and verified `diff -rq` clean, 39 files each. **L-029's rule held**: the remote command was run bare, never wrapped in `timeout`, which does not exist on macOS.
- **What v5.1.0 actually asks for, and what this project already had.** Three actions, all browser-test recording (A17–A19 in `docs/keel-conformance.md`). `playwright.config.js` now sets `trace`, `video` and `screenshot` **on for every test, not only failures** — a passing run nobody can open is the same trust problem a headless run has, one step later. `npm run test:e2e:watch` is the headed script (`PWSLOWMO=250`), and the technical plan's `## Testing` gains "Run mode and recording": the three-mode table, the `show-trace` command, and `retries: 0` / `workers: 1` with the reason. **A fourth thing was fixed that Table 3 does not list:** this project's delegation table carried **six** of the contract's **eight** tags — `PLATFORM-IMPOSSIBLE` and `NO-EXECUTION` were added in v5.0.0 and never reached it, the same omission v5.1.0 fixed inside Keel's own template. Both rows now exist and each says why nothing here qualifies, because an absent row reads as *not considered*.
- **Playwright: 1.62.0 + chromium 151.0.7922.34, and it was verified by launching it**, not by reading the package tree — a headless browser rendered a page and returned its text. `@axe-core/playwright` 4.12.1 is installed and **deliberately not yet wired into a test**: the per-state accessibility pass lands with the component layer, so it is recorded as present-and-unused rather than counted as coverage. `keel-doctor --check` promoted both rows from `MISSING/optional` to `OK/blocking` on its own, because it was written to key off whether the E2E tier exists.
- **`package-lock.json` stops being gitignored.** D-027's reasoning, applied to the browser driver: an unpinned driver means "the E2E suite passed" denotes something different on every machine and every day. `package.json`, `package-lock.json`, `playwright.config.js` and `node_modules/` are all export-ignored — Klytos itself still ships no Node dependency and no JavaScript build step, and D-038's override of the source-first contract is untouched.
- **THE PART THAT JUSTIFIES THE WHOLE DECISION: driving the shell found two real defects in code that stage 2 had already closed, and neither is cosmetic.**
  1. **The command palette was a keyboard trap — a WCAG 2.1.2 failure.** The sidebar's search field opens the palette on focus (`template-shell.md` §1) and `closePalette()` returns focus to whatever opened it. So closing a palette that the search field opened re-focused the search field, whose listener re-opened the palette, forever: **Escape did nothing and there was no way out with the keyboard alone.** Fixed with a `restoringFocus` guard raised across the focus hand-back, which is safe because `focus()` dispatches synchronously. Two listeners, each correct in isolation; reading them separately never puts them in the same sentence, and the server-rendered verification that passed 39 screens could not see it.
  2. **The rail's "Expand navigation" button could never be shown, at any viewport.** `.k-rail-expand { display: grid }` sat inside the 900–1199 media query and the base `.k-rail-expand { display: none }` sat **after** it in source order — same specificity, later rule wins. The button existed, carried its accessible name and its `data-testid`, and was `display: none` at 1024px with the media query matching. **This is build rule 1's exact shape** (`typography.css` shadowing `--type-body`), which the BUILD-SPEC warns about for tokens and which arrived here in a layout rule. Fixed by moving the base block above the media query, with the reason written into the stylesheet so nobody reorders it back.
- **Two of the four first-run failures were TEST defects, and separating them from the product defects was the work.** `locator.focus()` opens the palette but leaves focus on the search field, whereas a real click completes the hand-off — so the test had to reproduce the gesture, not the shortcut. And the drawer-scrim click at (5, 5) landed on the brand link *inside* the drawer, because the scrim spans the viewport and the drawer covers its left edge. Collapsing these into "the product is broken" would have produced two fixes to code that was correct.
- **One finding is recorded rather than fixed, because it is the design's own doing.** Inside the drawer, one Tab reaches the palette: `accessibility.md` §3.2 puts the search field second in the sidebar's tab order and `template-shell.md` §1 makes focusing it open the palette. The user is not stranded — the palette is modal, traps its own focus, and Escape returns focus to the search field inside the drawer — so the test asserts the accurate contract (focus stays in the drawer **or** the palette, never on the shell behind) instead of a stricter one the SPEC never promised.
- **`docs/keel-conformance.md` was recounted mechanically and the old total was wrong.** 68 rows — present 54 · n/a 7 · missing 6 · declined 1, every row stated. The 2026-07-28 line read "present 40 · n/a 9 · missing 6 · declined 1", 56 states over 67 rows, because it was written from the approved batch rather than from a count of the table. Row 39 also carried `**partial**`, a fifth state nobody authorised, bundling two directories that exist with one that does not; split into rows 39 and 49, which is why the `missing` count rose 5 → 6. A number that only ever falls is a number somebody is managing.
- Alternatives rejected (and why): **defer Playwright to Phase 5** (offered; the unverified list would have grown every stage and Phase 7's gate would inherit all of it — and the two defects above would have shipped inside a redesign that reported itself verified) · **install it but write no tests until stage 4** (offered; it would have made the doctor green while proving nothing, which is the shape of a check passing on zero) · **fix the palette trap without a guard, by dropping the focus-on-search behaviour** (that is `template-shell.md` §1, and deleting a specified behaviour to dodge a bug is a deviation) · **assert the drawer traps focus absolutely** (the SPEC promises the opposite; a test that fails on correct behaviour gets deleted, not believed) · **keep `**partial**` in the conformance table** (a fifth state is an unfinished row wearing a label) · **restamp the locks without applying the reconciliation** (offered; an advanced stamp over an unapplied delta hides exactly the gap the mechanism closes) · **leave `package-lock.json` ignored** (an unpinned driver makes "it passed" unfalsifiable).
- Bounds: `installer/admin/assets/js/klytos-shell.js`, `installer/admin/assets/css/klytos-shell.css`, the new `tests/E2E/` tier, `playwright.config.js`, `package.json`, `.gitignore`, `.gitattributes`, `docs/03-technical-plan.md`, `docs/keel-conformance.md`, both lock files, both embedded Keel trees. Sends **DR-004**. Supersedes nothing; D-075's `⚠ unverified` list is now closed and D-075 stands as the record of why it existed.

## D-078 — The component layer is built, and driving it per state found a page-wide reflow failure, a cascade defect and three contrast pairs the delivery itself gets wrong
- Date / phase: 2026-07-29 / **Phase 4 Step 4, stage 3 of 6 — the component layer**
- Decision: `installer/admin/assets/css/klytos-components.css` is rewritten as the redesign's `.k-*` component layer (BUILD-SPEC §5.9), implementing the README's component inventory, the per-template geometry and `SPEC/accessibility.md` §5's name/role/state contract, with §2.1's table markup as its centrepiece. A test-only specimen (`tests/E2E/fixtures/components-specimen.html`) and 64 driven tests (`tests/E2E/components.spec.js`) make it verifiable per STATE rather than per page. **DR-005 is drafted, not sent** — sending is the user's call.
- **The one structural call, and it is adaptation 9.** The file the contract names is also the live stylesheet of every screen the redesign has not reached: measured, its classes are drawn by up to **40** admin PHP files (`.btn` 40, `.card` 37, `.alert` 37, `.form-control` 27). Rewriting it in place would have stripped the styling from all forty at once. So the previous 991 lines moved **unmodified** to `klytos-legacy-components.css`, loading immediately before the new layer — the body was `diff`ed clean against the original before the original was replaced, so nothing was lost in a move that could not be undone. It is temporary and tracked exactly like adaptation 5 (Font Awesome): deleted when its last consumer is ported, and the redesign is not complete while it stands. Verified on six real admin screens as owner: both sheets load in order on every one, `.card` still resolves to the legacy 8px radius and `.k-card` to the design's 10px, zero console errors and zero failed requests.
- **A specimen was built because a stylesheet has no consumer until stage 4, and "the CSS looks right" is a reading, not a test.** Manifest entry 16 is explicitly "a catalogue — not a screen" with `—` as its entry point, so it is NOT built as a product URL: the specimen lives under `tests/` (export-ignored, can never reach a release archive) and is served by Playwright route interception at an `/installer/admin/` path, which gives it the playground's real origin and therefore the REAL stylesheet chain. The router was not touched — it serves nothing outside `/installer/`, and changing a security control to host a fixture would be the wrong trade.
- **THE PART THAT JUSTIFIES THE STAGE: driving it per state found four things reading did not.**
  1. **A WCAG 1.4.10 page-wide reflow failure caused by a 1 × 1 invisible span.** `.k-sr` is `position: absolute`; the table's "Actions" column header uses it (that is §2.1's own markup); with no positioned ancestor its containing block was the initial one, so at 320 CSS px it laid out at its static position **inside the 670px-wide row** — escaping the scroll container's `overflow-x: auto` AND the card's `overflow: hidden`, and scrolling the entire page by 346px. **Every containment measurement in the chain read correct** — 280 inside 320 at every level, `overflow-x: auto` present — while the page genuinely scrolled. The assertion was rewritten to TRY TO SCROLL rather than compare `scrollWidth`, because that is what a person experiences. Fixed by making `.k-table-scroll` a containing block, and re-proven by removing it and watching the 346 come back.
  2. **A cascade defect that took three attempts, which was itself the signal.** The record's name in a selected row rendered accent-on-selection at 4.11:1. The generic in-component link rule's widest branch is `(0,4,1)` and every table sits inside a card, so it outranked `.k-table tbody th a` at `(0,1,3)`. Two attempts to out-specify it failed. The fix was structural, not numeric: the default now lives inside `:where()` at **zero specificity**, so every named component rule beats it by construction. **This is build rule 1's shape for the third time in this build** — `typography.css` shadowing `--type-body`, the rail button losing to source order, now a colour losing to specificity. It does not generalise as "check source order", which is what the second occurrence taught; it generalises as **never assume which rule wins — read the computed value out of the browser** (L-032).
  3. **A whole class of link had no colour rule at all.** A bare `<a>` inside a card, a bulk bar or an empty state fell through to the user agent's `#0000EE` — **1.48:1** on `--fondo-elevado` in dark. Nothing in the admin's stylesheets had ever assigned one. The delivery's colour table gives links `--color-acento`; applied, it measures 4.82:1 / 6.40:1.
  4. **Three specified colour pairs measure below AA → DR-005.** Not the audit being wrong: all 72 declared pairs were recomputed at the gate and 72/72 agreed. These are **compositions the 72 do not include** — a badge tint over `--fila-seleccion` (4.44:1 light / **3.08:1 dark**), the code panel's specified `--texto-secundario` and `--color-acento` on `--fondo-ventana` (4.46:1 / 4.23:1 light), and `--color-peligro` on `--fondo-elevado` in dark (**4.32:1**), which hits every error message and destructive button inside a card. **This is L-030 one layer down:** the gate proved every declared pair correct and never asked whether the declared set covered every pair the screens compose.
- **Three OTHER failing pairs were fixed in the build and deliberately NOT sent to Design**, because the delivery already answers them — recorded so the distinction is on file. `tokens/klytos-admin.css` states the rule as universal ("text is NEVER painted in a raw semantic colour on top of that colour's own tint"), so: the selected chip goes `--sobre-tinte-acento` (4.11 → 5.02 light, 4.48 → 4.53 dark), a tinted log line's message text goes `--sobre-tinte-*` (3.82 → 4.53, 3.95 → 5.50), and a read-only field goes `--texto-primario` (4.46 → 14.79). Applying a stated universal rule to a case the README did not enumerate is integration; choosing a colour for a pair the delivery fixes is not. The secondary button's border is the same shape and is logged as adaptation 10.
- **The one axe rule disabled, and why that is not hiding anything.** `aria-required-children` fires on `<table role="table">` because an explicitly-roled table may own only rowgroup/row and a `<caption>` is neither — but §2.1 mandates BOTH the caption and the explicit role. The real tree was read out of Chromium rather than argued about, and it is exactly right (table → caption + rowgroup → row → columnheader ×7 → rowgroup → row [selected] → cell, rowheader…). The rule is disabled **only on the two table sections**, and what it would have protected is covered by two tests that were each tested against a planted defect: the markup test went red when a `role="rowgroup"` was removed, and the aria-snapshot correctly stayed green, because the implicit role survives — which is precisely the redundancy §2.1 exists to provide. Stated plainly because it means the snapshot alone would not catch a lost explicit role; the pair is the coverage, not either one.
- **A false failure that had to be separated from the real ones.** The first run reported the destructive button at 2.59:1 in dark. It is 4.86:1. The test toggled `data-theme` after paint and `.k-btn` transitions `color` over 120ms, so `getComputedStyle` returned the INTERPOLATED value. The fixture now bakes the theme into the markup before load — which is also how the product actually works (D-075: server-rendered from the cookie, no flash). A specified transition and a theme-switching test, each correct, composing into a lie about the product: L-031's shape, inside the tooling this time.
