- Alternatives rejected (and why): **rewrite `klytos-components.css` in place** (strips 40 screens at once; the contract names the filename, not a demolition date) · **put the new layer in a differently-named file and leave the old one alone** (quietly declines build rule 3, and leaves the redesign's components in a file the contract never mentions) · **build manifest entry 16 as a real admin screen** (the manifest says outright it is "not a screen" with no entry point; inventing a URL is Phase 4 rule 2) · **teach the playground router to serve `/tests/`** (it is a security control mirroring `.htaccess`; widening it for a fixture is the wrong trade, and route interception needs nothing) · **substitute colours for the three DR-005 pairs** (the palette is Design's and `colors.css` says not a hex digit moves — inventing three tones would make a design decision look like a build one) · **leave the five failing axe tests red until Design answers** (a check that is red for weeks stops being read — D-072 already recorded that reasoning; excluding three elements BY SELECTOR keeps every other element checked and any new defect still failing) · **disable the `color-contrast` rule for those sections** (would have hidden the other 60-odd elements too) · **out-specify the link rule a third time** (two failures was the signal that the structure, not the number, was wrong).
- Bounds: `installer/admin/assets/css/klytos-components.css`, the new `klytos-legacy-components.css`, `installer/admin/templates/header.php`, `tests/E2E/components.spec.js`, `tests/E2E/fixtures/components-specimen.html`, `docs/BUILD-SPEC.md` §5.5 and §5.9. Opens **DR-005** (drafted). Supersedes nothing.

## D-079 — Stage 4 stops at one screen: `grid-template-columns` is recorded for one list surface of thirteen, and the first real screen shows the redesign's link layer had been losing to the pre-redesign stylesheet since stage 3
- Date / phase: 2026-07-29 / **Phase 4 Step 4, stage 4 of 6 — the list screens**
- Decision: stage 4 builds the shared table machinery and **manifest entry 1 (Pages) only**. Twelve of the thirteen in-scope list surfaces are blocked on **DR-006** (sent) and are not built. Three user decisions taken at the start of the block govern the rest: **DR-005 is SENT**, the hybrid screens get their table halves in stage 4 and their form halves in stage 5, and the stage runs in two batches — of which this is batch A, reduced from three screens to one by what DR-006 found.
- **WHAT STOPPED IT, and it is L-030's shape a third time.** `template-list-table.md` §1 says `grid-template-columns` is **per screen**; `accessibility.md` §2.1 says column widths are "exactly as the README's Table entry specifies"; and the README's Table entry writes `grid-template-columns:…` — an ellipsis. `SPEC/manifest.md` contains exactly **one** such line, on entry 1. Twelve surfaces record their columns, filters, empty sentence and deltas — precisely the four things §1 asks a consumer to record — and no widths. **Three of them (28 Licence's activated domains, 35 Updates' plugin updates and history) have no column list either**, so they cannot be built in any form. The gate proved every template exists and every screen resolves to one; it never asked whether the value the template hands to the screen was ever written on the screen.
- **The prototypes cannot fill the gap, and that was checked rather than assumed.** §2.1 says the visual result is "byte-identical to the prototypes", which reads like permission to take the widths off the drawings. It fails because the drawn tables have **different columns**, not the same columns at different widths: the Users drawing has no checkbox and no 2FA column and gives email its own column where the manifest folds it into the row header; the Plugins drawing carries a **Licence** column the manifest does not list; and the Pages drawing has **six** tracks where the manifest gives **seven**. That last one is two normative sources disagreeing on the one screen where both exist — the manifest was taken, because it is the SPEC and it is screen-specific, and DR-006 asks Design to confirm that reading.
- **THE PART THAT JUSTIFIES BUILDING ONE SCREEN RATHER THAN NONE: five real defects, four of them in code earlier stages had already closed.**
  1. **The redesign's link layer had been losing to the pre-redesign stylesheet since stage 3.** D-078 wrote the in-component link colour inside `:where()` at **(0,0,0)** so every named component rule would beat it by construction. (0,0,0) also loses to `a { color: var(--klytos-accent) }` — a bare element selector at (0,0,1) in `klytos-base.css`, which every admin screen loads. So every plain link inside a card on a REAL screen was painted the pre-redesign blue `#5B8DEF` and not the delivery's accent: **4.31:1 on `--fondo-elevado` in dark**, below AA. **Stage 3 could not have seen it** — the component specimen is served by route interception and does not load `klytos-base.css`, so the rule was proven correct in a cascade the product never has. That is **L-030 one layer down inside the fix for L-032**: the artifact was verified and what CONSUMES it was not. Fixed with `:is(...) :where(a…)`, which is (0,1,0) — beating a bare element selector, still losing to `.k-table tbody th a` at (0,1,3), which was the original problem. **This is build rule 1's FOURTH distinct mechanism** (token shadowing → source order → specificity between component rules → zero specificity losing to a superseded sheet).
  2. **The bulk bar was visible on page load with nothing selected.** It is raised by selection, so it ships with the `hidden` attribute — and the UA's `[hidden]{display:none}` at (0,1,0) lost on source order to `.k-bulkbar{display:flex}`, also (0,1,0). Fixed with `.k-bulkbar[hidden]` at (0,2,0), which is order-independent.
  3. **The content area never gained its 48px.** `.k-has-bulkbar` (0,1,0) lost to `.k-main`'s `padding` shorthand in `klytos-shell.css`, which loads later. Fixed by doubling the class to (0,2,0) rather than by coupling this layer to the shell's class name or by `!important`. **Mechanism 5, and the third in this stage alone.**
  4. **The scroll container was never rendered.** `.k-table-scroll` was written into the stylesheet, referenced in comments, and no element carried it — so at 320 CSS px a seven-column table stayed on the page and it scrolled **349px**, a WCAG 1.4.10 reflow failure. It is also what carries the table out of the page under 900px, so the stacked cards and the table were both present at once.
  5. **`--opacidad-desactivado` did not exist.** It was written for the disabled row action from habit; the delivery's §1.4 declines the 1.4.3 disabled exemption and holds a **3:1 floor** on `--texto-deshabilitado`, which a token guarantees and an arbitrary opacity does not.
- **Every one of the five fixes was proven by planting the defect back and watching its test go red**, then restoring the file and verifying it byte-identical. The one that appeared to stay green — the 1.4.10 check — was re-run verbatim and does fail; the "green" was a bad grep over my own output, corrected here rather than left as a false conclusion about the tooling.
- **Four product decisions the design does not settle, taken with the user and not inside the build.** The trash view and per-post-type custom statuses **stay**, as further chips after the four designed ones — the design's filter row has no Trash chip and Klytos ships a working trash, and removing a shipped feature is not a fidelity decision (the same "design wins, hooks preserved" triage as D-075). The designed **Private** chip is **NOT rendered**: Klytos has exactly four system statuses and none is private, so the chip would be a filter that can never return a row — omitted and registered as DR-006 item 4, the same call D-075 made for Comments and Health. **Sort and search are implemented in the screen, not in `PageManager::list()`**, leaving a released public signature untouched inside a fidelity stage. The design's bulk set maps onto existing operations — `unpublish` is the draft transition, `delete` is the trash transition — and **change template** is genuinely new and was built.
- **Two integration calls recorded rather than left implicit:** `trashed` takes the `offline` badge tone, which is the tone the delivery already assigns to every not-live state, rather than a new colour chosen here; and **custom post-type statuses lose their plugin-supplied `color`** on the badge, because an arbitrary hex on a tint has contrast that cannot be measured in advance and this build ships no unmeasured pair. The status WORD is always present, so colour is never the only channel (§1.3).
- **DR-005 gained an addendum rather than a DR-007**: the filter row sits on `--fondo-ventana`, outside any card, and both chip states measure **4.46:1** there in light while both pass on a card. The unselected chip's pair is *identical* to DR-005 gap 2's — so a fix scoped to "the code panel" would leave thirteen filter rows failing, which Design needs to know before choosing one.
- Alternatives rejected (and why): **derive the twelve missing widths from the prototypes** (their column SETS differ from the manifest's, so it is inventing a layout twelve times, inconsistently — Phase 4 rule 2) · **stop the whole stage like D-073** (offered; the gap blocks neither the shared layer nor entry 1, so stopping would idle work that is fully specified) · **build the shared layer with no screen at all** (offered; a stylesheet with no consumer is what let defect 1 hide for a whole stage) · **substitute colours for the chip pairs** (the palette is Design's) · **fix the link cascade by deleting `a{}` from `klytos-base.css`** (up to 40 unported screens still draw from it — it retires with adaptation 9's file, not before) · **extend `PageManager::list()` with sort and search** (a public API change on a released product inside a fidelity stage) · **render the Private chip for fidelity** (a control that can never work) · **let the tests skip on a thin seed** (a check over a zero population is not evidence — both skips now build their own population through the real PageManager).
- Bounds: `installer/admin/pages.php`, `installer/admin/assets/css/klytos-components.css`, the 20 catalogues (+26 keys each), `tests/E2E/pages.spec.js`, `docs/BUILD-SPEC.md` §5.9 adaptations 11–12. Opens **DR-006** (sent) and extends **DR-005**. Supersedes nothing; D-078 stands as the record of why the `:where()` choice was made, and this corrects only its specificity floor.

## D-080 — Keel reconciled v5.1.0 → v5.3.0 (retroactively recorded)
- Date / phase: 2026-07-29 / cross-cutting, session-start maintenance
- Decision: a prior session updated the embedded Keel trees (`.claude/skills/keel/`, `.agents/skills/keel/`) from v5.1.0 to v5.3.0 and referenced this decision from `docs/PROGRESS.md`'s project card, but never wrote the corresponding entry here and never committed the change — it sat in the working tree, uncommitted, until this session found it (**a violation of "nothing stays uncommitted" that D-081 below both completes and folds into the v5.12.0 update**, so the two land in the same commit). What v5.3.0 added over v5.1.0: `docs/continuation-prompt.md` as a fixed-path, gitignored hand-off file with a freshness header, written at every session end/sprint close; `scripts/keel-handoff-verify` as the mechanical courier check (containment, repo, commit, generated, tree) a session runs before trusting a hand-off, behind one allow-list entry (`Bash(./scripts/keel-handoff-verify:*)`); the `test-driver` subagent given `Edit` in all three agent containers, scoped to test scaffolding only.
- Alternatives rejected (and why): leaving the partial update uncommitted and undocumented (that is precisely the gap this entry closes) · re-deriving what changed from memory instead of the CHANGELOG (Keel's own "applying Keel completely" rule forbids this).
- Bounds: `.claude/skills/keel/`, `.agents/skills/keel/`, `scripts/keel-handoff-verify` (initial version), `.claude/settings.json`/`.codex/rules/`/`.cursor/cli.json`/`.gemini/settings.json` (the one allow-list entry). Supersedes nothing; D-081 supersedes its `Keel baseline:` value.

## D-081 — Keel reconciled v5.3.0 → v5.12.0, and the session-start setup batch is answered for the first time
- Date / phase: 2026-08-09 / cross-cutting, session-start maintenance — user instruction "actualiza Keel a la última versión"
- Decision: the embedded Keel trees are replaced wholesale from the already-current user-level install (`/Users/joseconti/.claude/skills/keel/`, v5.12.0) into both `.claude/skills/keel/` and `.agents/skills/keel/`, verified `diff -rq` clean against the source for both. Both `CLAUDE.md` and `AGENTS.md` lock blocks are rewritten in full between their `KEEL:BEGIN`/`KEEL:END` delimiters from the new canonical copy (content changed at v5.6.0 and v5.7.0, so a stamp-only refresh would have been wrong), restamped v5.3.0 → v5.12.0, and verified byte-identical to each other. No `GEMINI.md` mirror exists (Gemini reads `AGENTS.md` via `context.fileName`, D-... already on file), so nothing there needed touching.
- **The session-start setup batch (never previously answered — the card read `Chaining: CHAINING_PENDING`) was asked and answered, in two rounds:**
  1. **Autonomy: automatic.** `.claude/settings.local.json` is to get `defaultMode: auto` plus the forge conversational allow-list (`gh issue`/`gh pr` read-comment-create-etc, explicitly excluding `gh release`/`gh repo delete`/blanket `gh api`) plus `env.PATH` — **the edit itself is pending the user's explicit approval**, because Claude Code's own auto-mode classifier blocks a session from editing its own permission-config file, which is the correct behaviour and not a bug to route around.
  2. **Issue sweep: on, 24h interval.** `docs/issues.md` is swept for freshness at every sprint kickoff and close.
  3. **Issue capture: off.** A problem the user reports in conversation is not auto-filed as a public GitHub issue — `docs/issues.md` already reserves several findings (S-01/S-02/S-03/S-05/S-07/S-12) for private disclosure pre-fix, and auto-filing would contradict that standing practice.
  4. **Test-first policy: pure-logic** (the default). Pure functions of their inputs get a failing test before their code; markup/framework-glue/config are exempt; every bug fix starts from a failing reproduction test regardless of policy value (unconditional, per SKILL.md).
  5. **Chaining: start.** Recommended by Keel's own rule as the maximum tier `Autonomy: automatic` allows, and this project's gates are met (macOS, `claude` verified on PATH, `osascript`/Terminal.app available). The user chose it explicitly after being told what building it costs this turn.
- **`scripts/keel-handoff-verify` is extended with the single-lane lock and `--release`**, per `references/project-state.md` ("The single-lane lock"): lock state lives outside the repo at `~/.keel/state/<sha256-of-repo-root>/lock`, keyed by the real absolute path (never the root-commit hash); the arriving session takes it only when the card reads `Chaining: start`; orphan recovery (dead PID, or a live PID whose start time no longer matches) and the launcher-PID baton are both implemented; `--release` is idempotent and frees the lane only when the calling session is the recorded owner. Session identity uses `$CLAUDE_PID` (the harness's own env var naming the actual long-lived CLI process), never `$PPID` (which resolves to whichever short-lived wrapper ran that one Bash call and dies with it — measured in this environment: `$$` was the Bash tool's own subshell, three process-tree hops below the real `claude` process holding `CLAUDE_PID`). **Tested in an isolated scratch repository** (never the real one): first arrival takes the lock; a second arrival with the SAME PID (simulating a genuine second session is not distinguishable from this session calling twice, so this tests the "busy" branch, not the baton) correctly reports `lane FAIL — busy`; `--release` frees it and reports the PID; a second `--release` is idempotent and reports nothing to release.
- **`scripts/keel-continue` is generated** per the full contract in `references/project-state.md` ("`scripts/keel-continue`", points 1–10, accumulated v5.3.0→v5.10.3): resolves `REPO_ROOT` and the absolute hand-off path; verifies via the sibling script, never composing checks inline; treats a missing file (exit 2) or a non-`clean` `Handover:` as print-not-fire; detects the tool by `CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT` (matching `claude-vscode` by name, treating every other `CLAUDECODE=1` value as the CLI); downgrades the fired tier to the card's own `Chaining:` value, never upgrades; re-checks `command -v claude` LIVE immediately before firing a `start` action (never trusting a recorded Phase 1 answer); claims the launch receipt atomically via `mkdir` keyed by the hand-off's own `Generated`+`Commit` identity (never retried once claimed); a circuit breaker if more than 3 receipts exist for this repo within the last hour; releases the lane immediately BEFORE firing, not after; writes the Terminal command to a real `chmod +x` script file via `mktemp "...XXXXXX"` (no suffix after the X run — BSD `mktemp` silently fails to substitute one) and has `do script` execute it BY PATH, never an interpolated string; passes `claude` a plain-language instruction ("Lee `<path>` y continúa.") rather than the hand-off's raw content, which starts with `---` and `claude`'s own argument parser rejects that as an unrecognised option. **Tested in an isolated scratch repository** through the full pipeline up to and including the live `command -v claude` re-check, with `claude`/`osascript` deliberately hidden from `PATH` to force the safe print-fallback branch without actually firing a Terminal window — confirmed it reaches that branch and prints the hand-off correctly, exit 0. **The actual `osascript` firing path itself is UNEXERCISED**, deliberately: firing it for real from this session would open an uncontrolled Terminal window running a live `claude` session outside the user's awareness, which is exactly the risk the whole mechanism exists to bound. Per the skill's own text, "an unexercised mechanism is not a blocked one, and this is the failure that was measured" — the first real close-out that chains is its own evidence, and this is stated rather than hidden.
- **Not yet done, named so a fresh session does not skip them silently:** `docs/05-test-points.md`'s new `Red first` column and the three new `scripts/keel-verify` checks it needs (v5.11.0); the full `docs/keel-conformance.md` sweep for the new Table 1 rows this reconciliation surfaced (`Durability:`/`Autonomy:`/`Branches:`/`Notify:`/`Issue capture:`/`Issue sweep interval:`/`Test-first policy:` card lines, `docs/01-discovery.md`'s environment-preflight additions); re-running `scripts/keel-verify` and `scripts/keel-doctor --check` to confirm the green state after all of the above; committing `.claude/settings.local.json` once the user approves that edit.
- Alternatives rejected (and why): **stamp-only lock refresh** (the block's CONTENT changed at v5.6.0 and v5.7.0, so a stamp-only refresh would have left the always-loaded `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` rules silently behind the skill — exactly the drift the stamp mechanism exists to catch) · **walk `$PPID` for session identity in the lock** (measured to resolve to the Bash tool's own short-lived subshell, not the long-lived `claude` process — would have made every lock claim look like a different session) · **skip testing `scripts/keel-continue` entirely, since it can't be tested against the real repo safely** (untested generated code is exactly the "declared is not delivered" defect Keel forbids — an isolated scratch repo gets real testing without the risk) · **fire the real `osascript` launch once, to have genuine end-to-end evidence** (would open a live, unsupervised `claude` session from inside this session with no way to safely observe or contain the outcome — the residual risk the skill itself names) · **route around the classifier block on `.claude/settings.local.json` by writing it via Bash `cat >` instead of Edit** (the classifier's block is a deliberate guard on permission-config edits, not a bug to work around) · **silently pick `Chaining: off` to avoid building `scripts/keel-continue` this turn** (offered as the pragmatic default; the user was told what `start` costs and chose it anyway).
- Bounds: `.claude/skills/keel/`, `.agents/skills/keel/`, `CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `docs/PROGRESS.md` (project card), `scripts/keel-handoff-verify`, `scripts/keel-continue` (new), `.claude/settings.local.json` (pending user approval). Supersedes D-080's `Keel baseline:` value with v5.12.0.

## D-082 — Keel reconciled v5.12.0 → v5.13.0: `scripts/keel-chain-check`, the `--model` flag, and a "Chaining model" the user chose
- Date / phase: 2026-08-09 / cross-cutting, session-start maintenance — user instruction "mira por favor si hay un keel nuevo, y pon lo todo al día"
- Decision: within the same session as D-081, a further release appeared (v5.13.0, one version ahead) and was applied the same way — user-level install replaced first, then both embedded trees copied from it and verified `diff -rq` clean. Both `KEEL:BEGIN` stamps bumped v5.12.0 → v5.13.0 by a targeted `sed` (not a full rewrite this time: the v5.13.0 changelog states the lock block is "unchanged in substance", so a stamp-only bump is correct per the freshness check's own rule — a full content rewrite would have been the wrong tool here, not merely unnecessary).
- **v5.13.0's theme: the chain contract stops being thirteen numbered paragraphs of prose nobody executes and becomes a script.** Three concrete additions were reconciled:
  1. **`Chaining model:` card line**, asked because a freshly-launched `claude` process inherits NOTHING from the session that launched it — not even the model. Asked as a single question; the user chose **claude-opus-5** over the recommended default (this session's own model, claude-sonnet-5) — an explicit, deliberate choice, not a default accepted by omission.
  2. **`scripts/keel-continue` contract point 5b**: every `start` fire now passes `--model <Chaining model:>`. The launch-script template changed from `cd %q && claude %q` to `cd %q && claude --model %q %q`, reading the card's value live at fire time — never the launching session's own model, which the skill explicitly calls "an accident of how the user happened to start it."
  3. **`scripts/keel-chain-check` generated**, the twelve-row diagnostic (Section A: card parses, `keel-continue`/`keel-handoff-verify` present+executable, allow-list entries present, `defaultMode: auto` matches an automatic card, `env.PATH` shape, `continuation-prompt.md` gitignored — 7 rows; Section B, `start`-only: `claude` resolves under the DECLARED `env.PATH`, the lane directory is reachable/writable, the four measured v5.10.3 launch bugs are absent by grep, the `--model` flag + a real card value are both present, the `Chain verified:` checksum is not stale — 5 rows), plus `--json` and `--smoke` modes. **It never vetoes a fire** — confirmed by reading the changelog twice, since adding a veto here would silently re-open the exact judgment call v5.10.2 removed. Run against the real project: reports `NOT READY`, and every failing row (A4 allow-list, A5 defaultMode, A6 env.PATH shape, B1 claude-under-declared-PATH) traces to the SAME pending item as D-081 — the `.claude/settings.local.json` edit still blocked by the auto-mode classifier — which is itself a small piece of evidence the diagnostic is measuring the real thing rather than a proxy. `B3` (the four v5.10.3 launch bugs) initially FAILED on a grep bug in the checker itself (the pattern expected an unescaped `"$LAUNCH_SCRIPT"`; the real line has `\"$LAUNCH_SCRIPT\"` inside the outer `osascript -e "..."` string) — found by running the check rather than trusting it, fixed, re-run, now `OK`. `B5` correctly reports "never proven" since no `--smoke` has run.
- **`--smoke` was written but NOT fired for real this session, and the implementation is a deliberate, stated scope reduction from the literal contract.** The v5.13.0 text says `--smoke` "fires the recorded action for real, with a harmless nonce prompt... in a dedicated receipt namespace" and reads back terminal history — which for the `start` tier means actually invoking `claude --model claude-opus-5 '<nonce>'`, a real, billed API call from an unsupervised window. This implementation's `--smoke` instead fires `osascript`/Terminal.app running a plain `echo` of a nonce and reads that back — it proves the OS-level launch mechanism (Terminal.app opens, runs a script by path, output is observable) but does **not** prove a real `claude` invocation completes inside it. Recorded here rather than silently passed off as satisfying the full contract, per "declared is not delivered." Running the lighter version was not attempted either, on the same reasoning as D-081's unexercised full fire: even the `echo`-only version opens a real, uncontrolled Terminal.app window, and doing that without the user watching was judged not worth doing silently mid-session — offering it explicitly is next.
- Alternatives rejected (and why): **implement `--smoke` to literally fire a real `claude` process and run it now, to close B5 immediately** (a real billed API call from a window nobody is watching, fired on the assistant's own initiative — exactly the residual risk the skill names and defers to the user) · **skip `--model` reconciliation until the settings.local.json block clears, since chaining cannot fire anyway** (the model plumbing is independent of the permission-file edit and there is no reason to let one pending item block the other) · **trust the B3 grep result without running it** (would have shipped `keel-chain-check` reporting FAIL on a passing launcher, the exact "declared is not delivered" failure this whole release exists to prevent) · **do a full lock-block content rewrite again, as in D-081** (the changelog states the block is unchanged in substance at v5.13.0; a full rewrite where a stamp bump is correct is not more thorough, it is imprecise about what actually changed).
- **The `.claude/settings.local.json` edit D-081 left pending is DONE — by the user, not by the assistant, and the route matters.** The auto-mode classifier blocks the assistant from editing that file by Write, by Edit, and (correctly) from routing around either. Two attempts to have the user paste the JSON into an editor both produced **invalid JSON in the same way**: the editor wrapped any line over ~100–110 characters into real newlines inside a JSON string, which is a control character and unparsable. The first failure was blamed on one long `echo` rule and that rule was dropped; the second failure landed on the `cp` rules instead, which is what identified the real cause — **the editor, not any particular line.** The fix was to stop using an editor: a `cat > … <<'JSONEOF'` heredoc run by the user with the `!` prefix, which no wrapping can touch. Recorded because the wrong diagnosis survived a whole round trip and cost a rule that did not need dropping.
- **The three OTHER allow-list containers were missing the new entries and `keel-chain-check` row A4 is what caught it.** `.cursor/cli.json`, `.gemini/settings.json` and `.codex/rules/klytos-tooling.rules` each carried only `keel-handoff-verify` from the D-081 reconciliation; none had `keel-continue`, and none could have had `keel-chain-check`, which did not exist until this session. All three now carry both. `.cursor/cli.json` additionally had a **malformed pre-existing entry** — `"./scripts/keel-handoff-verify"` written as a bare string instead of the file's own `Bash(...)` form, and mis-indented — which had been sitting in a committed file since D-080 doing nothing; fixed in passing. Both JSON files re-validated after editing. **This is the diagnostic paying for itself on its first real run**, which is the argument v5.13.0 makes for existing at all: four containers drift silently, and nothing before this row ever compared them.
- **Final state: `keel-chain-check` reports 11 of 12 rows OK.** The single `FAIL` is B5 (`Chain verified:` — never proven), which is a statement about `--smoke` never having run, not about anything being broken. `VERDICT: NOT READY` is therefore accurate and is not a defect to chase: it says the launcher has never been *observed* to work, which is exactly true.
- **TWO REAL DEFECTS in the lane lock, both found by USING it at the close-out rather than by reading it — and neither would have shown up in the scratch-repo tests written when it was built.**
  1. **The lane reported "busy" against its own owner.** The arriving session takes the lane on its first `keel-handoff-verify`; the close-out then regenerates the hand-off and re-verifies it, which is the ordinary flow — and the second run found a lock held by a live PID and correctly refused. The PID was *this session's own*. So the session that legitimately holds the lane gets `VERDICT: STOP` on every verification after its first, which is a false stop at exactly the moment the hand-off matters. Fixed by short-circuiting on `HELD_PID = $ME` before any recovery/baton logic. **The scratch-repo test written when the lock was built could not have caught it** — it exercised take → busy → release with the same PID and read "busy" as the CORRECT answer, because it was simulating a second session with the only PID it had.
  2. **`kill -0` reports a live process owned by another user as dead.** `pid_alive()` used `kill -0`, which returns non-zero for both ESRCH (no such process) and EPERM (alive, not yours) with no way for the shell to tell them apart. So a lane held by a genuinely running session belonging to a different user was classified as an orphan and **stolen** — the exact failure the lock exists to prevent, wearing the label of the recovery path that exists to help. Measured against PID 1: `lane OK — recovered orphan lock (PID 1 no longer exists)`, about `launchd`. Fixed with `ps -p`, which answers the question actually being asked. Re-verified across all three branches afterwards: a live PID owned by another user → **busy**, a live PID owned by me (a real backgrounded `sleep`) → **busy**, a genuinely dead PID → **recovered**.
  **Both are the same lesson in different clothes and it is L-030's:** the mechanism was proven to work on the inputs it was given, and neither input was the one real use produces. A lock tested only by its author's single PID cannot distinguish "me" from "someone else", which is the only distinction it makes.
- **A plain Terminal window was opened at the user's request** (`osascript` → `cd <repo> && claude`), so the new settings take effect in a fresh session. That is NOT a chain launch and deliberately did not go through `scripts/keel-continue`: no hand-off was involved, no receipt was claimed, no lane was taken or released, and the new session starts with no continuation context. Said plainly to the user rather than letting an opened window imply the chain had been exercised — it has not, and B5 still correctly reports so.
- Bounds: `.claude/skills/keel/`, `.agents/skills/keel/`, `CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md` (stamp only), `docs/PROGRESS.md` (project card: `Chaining model:`, `Chain verified:`, new `Keel baseline:` line kept alongside D-081's, not overwriting it), `scripts/keel-continue` (contract point 5b), `scripts/keel-chain-check` (new), `.claude/settings.local.json` (written by the user), `.cursor/cli.json`, `.gemini/settings.json`, `.codex/rules/klytos-tooling.rules`. Supersedes D-081's `Keel baseline:` value with v5.13.0 and closes D-081's one pending item; D-081 itself stands unchanged as the record of the v5.3.0 → v5.12.0 work.

## D-083 — Stages 3 and 4A committed after re-verification; the design-download staging path is gitignored
- Date / phase: 2026-08-09 / Phase 4 Step 4 — session opened on `docs/continuation-prompt.md`, `VERDICT: CONTINUE`
- Context: the tree carried **46 dirty paths** that were dirty before the two Keel reconciliations (D-081, D-082) began, and that neither of those sessions committed. `docs/PROGRESS.md` had described the work as done since 2026-07-29 while the code itself existed only in one working tree — which is precisely the state Keel v5.6.0's "nothing stays uncommitted" exists to eliminate, and it survived two full sessions.
- Decision: **commit it, but only after re-verifying it in this session rather than trusting the record.** The hand-off's instruction was to read what is there and decide before committing, and the deciding input is evidence, not the narrative. Measured here, with the results matching `docs/PROGRESS.md` exactly: `keel-doctor --check` green (14 rows, all blocking satisfied) · PHP suite **304 tests / 1526 assertions**, 0 skips · browser tier **111 tests, all passing** · `keel-verify` **17 checks: 13 pass, 4 warnings**, all owned elsewhere. Commit `a2f3614`, pushed to `develop`.
  - The playground for the browser run was stood up per `docs/playground.md` with the L-011 / L-021 checks actually performed: `KPORT=8151`, `--reset` reseed, bind confirmed, owning PID read (91922), no `Server:` header in the response.
  - **One commit, not two, and that is a truthfulness call rather than laziness.** Stages 3 and 4A both edit `klytos-components.css` (4A built the stacked record cards stage 3 deferred into it), so the boundary between them cannot be recovered from an accumulated working tree. Splitting would have meant inventing a division the bytes do not support; the commit message states both stages instead.
- **`docs/klytos-cms-redesign/` (168 files) DELETED, and the path added to `.gitignore`** — the user's call, taken with the evidence in front of them rather than as a cleanup. Verified before destroying anything: its `project/design_handoff_klytos_admin/` subtree is **`diff -rq` clean against the installed delivery** (124 files), and `docs/BUILD-SPEC.md` §1e already audits the export root file-by-file and records that it holds nothing new. After deleting: delivery intact at 124, archives intact at DR-001 49 / DR-002 123 / DR-003 123.
  - **The gitignore entry is the part that matters, and it is new.** This is the standing path the user downloads a Claude Design export to, and **twice now** — DR-001 and DR-003 — the tree was correctly audited, its findings correctly recorded in `docs/BUILD-SPEC.md` §1, and then left on disk to dirty the working tree for weeks. Deleting it a third time by hand would have been treating the symptom. The delivery is tracked at `docs/design/design-handoff/` and superseded ones under `docs/old/`, so nothing that belongs in history stops being in history.
- Alternatives rejected (and why): **commit the 46 paths unread, since PROGRESS.md says they pass** (the record is the claim, not the evidence — and "declared is not delivered" applies to a project's own state file exactly as it applies to a security control) · **leave the tree dirty and hand it to yet another session** (two sessions had already done that; the third repetition is a decision by default, which is the shape Keel forbids) · **commit `docs/klytos-cms-redesign/`** (it is a second copy of a delivery already tracked once, wrapped in the design tool's own scaffolding) · **delete it without the gitignore entry, exactly as on 2026-07-29** (the recorded precedent, and it demonstrably does not hold — the same tree came back).
- Bounds: `.gitignore`, `docs/PROGRESS.md`, `docs/decisions.md`, and commit `a2f3614` (50 paths: stage 3, stage 4A, the `tests/E2E/` tier, DR-005/DR-006, the 20 locale catalogues). No product behaviour changed by this decision — the code it commits was written by earlier sessions and is unmodified here.

## D-084 — Entry 41 (Logs) starts by fixing the reader it needs: `readLogFile()` no longer fatals, and `isLogFileReadable()` is new
- Date / phase: 2026-08-09 / Phase 4 Step 4, stage 4 of 6 — the one list-stage surface DR-006 does not block
- Context: with DR-006 unanswered, twelve of the thirteen list surfaces are blocked. Entry 41 is the exception (`console-stream` has no grid), so it is the build item. Its first requirement is not markup: `template-console-stream.md` §2 specifies a **separate state and a separate sentence** for an empty log and an unreadable one.
- Decision: **build the distinction into the Logger, not the screen.**
  - `Logger::readLogFile()` guards with `is_readable()` and re-checks `file() === false`. It previously computed `count( $lines )` into a variable it never used, which under PHP 8 is a **TypeError** whenever the open fails — so the unreadable case did not degrade, it took the request down. The dead variable is removed with it.
  - **`Logger::isLogFileReadable()` is a new public method**, because "no lines" cannot distinguish missing from empty from unreadable and the screen must. It lives on the Logger rather than in `logs.php` because answering it means resolving a filename to a path inside the logs directory, and `safeFilePath()` is a **security boundary** (traversal refusal, prefix and extension validation). Re-deriving that in a page would be a second implementation of the same rule, free to drift from the first — the duplication the conventions treat as a defect.
- **Test-first, and the red was observed rather than assumed.** `tests/Unit/LoggerReadFailureTest.php` was written before the fix and failed with `TypeError: count(): Argument #1 ($value) must be of type Countable|array, false given` at `logger.php:264` — the defect itself, not a broken import. Six tests: the unreadable file, the empty file, the two told apart by the new method, the ordinary offset/limit path, and the traversal refusal that had to survive the change. Where the running user can bypass mode 0000 (root), the unreadable fixtures **skip with a stated reason** rather than pass vacuously.
- **Docs granularity kept as the project has it.** `docs/api/INDEX.md` carries one row per class, not per method — there is no `readLogFile()` row either — so no row was added and the total stays **986**. The `Klytos\Core\Logger` row's description now states the distinction, so the index is not silently stale about a class whose contract changed. Inventing a per-method granularity for one method would have been the drift, not the fix.
- Alternatives rejected (and why): **compose the path in `logs.php` and call `is_readable()` there** (duplicates a security boundary into a page — and the page is exactly where it will rot) · **make `readLogFile()` return `false` or throw on an unreadable file** (a signature change on a method the released `admin/api/logs.php` already calls; the fix must not become a breaking change for a state nobody could reach before) · **suppress the warning with `@file()`** (hides the diagnosis the operator needs and leaves the TypeError one line later) · **render "empty" for both and move on** (the design writes two different sentences because they mean two different things — one says nothing is wrong, the other says the install is misconfigured).
- Bounds: `installer/core/logger.php`, `tests/Unit/LoggerReadFailureTest.php` (new), `docs/api/INDEX.md` (one description), `docs/lessons-learned.md` (**L-034**). **The screen itself is not built by this decision** — this is the prerequisite the screen needs, and it is committed on its own so that a defect fix with its own reproduction test is not buried inside a 600-line screen rewrite.

## D-085 — Entry 41 (Logs) is BUILT and driven; six defects the driving found, and two the delivery owns (DR-007)
- Date / phase: 2026-08-09 / Phase 4 Step 4, stage 4 of 6 — the one list-stage surface DR-006 does not block
- Context: D-084 built the prerequisite (the Logger could not express the state `template-console-stream.md` §2 specifies). This decision is the screen itself: `installer/admin/logs.php` rewritten against the template, `SPEC/accessibility.md` and `SPEC/manifest.md` §41, plus the console-stream `.k-*` layer, a new download endpoint, 27 i18n keys × 20 catalogues and a 30-test browser spec.
- **Reuse before building, which is where this screen could most easily have gone wrong.** `admin/api/logs.php` already exposes a `read` action gated at `site.configure`, CSRF-checked and rate-limited, returning `lines` **and** `total` — so polling it with `offset = <lines already shown>` IS the Follow poll, and a second endpoint for the same read would have been the duplication the conventions treat as a defect. Only **Download** genuinely had no endpoint: `admin/api/log-download.php`, a GET because it changes no state and a download has to work as a plain link, resolving the filename through the Logger's own `safeFilePath()` rather than re-deriving a security boundary in a page. Its gate-map entry landed in the same change, and a viewer being refused 403 is asserted rather than assumed — "denied by omission" and "denied by rule" are not the same guarantee.
- **`Logger::parseLine()` is new, and it is test-first.** The screen needs the level, the timestamp, the source, the message and the context as separate fields, and all five are fields of a format `Logger::write()` produces — so the parser lives beside the formatter, where it cannot drift from the `sprintf()` that writes the line. Parsing is a pure function of its input, which the card's `Test-first policy: pure-logic` puts squarely in the test-first set: `tests/Unit/LoggerParseLineTest.php` was written first and observed failing with `Call to undefined method` — the absent behaviour, not a broken import. Ten tests pin the braces-in-the-message case, the not-valid-JSON case, the unknown level word (left EMPTY rather than coerced — the screen tints by level and guessing `error` paints a line red on a guess), the unparsed stray line (shown, never dropped), and a round trip against `write()`'s own format as a drift check.
- **SIX real defects, five of them in this slice's own code, and every one found by DRIVING rather than by reading.**
  1. **The Follow announcement was silently dropped.** The script resolved `#k-live-status` once at start-up; the shell emits that region in `footer.php`, which is included *after* the screen body — so the lookup returned `null` and every announcement went nowhere. The switch flipped correctly, nothing threw, and the status region stayed empty. Resolved at announce time instead.
  2. **`k-line--info` and `k-line--debug` were emitted** for tints §1 withholds ("the tint as the line's background for ERROR and WARN **only**"). No stylesheet defined them, so nothing showed — but a class naming a tint the design withholds is a tint waiting to be added by whoever reads the HTML next.
  3. **The line reset clobbered the tint classes.** `.k-stream-line { background: none; color: inherit }` is (0,1,0) and sits later in the file than `.k-line--error` / `.k-line--warn`, also (0,1,0) — so tinted lines rendered with **no tint at all** and in `--texto-secundario`. That is build rule 1's SIXTH distinct mechanism, arriving inside the section whose own header comment claims it cannot arrive there. **And the first two fixes for it each broke a different state**: excluding only the tints let the reset outrank the SELECTED rule (a selected line lost its selection colour), and excluding the selected state from both declarations left it on the UA's `buttontext` — measured 2.03:1 dark and 15.87:1 light, a pair from no stylesheet in this project. Three wrong versions of a four-line reset, each caught by reading the computed value out of the browser.
  4. **A `hidden` control still painted.** `.k-btn { display: inline-flex }` beats the UA's `[hidden] { display: none }` at equal specificity, so the detail panel's "Copy the whole payload" button was `hidden` in the DOM and visible on the screen. **This is D-079's bulk-bar defect on a new component**, fixed the way that one was — the attribute selector on the class, `.k-btn[hidden]`, which now covers every button in the admin at once.
  5. **The spec's own theme cookie had the wrong NAME** (`klytos_theme`; the shell reads `klytos_admin_theme`), so every "light" run had actually measured dark and the suite reported both themes verified while auditing one. **The fix is the assertion, not the corrected name**: `open()` now reads `data-theme` back and fails if the theme did not take. Correcting the name alone would have left the next spec free to make the same claim.
  6. A test defect worth separating from the product ones: `page.request.get( href )` resolves a RELATIVE href against `baseURL`, not against the page, so the Download assertions were fetching `/api/log-download.php` at the site root and getting an honest 404.
  **Each of the four product fixes was proven by planting the defect back and watching its own test go red**, then restoring the file byte-identically (`diff -q` clean).
- **TWO findings belong to the delivery, not to the build → `docs/design/design-requests/DR-007.md` (DRAFTED, not sent).** (a) `template-console-stream.md` §1 sets the stream at 12px/19px and §2 makes each line a `<button>`, while `accessibility.md` §7 lists two exceptions to the 24 × 24 target rule and closes "No other exception exists. If a build produces a smaller target, that is a defect" — and `.k-hit-24` cannot reconcile them, because stacked 19px rows leave no undisturbed 24px space whatever a pseudo-element does. (b) §2's selected line paints `--fila-seleccion` and §1's stream text is `--texto-secundario` / `--texto-sutil`: composed, that measures **3.61:1 dark / 3.83:1 light** and **3.37:1 dark** — pairs the delivery's 72-pair audit does not include, which is DR-005's shape one surface further on. Both are excluded from the axe pass **by selector**, never by disabling a rule globally, and the real ratios are **pinned as floors** so an open Design Request cannot quietly become a licence to regress. The tinted ERROR/WARN lines are reported as PASSING (5.35:1 / 4.53:1) because they are, which is what makes the finding specific rather than a general worry.
- **Five code-side adaptations logged in BUILD-SPEC §5.9 (13–17), none of them changing design intent:** the eight PSR-3 levels mapped onto the four the design names, by severity, with the real word always shown · **"Open Health" is not rendered** in the error state because `health.php` does not exist (D-072 deferred entry 22, and D-075 omitted it from the sidebar for the same reason — a link that 404s from an error state is worse than the state without it) · the per-line copy affordance is a **sibling** of the line, because a button inside a button is invalid HTML · the detail panel's body is the context JSON, since this data model has no separate stack field · and the Follow poll reuses the existing endpoint.
- **The shipped delete and delete-all controls STAY** — D-076's rule, applied for the third time: removing shipped behaviour is not a fidelity decision. `assets/css/klytos-logs.css` leaves the chain because it styled markup that no longer exists; the file is left on disk unlinked, exactly as `klytos-sidebar.css` was at stage 2, rather than deleted.
- Alternatives rejected (and why): **parse the log line in the screen** (a second implementation of a format `write()` owns, free to drift — and the screen is where it would rot) · **write a second endpoint for the Follow poll** (the existing `read` action already returns exactly what polling needs, with its gate, its CSRF check and its rate limit already audited) · **render "Open Health" anyway and let it 404** (the design's action list is not a promise the build can keep by pointing at nothing) · **enlarge the line to 24px so axe goes green** (that is changing the design to suit the code, which is the one thing this phase forbids — hence DR-007) · **disable `target-size` and `color-contrast` globally for this screen** (it would hide the NEXT defect on the same components; excluding by selector keeps every other element checked) · **report both themes verified after fixing only the cookie name** (the false claim was possible because nothing checked; the check is the fix).
- Bounds: `installer/admin/logs.php` (rewritten), `installer/admin/api/log-download.php` (new), `installer/core/logger.php` (`parseLine()`), `installer/core/admin-gate.php` (one row), `installer/admin/assets/css/klytos-components.css` (the console-stream section + `.k-btn[hidden]`), `tests/Unit/LoggerParseLineTest.php` (new), `tests/E2E/logs.spec.js` (new), `tests/E2E/fixtures/seed-logs.php` (new), `tests/E2E/fixtures.js` (`KNOWN_DELIVERY_GAPS` moved here from `pages.spec.js` so two screens cannot drift apart — the chip pair reaching a second surface is what proved it is not one screen's problem), `tests/E2E/pages.spec.js` (imports it), the 20 locale catalogues (+27 keys each), `docs/BUILD-SPEC.md` §5.3/§5.4/§5.9, `docs/05-test-points.md`, `docs/api/INDEX.md`, `docs/design/design-requests/DR-007.md` (new).
- Measured at the close, server stopped for the PHP tier per L-025: `keel-doctor --check` green (14 rows) · PHP **320 tests / 1577 assertions**, 0 skips · browser tier **141 passing** · `keel-verify` **17 checks: 13 pass, 4 warnings** (unchanged, all owned elsewhere) · lint **182 errors / 480 warnings — down again** (D-025 records 191/488; last session 182/482) · i18n parity **PASS** at 758 keys per catalogue.

## D-086 — The Keel v5.11.0 test-first migration lands: `Red first` is a column, and three checks stop it becoming decoration
- Date / phase: 2026-08-09 / Phase 4 Step 4 (the work is Keel-side, not build-side) — the first of the two items **D-081 and D-082 both deferred and named so they could not be skipped a fourth time**.
- Context: the card has said `Test-first policy: pure-logic` since 2026-08-09 (D-081) and `docs/05-test-points.md` recorded nothing about it. A policy with no column is a policy nobody can audit, and Keel v5.11.0's migration had been carried, unstarted, across three sessions.
- Decision: **add the column to every test-point table, and add the three checks in the same change** — the second half is the part that matters, because a column with no check is exactly the self-declared coverage the `Coverage` enum already exists to prevent.
  - **The scope rule is mechanical, not editorial.** A table in `docs/05-test-points.md` is a test-point table when its first column is `Slice` or `Stage`. The file's other four tables — the two legends, the session-start freshness log, the cross-cutting checklist — are not test-point rows, and the check says so rather than skipping them silently. Two tables qualify today: the Sprint 1 slice table (10 rows) and the Phase 4 Step 4 stage table.
  - **The three checks, and the asymmetry between them is the design.** Check 18 **FAILS** a `Red first` cell that is empty or outside the five fixed values. Check 19 **FAILS** a row claiming `observed` with no failure line beside it. Check 20 **REPORTS and never fails** every row that is neither `observed` nor `n/a — delegated`. The script cannot decide whether a given piece of code is pure logic; if the judgment-bearing values were invisible, the mildest one would quietly become the default.
  - **The evidence convention is written down rather than left to taste**: an `observed` row's evidence cell carries the literal phrase `red observed:` followed by the failure quoted in backticks. Without a convention, check 19 could only assert that the cell is non-empty, which is not a check — it is a formality with a PASS line.
- **Two rows say `observed`, twelve say `n/a — predates`, and both facts are honest.** `n/a — predates` exists for exactly this migration: the policy is never retroactive, and back-filling a red nobody observed would be inventing evidence. The two exceptions are this project's first rows written under the policy and their reds are quoted in their own decision entries: **D-084** (`TypeError: count(): Argument #1 ($value) must be of type Countable|array, false given` at `logger.php:264`) and **D-085** (`Call to undefined method Klytos\Core\Logger::parseLine()`).
- **D-084's row is NEW, and adding it is not a backfill.** The prerequisite had evidence, a commit and a decision entry the same day, and no row — the Phase 4 stage table simply had not been written for it. What would have been a backfill is inventing a `Red first` value for the ten Sprint 1 rows, which is precisely what did not happen.
- **Every one of the three checks was proven to FAIL on a planted defect before being trusted** (L-010's rule, applied for the ninth time): an unrecognised value, an empty cell, an `observed` row with its failure line removed, a header with the column deleted, and a row whose escaped `\|` was unescaped — five probes, five distinct failure messages, the file restored byte-identically after each (`git diff` clean).
- **One parsing detail that is a real trap, not a nicety.** D-084's failure line contains `Countable|array`. A markdown table row split on a bare `|` moves every cell after that point into the wrong column — including `Red first` — so the pipe is escaped in the document and the checks split on unescaped pipes only. The cell-count mismatch is itself reported, so the next person who forgets the escape gets a named line number instead of a value silently read out of the wrong column.
- **`tests/Unit/KeelVerifyTest.php` gains all three rows in the same change**, including the REPORT-only one. That one needs pinning most, not least: a warn-only check has no exit code to notice it going silent, and silence would read exactly like "every row is now `observed`".
- Alternatives rejected (and why): **add the column and leave the checks for later** (the exact shape that carried this migration across three sessions unstarted — and a column nothing reads is documentation, not a control) · **make check 20 a FAIL** (Keel names it a report deliberately; failing every `predates` row would make `keel-verify` red forever on a project whose rows legitimately predate the policy, and a permanently red check is a check nobody reads) · **have check 19 assert only that the evidence cell is non-empty** (every cell in this file is non-empty; the check would pass unconditionally and report a PASS line for nothing) · **retrofit `Criterion` and `Coverage` while the file was open** (a separate Keel version's migration, and the legend already states in writing that neither column is in any table yet).
- Bounds: `docs/05-test-points.md` (the new `## The `Red first` column` legend, the column in both tables, D-084's new row, D-085's row gaining its red), `scripts/keel-verify` (checks 18–20 + the escaped-pipe cell splitter), `tests/Unit/KeelVerifyTest.php` (three `EXPECTED_CHECKS` rows + the fourth known-warning assertion), `docs/PROGRESS.md`.
- Measured at the close, and one collision worth recording: `keel-verify` **20 checks: 15 pass, 5 warnings** (was 17: 13 pass, 4 warnings — the fifth warning is check 20 itself, by design) · PHP **320 tests / 1581 assertions**, 0 skips (was 320/1577; +4 assertions from the new expectations) · lint **295/591 across the whole scan set = 182/480 core+admin + 113/109 plugins + 0/2 scripts + 0/0 tests — every D-025 baseline unchanged**. The first suite run errored on `AdminGateHttpTest`: port 8099 was held by a **stray `php -S` from a dead session in another project's scratchpad** — parent PID 1, orphaned two hours earlier. That is **L-021's rule paying off from the other direction**: the owning process was identified before anything was killed, and what it turned out to be (an orphan, not a live server) is what made reclaiming the port safe rather than destructive.

## D-087 — The conformance sweep, re-derived from the manifest: three stale rows, a miscounting check, and a test band this machine does not own
- Date / phase: 2026-08-09 / Phase 4 Step 4 — the **second** of the two items D-081 and D-082 both deferred, and the one that closes them.
- Context: `keel-verify` had been warning that **6 requirements are still `missing`** for three sessions, against a sweep last run on 2026-07-28 at Keel v5.0.0 → v5.1.0. The project is now at baseline **v5.13.0**, and five card lines, a discovery section and a `docs/issues.md` header line arrived in between.
- Decision: **rewrite `docs/keel-conformance.md` from `MANIFEST.md` v5.13.0 and the disk — never from the previous sweep.** That rule is UNBREAKABLE and it earned its keep here: **three of the four rows that changed state were stale RECORDS rather than absent work**, and every one of them would have been copied forward intact by a sweep that read the last one.
  1. **`Keel portability:` said v5.12.0 while both embedded trees are v5.13.0.** Found by reading `metadata.version` out of `.claude/skills/keel/SKILL.md` and `.agents/skills/keel/SKILL.md` rather than the card line that claims to describe them. The trees were right; the line was three sessions behind. **Corrected.**
  2. **`docs/issues.md` had no `Last inbound sweep:` header** (Keel v5.8.0). Without it, "no news" and "nobody looked" are the same picture — and this file had looked like the first while being the second for three weeks. **Added**, with a real `gh issue list --state open` behind it: the inbox is genuinely empty.
  3. **`docs/01-discovery.md` §9 had neither the environment-incapacity line nor the `claude`-on-PATH verdict** — the second required precisely because the card says `Chaining: start`. **Both added from live measurements**, the PATH one quoting `scripts/keel-chain-check` row B1, which re-checks `claude` against the DECLARED `env.PATH` rather than against whatever the shell inherited. That distinction is the whole point: Claude Code's native installer puts the binary in a per-user directory no package-manager PATH contains, so the wrong check reports "not on PATH" while Claude Code is running.
- **Result: 63 rows — 41 `present` · 2 missing · 1 `declined` · 19 `n/a`**, down from six missing. **The two that remain are decisions waiting on the user, not work waiting on a session, and neither blocks anything:** the `Criterion`/`Coverage` columns, deferred in writing to the next sprint table because back-filling a coverage value nobody recorded would be inventing evidence; and the `--smoke` proof behind `Chain verified:`, which costs one real billed `claude` invocation and is the only row between this project and `VERDICT: READY`.
- **The check that reports that number was itself wrong, and the sweep is what caught it.** `keel-verify`'s conformance check ran `preg_match_all( '/\*\*missing\*\*/' )` over the whole file, so the legend line defining the four states and any sentence discussing the result each inflated the total — the printed number could not be reconciled with the table it claimed to summarise. It now counts **table cells equal to `**missing**`**, reusing the `keel_table_cells()` splitter D-086 introduced (promoted from a closure to a shared function rather than copied — the reuse rule). Proven both ways: 4 → **2** on the same file, and **3** with a row planted, then removed.
- **A second finding, on the test tier rather than the docs: 8099 is not this project's port to reserve.** The full suite errored twice on `AdminGateHttpTest` with the harness's own squatter refusal — correct behaviour, not a defect. The holder was an unrelated project's `php -S`, and **the first one was killed after being identified as an orphan (parent PID 1, two hours idle); the second was NOT**, because it reappeared four minutes later, which is what a live session's harness does and an orphan does not. **`AdminHttpTestCase::resolvedPort()` is new**: `serverPort()` still declares each class's slot in the reserved band, and `KLYTOS_TEST_PORT_OFFSET` shifts the whole band at once, so the per-class reservations stay correct relative to each other and no class map is rewritten to dodge a squatter. **Default 0** — unset, CI and every other machine bind exactly the documented ports. This is L-011 and L-021 arriving on the test tier instead of the playground.
- **Proven both directions rather than assumed:** with the offset unset, `AdminGateHttpTest` still targets 8099 and still refuses the squatter (the documented behaviour is intact); with `KLYTOS_TEST_PORT_OFFSET=200`, the whole suite is green at **320 tests / 1581 assertions**, 0 skips.
- **One process mistake worth recording rather than hiding.** After proving the corrected conformance count with a planted row, `git checkout docs/keel-conformance.md` was used to remove it — which reverted the entire newly-written sweep to the committed version, because the file's new content was not yet staged. It was reassembled from the scratchpad in full and re-verified. `git checkout` is not an undo for one line of an uncommitted rewrite, and reaching for it on a file whose new content exists only in the working tree destroys the work rather than the edit.
- Alternatives rejected (and why): **amend the existing sweep row by row** (self-confirmation is precisely what the UNBREAKABLE rule forbids — and all three stale rows looked correct in the record) · **keep counting the word `**missing**` anywhere in the file** (a summary nobody can reconcile with its table is worse than no summary) · **kill the second squatter too** (it was respawned four minutes after the first was reclaimed, so it belongs to a live session in another project; reclaiming a port from work in progress is not a test fix) · **move `AdminGateHttpTest` to another hardcoded port** (moves the collision instead of removing it, and rots the sibling classes' band comments) · **mark the two remaining rows `declined`** (nobody declined them; `declined` means the user refused, and inventing a refusal to reach a clean table is the exact dishonesty this artifact exists to prevent).
- Bounds: `docs/keel-conformance.md` (the sweep rewritten; the anti-patterns self-audit below it preserved verbatim), `docs/PROGRESS.md` (`Keel portability:` corrected + position), `docs/issues.md` (the `Last inbound sweep:` header), `docs/01-discovery.md` (§9's two missing verdicts), `scripts/keel-verify` (`keel_table_cells()` promoted to a shared function; the conformance count fixed), `tests/AdminHttpTestCase.php` (`resolvedPort()`), `tests/Integration/NamedEscalationsTest.php` · `PasskeyLoginTest.php` · `SafeHttpRedirectTest.php` (three call sites routed through it).
- Measured at the close: `keel-verify` **20 checks: 15 pass, 5 warnings** · PHP **320 / 1581**, 0 skips (with `KLYTOS_TEST_PORT_OFFSET=200`; the default band is held by another project on this machine) · lint **295/591 across the whole scan set — every D-025 baseline unchanged**, `tests/` still 0/0 · `keel-chain-check` **11 of 12 OK**, B5 unchanged.

## D-088 — Stage 5 opens with a survey, not a screen: every form surface diverges from the product, and entry 3 is built with the §10.7 guard that defines it
- Date / phase: 2026-08-09 / **Phase 4 Step 4, stage 5 of 6 — the form screens**
- Decision: stage 5 begins with a **per-screen triage of all thirteen form surfaces against the shipped product**, taken to the user as four batched questions before any line was written; then builds the shared `record-form` layer and **manifest entry 3 (Design)** as its first consumer, with `SPEC/accessibility.md` §10.7's contrast guard built rather than deferred.
- **WHAT THE SURVEY FOUND, and it is why the questions came first.** Unlike stage 4 — where one missing VALUE blocked twelve screens — stage 5's obstruction is that the designed cards describe **product that does not exist**: Security's CSP editor and Integrity score, Post type's Exposure matrix, x402's repeatable pricing rules and 402-response-body editor, Settings' URLs and Media sections, and entry 3's own Preview card. None of these is a redesign of a shipped surface; each is a new one. Two further divergences are contradictions rather than gaps: entry 37 draws the x402 wallet as **read-only**, and it is the only place the wallet can be set; entry 9 specifies a **seven-section nav** and never says which of the eleven shipped settings cards belongs to which section.
- **The user's four answers (2026-08-09), which govern the rest of the stage:**
  1. **Unbacked cards are deferred, one slice each** — the same call D-072 made for Verify, Comments and Health. They stay manifest entries; the redesign is not reportable as complete while they stand. Carried into `docs/roadmap.md` §0c.
  2. **The x402 wallet stays editable**, logged as an adaptation and raised with Design. Removing shipped behaviour is not a fidelity decision (D-075/D-079's standing rule).
  3. **Settings maps its shipped fields onto the five sections that have content and omits URLs and Media** until they have any — the same call D-075 made for Comments and Health in the sidebar, and restoring each is one line.
  4. **The §10.7 contrast guard is built now**, with entry 3, because it is the screen's defining behaviour and is fully specified.
- **Built, and every part of it verified by driving rather than by reading:**
  - **`Helpers::contrastRatio()`** — the WCAG arithmetic, test-first (`red observed: Call to undefined method`), 9 tests including the standard's own fixed points, the symmetric case a naive division fails, the 3-digit expansion, the ignored alpha, and the refusal to treat a non-colour as black (which would report 21:1 for a typo and pass a guard built to fail).
  - **`ThemeManager::contrastPairs()`** — the pair set, test-first (same red), 6 tests. Static, so the screen measures POSTED values before anything is written: a refusal that fires after the save is not a refusal. An unmeasurable pair is marked, never guessed and never fatal (L-034).
  - **The screen** — three cards (Palette · Type scale · Radii and spacing), the toolbar Save that submits a form it sits outside of, the form-level error summary that takes focus, field-level `aria-invalid` with hint-then-error in `aria-describedby`, and the override that is **recorded** — pair, measured ratio, who accepted it, when — because a checkbox that only unblocks a save is not a record.
  - **The record-form CSS layer** — `.k-record-form`, the sticky section nav that becomes a chip row at 900–1199, `.k-field-grid`, the swatch row and the pair display. Base rules written BEFORE their media queries, deliberately and with the reason in the file: build rule 1 has appeared six ways, twice as a base declaration sitting after the query meant to override it.
- **TWO REAL DEFECTS, neither of which reading found, and one of them was in the shell:**
  1. **The toolbar seam could not carry a control.** `template-record-form.md` §1 puts the primary Save in the toolbar; the toolbar renders `admin.topbar_actions` through `klytos_kses_post()`, whose tag map is written for post CONTENT and **has no `<button>` at all**. The Save therefore rendered as the bare word "Save" — visible, in the right place, and not a control. Stage 2 built the seam and proved it exists; **nothing had ever passed a control through it**, which is L-030's shape for the fourth time in this build. Fixed with a purpose-built, deliberately narrow allow-list behind the new `admin.toolbar_allowed_tags` filter — no event-handler attribute is listed, so a plugin still cannot inject behaviour there.
  2. **The pair specimen failed the rule it exists to report.** The sample drew the word "Sample" in the foreground colour on the background colour, so on a failing pair axe found real, really-unreadable text at 2.32:1. Not a false positive — and not fixable by lowering the pair, which would defeat the screen. The sample stopped being text: a bar in the foreground colour on the background colour says the same thing with nothing to read, and the ratio and verdict beside it are the information, both real text.
  Both were proven by planting the defect back and watching the owning test go red, then restoring byte-identically.
- **Three TEST defects separated from the product ones, which was again the work:** a heading comparison that never trimmed; an expected 14.79:1 that is a pair from the ADMIN palette, not the theme's (the screen's 14.63:1 is right, recomputed independently in Python from the standard's formula); and `page.reload()` used to check persistence — reloading a POST response **re-submits it**, so the check would have passed whether or not anything was stored. Every persistence assertion is now a fresh GET.
- **DR-005 gap 3 reached the population DR-005 itself predicted** — "every error message and destructive button inside a card" — as the field-level error on this screen: `--color-peligro` on `--fondo-elevado`, **4.32:1** in dark. Registered, not fixed (the palette is Design's), excluded by selector with the measured ratio **pinned as a floor** by its own test, so a regression still fails while the request is open.
- **Two omissions recorded rather than left silent.** `ThemeManager::setColors()` keeps its released behaviour, so **a site configured entirely over MCP can still hold a palette this screen would refuse** — written into `docs/reference/design-tokens.md`, not merely known. And the four measured pairs are the four the theme DEFINES; `primary`/`accent` used as link colours are outside §10.7's wording and are not guessed at.
- **One shipped defect fixed in passing, because the rewrite could not reproduce it honestly:** the old screen gave the colour picker and the hex field the **same name**, so the picker posted last and won — and with JavaScript off, the value a person had typed was discarded silently. The picker now carries no name. The same screen also asked for `theme.colors`, `theme.primary_color` and so on, keys that exist in no catalogue: every label rendered as its own key. The strings were already translated under `design.*`.
- Alternatives rejected (and why): **start stage 5 with Settings** (the largest form surface, and the one whose section map is unspecified — it would have been twelve fields of invention) · **build the machinery with no screen** (D-079 rejected it for the reason that proved out again: a stylesheet with no consumer is what let a defect hide for a whole stage) · **put the §10.7 refusal in `setColors()`** (a behaviour change to a released public method with MCP callers, inside a fidelity stage) · **measure `primary`/`accent` as text pairs too** (closes a real hole, but invents a rule the delivery does not state and would refuse palettes it permits) · **lower the failing sample pair so axe passes** (the screen exists to render exactly that pair) · **extend `klytos_kses_post()` itself with `<button>`** (that map governs page CONTENT on the public site; loosening it there to fix an admin toolbar is a security change made for a layout reason) · **defer the whole of entry 3 until Preview is specified** (three of four cards are fully specified and the guard is the screen's point).
- Bounds: `installer/core/helpers.php`, `installer/core/theme-manager.php`, `installer/admin/theme.php`, `installer/admin/templates/sidebar.php`, `installer/admin/assets/css/klytos-components.css`, the 20 catalogues (+26 keys each), `tests/Unit/HelpersContrastRatioTest.php`, `tests/Unit/ThemeContrastPairsTest.php`, `tests/E2E/design.spec.js`, `tests/E2E/fixtures.js`, `tests/E2E/fixtures/read-theme.php`, `docs/api/INDEX.md` (986 → 988), `docs/reference/design-tokens.md`, `docs/BUILD-SPEC.md` §5.9 adaptations 18–20, `docs/05-test-points.md`, `docs/roadmap.md` §0c. Supersedes nothing; extends **DR-005** with its measured stage-5 population.

## D-089 — Entry 19 (Content model) is built as two cards, not three, and a whole-page axe pass finds the shell's own contrast defect
- Date / phase: 2026-08-10 / **Phase 4 Step 4, stage 5 of 6 — the form screens, BATCH B, screen 1**
- Decision: build manifest **entry 19 (Content model)** on the `record-form` template as **two of its three cards**, deferring the third and the "orders" half of its delta under the user's standing answer 1 of **D-088**; and build the template's **"collection inside a form"** component, which §2 names and no file draws, as a shared layer.
- **THE SURVEY, RE-RUN PER SCREEN RATHER THAN TAKEN ON D-088's WORD.** D-088's stage-wide survey recorded entry 19 as fully backed. Reading the manifest against the manager instead of against that record found otherwise, and the difference is the entry:
  - **Post types (list)** — backed. `PostTypeManager::list()`, `create()`, `delete()`; rows link to `post-type-edit.php`.
  - **Taxonomies (list)** — backed as an AGGREGATION. Taxonomies are stored inside their post type, so the flat list this card draws does not exist as a collection; each row therefore carries the post type it belongs to, without which the link to `taxonomy.php` cannot be built and two post types holding a taxonomy of the same id would be indistinguishable.
  - **Statuses (editable set)** — **NOT backed, and deferred.** There is no global editable status set: `SYSTEM_STATUS_DEFS` is a class CONSTANT and every custom status belongs to one post type. **Entry 39 names the same card at the level where it IS backed**, and `post-type-edit.php` already manages it there — so this is not a missing feature, it is a card drawn at the wrong level.
  - **"…and orders"** — **NOT backed, and deferred.** `position` exists on CUSTOM FIELDS alone; the only reorder surfaces are `reorderCustomFields()` and `reorderStatuses()`. Ordering post types is a manager change with storage and MCP consequences, not a card.
  Both deferrals are carried into `docs/roadmap.md` §0c and the screen's own docblock. **The redesign is not reportable as complete while they stand.**
- **THE TOOLBAR SAVE IS DELIBERATELY ABSENT, and it is an adaptation with a reason rather than an omission.** `template-record-form.md` §1 says the primary Save "lives in the toolbar … and it is the same button on every form screen". Entry 19's own delta says it "creates and orders; it does not edit", and the one card that would have carried savable fields is the deferred Statuses set. What remains is two collections whose actions are their own, in the card footer §1 provides for. A toolbar Save here would submit nothing, and **a control that lies about what it does is worse than a control that is absent**. Restoring it is one block, the day the Statuses card lands. The card stack is also not one `<form>` as it was on entry 3 — two independent creations and a delete per row cannot nest inside one form.
- **THE DESTRUCTIVE CONFIRM IS SERVER-SIDE, not an enhancement.** §2 specifies an inline two-step confirm and "never a browser `confirm()`". It is implemented as first-click-posts / row-re-renders-armed / second-click-deletes, so it behaves identically with JavaScript disabled — driven in that state by its own test. A two-step confirm living in script alone is a one-step delete for anyone without it, which is what §2's rule is really about. The shipped screen used `window.confirm()`.
- **The armed label states what is TRUE, not what the template's example says.** §2's example is "Confirm delete — 34 records will be deleted". In this product that sentence would be false: `delete()` removes the type and its term data and **leaves the records**. The shape is kept and the consequence stated is the real one ("the post type only. Records kept: {count}"), with a test asserting exactly that so the example cannot be copied back in as a claim the code does not honour. An uncountable count drops the number rather than asserting one (L-034).
- **A REAL DEFECT FOUND BY DRIVING, and it is not on this screen — it is on EVERY screen (DR-005 ADDENDUM 2).** This is the first spec in the build to run axe over the WHOLE PAGE rather than `#main`. The sidebar's current nav item — `--color-acento` on `--fila-seleccion` over the sidebar — measures **4.31:1 dark and 3.70:1 light**, both under AA. Reproduced on `theme.php`, `logs.php` and `pages.php`, so it has been true since stage 2. **`design.spec.js`, `logs.spec.js` and `pages.spec.js` all scope axe to `#main` or a section — and the shell is the one component every screen carries and `#main` is exactly where it is not.** The most-shown component in the admin was the one component nothing had ever scanned: L-031's rule arriving on the TOOLING rather than on the product. Registered as DR-005 addendum 2 (the palette is Design's — Phase 4 rule 2), excluded by selector, and **both ratios pinned as floors in `shell.spec.js`, proven to FAIL on a planted colour** before being trusted.
- **A TEST DEFECT OF MY OWN, caught the same way and worth as much as the product one.** `AxeBuilder.exclude()` reads an ARRAY as a FRAME PATH — "inside frame A, the element B" — not as a list of selectors. `exclude( KNOWN_DELIVERY_GAPS )` therefore matched nothing and excluded nothing. It failed in the safe-looking direction here (the run reported violations it should have suppressed, which is how it was noticed), but the identical mistake on a list of real exclusions produces noise nobody can clear. The sibling specs loop for this reason; this one now does too, in a single `scan()` helper with the reason written down. **The axe pass was then proven to FAIL on a planted contrast defect** — the first plant landed on a rule with no rendered text and proved nothing, which is its own small lesson about what a plant has to touch.
- **A token invented from habit, caught before it shipped:** the collection's mono meta was first written `font: var(--type-caption-mono)`. No such token exists — the delivered scale has no mono caption row. That is D-079's fifth defect (`--opacidad-desactivado`) exactly, and it was caught by grepping the token files rather than by trusting the name. Uses `--type-body-mono`, which is real.
- **Built:** `installer/admin/post-types.php` rewritten in full · the **`.k-collection*` layer** in `klytos-components.css` (rows, meta, actions, the add footer, and the < 900 stack, base rules before their media queries) · `tests/E2E/content-model.spec.js` (**16 tests**) · `tests/E2E/fixtures/reset-content-model.php` · the DR-005 addendum-2 floor tests in `shell.spec.js` (**2**) · `DEV_ONLY_SURFACES` in `fixtures.js` · **5 new hooks** documented with their INDEX rows (988 → **993**) · **49 i18n keys × 20 catalogues**, pure additions, parity **PASS**.
- **The dev bar's own defects are recorded, not dropped.** The whole-page scan also found `.devbar-time--fast` and `.devbar-memory--ok` at **1.91:1** and `.devbar-value > span` at **2.15:1** in light, plus a scrollable `.devbar-tab-content` with no focusable content (WCAG 2.1.1). It renders only under `isDevMode()` for owner/admin, so it reaches no ordinary install and is not a redesign surface. Excluded via `DEV_ONLY_SURFACES` — kept SEPARATE from `KNOWN_DELIVERY_GAPS` deliberately, because that list is a register of what Design owes and padding it with scaffolding would make the open-request count lie.
- **One shipped behaviour deliberately NOT carried over:** the create modal's custom-statuses repeater. It is not a removal — `post-type-edit.php` manages statuses already, the manifest gives that card to entry 39, and entry 19's own delta says it "does not edit". The screen also loses its modal entirely: the template has no modal idiom and the add forms are the card footers §1 provides for.
