- Alternatives rejected (and why): **build the Statuses card over the four system constants** (a card that can display but never edit, against a manifest that says "editable set") · **invent a global status store** (product scope inside a fidelity stage, with MCP and storage consequences) · **build a drag-to-order UI over a `position` field added for the purpose** (same objection, plus a released manager signature) · **render the toolbar Save anyway for template consistency** (a control that submits nothing) · **fix the sidebar contrast by darkening the accent** (substituting a delivered colour is what DR-005 exists to avoid — Phase 4 rule 2) · **put `.devbar` in `KNOWN_DELIVERY_GAPS`** (it would inflate the count of what Design owes) · **scope the axe pass to `#main` like the sibling specs** (it is precisely that scoping that hid the shell defect for four screens) · **copy §2's "34 records will be deleted" verbatim** (false in this product).
- Bounds: `installer/admin/post-types.php`, `installer/admin/assets/css/klytos-components.css`, the 20 catalogues (+49 keys each), `tests/E2E/content-model.spec.js`, `tests/E2E/shell.spec.js`, `tests/E2E/fixtures.js`, `tests/E2E/fixtures/reset-content-model.php`, `docs/api/INDEX.md` (988 → 993), `docs/design/design-requests/DR-005.md` (addendum 2), `docs/roadmap.md` §0c, `docs/BUILD-SPEC.md`, `docs/05-test-points.md`. Supersedes nothing; extends **D-088** and **DR-005**.

## D-090 — Entry 39 (Post type) is built as five cards of six, and the section nav — built two slices ago and never rendered — was painting below AA
- Date / phase: 2026-08-10 / **Phase 4 Step 4, stage 5 of 6 — the form screens, BATCH B, screen 2**
- Decision: build manifest **entry 39 (Post type)** on the `record-form` template as **five of its six cards**, deferring Exposure under the user's standing answer 1 of **D-088**; render the template's **section nav** for the first time in this build; and fix, rather than register, the contrast defect that first rendering exposed.
- **THE SURVEY, RE-RUN PER SCREEN RATHER THAN TAKEN ON D-088's OR D-089's WORD** — the rule D-089 earned, applied to the screen that follows it. Read against `PostTypeManager` rather than against a record:
  - **Identity** (`name`, `slug`) · **Editor choice** (`editor`) · **Per-locale slugs** (`slug_i18n`, locales from `SiteConfig`) — all three backed by `update()`'s own allow-list.
  - **Custom fields (repeatable)** — backed: `addCustomField()` / `removeCustomField()` / `listCustomFields()`, and the type list comes from `PostTypeManager::getFieldTypes()` rather than being re-listed in the screen, so a type added to the manager appears here instead of silently not.
  - **Statuses (editable set)** — backed HERE, which is exactly what D-089 predicted when it deferred the same card on entry 19: `getStatusesForPostType()` merges the four class constants with this post type's own, and `addStatus()` / `updateStatus()` / `removeStatus()` are per post type. **The card entry 19 could not draw is the card entry 39 was always the right level for.**
  - **Exposure (REST · MCP · sitemap · feeds)** — **NOT backed, and deferred.** `buildPostTypeData()` stores id, name, slug, slug_i18n, editor, taxonomies, custom_fields and statuses, and nothing else. These switches change what the outside world can read, so they are a slice with an authorization review, not a card invented inside a fidelity stage. Carried in `docs/roadmap.md` §0c; the redesign is not reportable as complete while it stands.
- **THE ONE REAL DEFECT WAS IN A LAYER THAT HAD NEVER BEEN RENDERED, and that is the finding.** `.k-section-nav-item` painted `--texto-secundario` on `--fondo-ventana`: **4.46:1 in light**, under AA by 0.04, recomputed independently in Python from the WCAG formula and agreeing with axe's 4.45. It is DR-005 gap 2's own pair, arriving on a third surface. Entries 3 and 19 both render `--no-nav`, so the entire `.k-section-nav` block written with the record-form layer in **D-088** had painted nothing at all until this screen — **L-030's shape for the fifth time in this build, and the first occurrence this build introduced itself rather than inherited from the delivery.**
  - **Fixed in the build, NOT registered as a Design Request**, and the distinction is the load-bearing part: no delivered file states this control's colour. `template-record-form.md` §1 names the section nav and specifies its geometry — 180px, sticky, a chip row at 900–1199 — and says nothing about its text colour. The token was therefore the build's own choice and the build owns the defect, which is the same call **D-078** made for the three pairs `klytos-admin.css` had already ruled on. Substituting a colour the delivery DOES specify would have been Phase 4 rule 2's violation; this is not that.
  - Now `--texto-primario`: **14.79:1 light / 15.29:1 dark**. Both **pinned as floors** at the fixed value rather than at 4.5, so a quiet slide back fails here instead of at some later screen's axe run — and **proven by planting the original token back**, which turned three light-theme axe runs and both floor tests red together, then restoring the file byte-identically (`diff` clean).
  - The `:hover` rule's redundant `color` declaration went with it: the colour is `--texto-primario` at rest, so restating it in hover was a second place for one value to drift. §2's hover is a background change and the label not moving, which is what remains.
- **THREE DELIBERATE ABSENCES, each an adaptation with a reason rather than an omission** (`BUILD-SPEC.md` §5.9, rows 23–25):
  1. **No Taxonomies card**, although the shipped screen had one. The manifest gives taxonomies to entry 19, which now creates one into a chosen post type, deletes one, and links each to `taxonomy.php` — the same three operations, one screen away and driven. Nothing leaves the product; drawing the collection twice would be two implementations free to drift.
  2. **No "Delete this post type" card.** §2 names a destructive section on this template and uses a post type as its example, but entry 39's own card list has none and entry 19's collection already carries the delete with its server-side two-step and its truthful armed label. A second destructive path to one operation is duplication. **Raised with the user in the session report rather than decided silently** — it is the one genuinely two-sided reading in the slice, and it is reversible in one block.
  3. **The option rows are three static rows plus a JavaScript "Add another option"**, where the shipped screen built every row in script and revealed them only for choice types. With the script absent that screen let a `select` be created with **no options and no explanation**. Driven with JavaScript actually disabled: a two-option `select` is created and the row reports "Options: 2", while the enhancement button stays hidden because its script never ran.
- **THE TOOLBAR SAVE IS PRESENT HERE, unlike entry 19, and the card stack is still not one form.** Three cards carry savable fields and two are collections whose rows own their actions — and a form cannot nest inside a form. The savable controls therefore associate with `#k-post-type-form` by `form=`, the same mechanism the toolbar button itself already uses (D-088). That association is also what makes **Enter in a text field save** (§4), with no script involved in either half; both are driven.
- **The armed labels state what the code really does, in both collections.** `removeCustomField()` drops the DEFINITION and leaves whatever records already store, so the label says the field only and the values are kept. `removeStatus()` **reassigns every record holding that status to `draft`**, so the label says records become drafts. §2's "34 records will be deleted" would be false twice over; the shape is the design's and the consequence is the code's, exactly as D-089 established.
- **The ID is `readonly`, not `disabled`, and carries neither `name` nor `form`.** §2's "read-only vs disabled" distinction is a statement about truth: `update()` refuses the id by construction, so read-only is accurate and disabled would say "not now". It cannot post at all, and the assertion was **proven by planting `disabled` back** and watching its test go red.
- **`SYSTEM_STATUSES` is refused in the SCREEN as well as in the manager** — not a duplicated rule but a duplicated MESSAGE: the manager throws, and a thrown message lands in the summary as a server-reason row with no field to jump to. Refusing it here puts the sentence beside the field the person has to change. Driven, and asserted not to contain the word "Exception".
- **One i18n call the screen deliberately does not make:** the field-type `<option>` text is the type **id** in mono (`text`, `checkbox_group`, `relationship`), as the shipped screen drew it, grouped under translated category headings. Translating 26 type ids would be 520 catalogue strings for identifiers people paste into code; leaving `getFieldTypes()`'s English labels in place would be untranslated prose. The id is neither.
- **i18n: 85 new keys × 20 catalogues**, pure additions (**1,740 insertions, 0 deletions**), parity PASS. New domain `post_type.*`; every count string number-neutral (D-076).
- **Seven new hooks** — filters `admin.post_type.custom_fields` / `.statuses`, actions `admin.post_type.before` / `.before_identity` / `.before_custom_fields` / `.after_statuses` / `.after` — each with its `docs/api/INDEX.md` row (**993 → 1000**). The two released hooks the shipped screen exposed, `admin.post_type_edit.update_data` and `admin.post_type_edit.after_settings`, are **kept firing exactly where they were**: plugins use them.
- **Tree state (re-measured):** PHP **335 tests / 1628 assertions**, 0 skips (unchanged — this slice added no PHP surface) · browser tier **218 passing** (was 190; +28), whole tier re-run · `keel-verify` **20 checks: 15 pass, 5 warnings**, test-point rows 16 → **17** (row count confirmed to move, per L-038) · `keel-doctor --check` green · lint on the rewritten screen **0 errors / 0 warnings** · `docs/api/INDEX.md` **1000**.
- Alternatives rejected (and why): **build the Exposure card over four new booleans** (product scope inside a fidelity stage, and it changes what the outside world can read — an authorization review, not a card) · **register the section-nav contrast as a DR-005 addendum 3** (the delivery never specified that colour; calling a build-side token choice a delivery gap would inflate what Design owes and leave a real defect shipped) · **darken `--texto-secundario` itself** (that token IS delivered, and changing it would move every surface DR-005 measures) · **keep the Taxonomies card so nothing is "removed"** (the operation is not removed, only its second drawing) · **add a Delete card because §2 says "always"** (§2 is the template's; the manifest's per-screen card list is the screen's, and entry 19 already delivers the operation) · **edit statuses through a modal like the shipped screen** (the template has no modal idiom, and the shipped one worked only with JavaScript) · **re-list the 26 field types in the screen** (a second source of truth for what the manager accepts) · **translate the 26 type ids** (520 catalogue strings for identifiers).
- Bounds: `installer/admin/post-type-edit.php`, `installer/admin/assets/css/klytos-components.css`, the 20 catalogues (+85 keys each), `tests/E2E/post-type.spec.js`, `tests/E2E/fixtures/reset-post-type.php`, `docs/api/INDEX.md` (993 → 1000), `docs/BUILD-SPEC.md` §5.9 adaptations 23–26, `docs/05-test-points.md`, `docs/PROGRESS.md`. Supersedes nothing; extends **D-088** and **D-089**.

## D-091 — Entry 6 (Security) is built as three cards of five plus three the manifest never named, and `[hidden]` lost to `display:flex` for the fifth time
- Date / phase: 2026-08-10 / **Phase 4 Step 4, stage 5 of 6 — the form screens, BATCH B, screen 3**
- Decision: build manifest **entry 6 (Security)** on the `record-form` template as **three of its five cards**, deferring Content-Security-Policy and Integrity score under the user's standing answer 1 of **D-088**; carry over the **three shipped cards the manifest's list does not name**; build §6's re-auth step as a server-side second step that doubles as §2's destructive confirm; and close the recurring `[hidden]` defect with a mechanical check rather than another comment.
- **THE SURVEY, RE-RUN PER SCREEN RATHER THAN TAKEN ON D-088's WORD** — the rule D-089 earned, applied for the third time. Read against `TwoFactor` and `UserManager` rather than against a record:
  - **Two-factor** — backed. `enableTotp()` / `disableTotp()` / `enableMagicLink()` / `disableMagicLink()` / `getUserConfig()`, and the re-auth §6 requires is `UserManager::authenticate()`, which this screen already used for the encryption-level change.
  - **Passkeys** — backed for LIST and REMOVE (`getUserConfig()`, `removePasskey()`). ADD is a WebAuthn ceremony and cannot exist without script; the endpoint it posts to already exists and is unchanged.
  - **Recovery codes** — backed. `generateRecoveryCodes()` / `countRecoveryCodes()`.
  - **Content-Security-Policy** — **NOT backed, and deferred.** Klytos SENDS a CSP (`Auth::sendSecurityHeaders()`, per-request nonce) and has no editor, no store and no validator for a directive set. A textarea with a validate action over a live security header is a slice with its own failure modes.
  - **Integrity score** — **NOT backed, and deferred.** The data belongs to entry 34 (System integrity); `IntegrityManager` reports per-file verdicts and nothing summarises them into a figure. A score would be a number this build invented.
  Both were already recorded in `docs/roadmap.md` §0c by D-088 and stay there. The redesign is not reportable as complete while they stand.
- **THREE CARDS THE MANIFEST'S LIST DOES NOT NAME ARE BUILT ANYWAY, and that is the standing rule rather than an exception** (§5.9 row 29): **Encryption level**, **Recovery keys** and the destructive **Turn off two-factor authentication**. All three are shipped product, this screen is their only surface, and no other manifest entry claims them — entry 34 is a different thing. Removing shipped behaviour is not a fidelity decision (D-075, D-079). Each keeps its `site.configure` gate exactly where it was, enforced on the POST branch and mirrored in the markup's visibility, and the split is driven as `editor`.
- **THE RE-AUTH STEP IS SERVER-SIDE, AND IT IS ALSO THE DESTRUCTIVE CONFIRM** (§5.9 row 28). §6's delta says the switches are "each confirmed by a re-auth step"; §2 requires an inline two-step confirm that is never a browser `confirm()`. One mechanism serves both: the switch posts, the card re-renders asking for the current password, the second post applies it — so it behaves identically with JavaScript disabled, and it is driven in that state. **Turning TOTP ON is the one toggle with no password step**, deliberately: its confirmation is the enrolment ceremony, and a code from the authenticator is a strictly stronger claim than the password, asked in the same interaction. The absence of the password step there is ASSERTED, so the two cannot be quietly collapsed later.
- **THE TOOLBAR SAVE IS ABSENT, and it is an adaptation with a reason** (§5.9 row 27). §1 says the primary Save lives in the toolbar on every form screen; §6's delta makes every control on this screen immediate-effect, which §4 defines as the switch idiom. A Save here would submit nothing — D-089's rule, earned on entry 19, applied a second time. The one card with staged fields, Encryption level, carries its Save in that card's footer because it submits that card's two fields and nothing else.
- **THE PASSKEYS CARD IS A COLLECTION, NOT A SWITCH** (§5.9 row 30), although §6 says "2FA and passkey controls are switches". A switch restores what it turns off; a passkey is created by an authenticator ceremony and destroyed by `removePasskey()`, there is no state an "off" position could put back, and an account may hold several. The row's Remove goes through the same re-auth step, and the ADD control is **hidden** where `navigator.credentials` is absent rather than shown as a control that cannot act.
- **THE ONE REAL PRODUCT DEFECT WAS THAT HIDING, AND IT IS THE FIFTH OCCURRENCE OF ONE MECHANISM.** `.k-field { display: flex }` is (0,1,0) — the same specificity as the user agent's `[hidden] { display: none }` — and an author stylesheet beats the UA on ORIGIN whatever the order. So the passkey enrolment form kept its `hidden` attribute, kept being reported as hidden by every reading of the DOM, and was painted and operable in a browser with no WebAuthn at all: a control that cannot do the only thing it exists for. It has now arrived as the bulk bar visible on load (D-079), the Logs payload copy button (D-085) and this, and **every one of them was found by driving, never by reading**, because the source says `hidden` and looks right.
  - Fixed with `.k-field[hidden]`, the pattern the file already documents. **But the pattern is the problem:** the stylesheet carried a comment at `.k-btn[hidden]` stating the general rule in as many words, and it has been forgotten five times. So the fix is a rule **plus `scripts/keel-verify` check 21** — every element in the admin's markup carrying a bare `hidden` attribute and a `k-*` class is checked against the component stylesheets, and a class that declares a non-`none` `display` without its own `[hidden]` rule FAILS. **Proven by planting the missing rule back**, watching it name the real file and class, and restoring byte-identically. Its FIRST run produced a false positive of its own — `\bhidden\b` matches inside `aria-hidden="true"`, because `-` is a word boundary — caught and fixed before the check was trusted, which is the same duty as proving it fails.
- **ONE FINDING IS THE DELIVERY'S, and it is DR-005 gap 3 reaching the other half of the population it named.** The request said "every error message and destructive button inside a card". The message arrived on entry 3; the **button** arrives here — `.k-btn--destructive` is `--color-peligro` on `--fondo-elevado`, **4.32:1** dark, the identical pair. It is not new and it is not this screen's: the class has existed since stage 3 and entries 19 and 39 both render it, but only in the ARMED state of a two-step confirm, and every axe run on those screens measured the default, the populated and the error states. **This is L-030's shape once more, about which STATE a pass reaches rather than which element it scopes to.** Registered rather than fixed (the palette is Design's — Phase 4 rule 2), excluded by selector, and **both themes pinned as floors, each proven to FAIL on a planted colour** in its own theme.
- **A FLOOR THAT COULD NOT FAIL, caught by trying to make it fail.** `.k-card--secret` leaves `--texto-sutil` for `--sobre-tinte-aviso`, and the floor guarding that choice was first written at 4.5 — where it **passed with the override removed**, making it a test that could not fail for the reason it existed. Re-pinned at the measured values (**6.75:1 dark / 5.95:1 light**) and re-proven red without the override. Said plainly in the test rather than implied: `getComputedStyle().backgroundColor` returns the card's base colour, not the tint composited over it, so that reading is of the token pair and the axe runs beside it are what measure the true composite.
- **`.k-card--secret` layers the tint over the card rather than replacing its background** (§5.9 row 31). `--sobre-tinte-aviso` is measured against `--tinte-aviso` composited over `--fondo-elevado`, which is what every other consumer of the pair sits on; a card sits on `--fondo-ventana`, so `background: var(--tinte-aviso)` alone would have composited over the WINDOW and quietly measured a different pair — DR-005's addendum in miniature, the same tokens one surface further out.
- **One shipped string corrected rather than carried:** `security.recovery_codes_remaining` was "{count} codes remaining", which reads "1 codes remaining" on a real site. This mechanism has no plural forms (D-076), so it is now number-neutral in all 20 catalogues; the Spanish string also carried "codigos" without its accent.
- **Two browser dialogs removed, for the reason §2 gives about `confirm()`:** the shipped passkey flow used `prompt()` for the label and `alert()` for both outcomes. The label is now a real field with a visible `<label for>` in the card, and the outcome is announced in the page's own `role="status"` region.
- **i18n: 39 new keys × 20 catalogues** plus the one correction, pure additions otherwise, parity PASS.
- **Five new hooks** — filters `admin.security.methods` / `.passkeys`, actions `admin.security.before_passkeys` / `.before_recovery_codes` / `.after` — each with its `docs/api/INDEX.md` row (**1000 → 1005**). The five hooks the shipped screen already fired are **kept firing exactly where they were** and now carry a payload; adding parameters to an action does not break a listener.
- **Tree state (re-measured):** PHP **335 tests / 1629 assertions**, 0 skips (+1 assertion: check 21's row in `KeelVerifyTest::EXPECTED_CHECKS`) · browser tier **253 passing** (was 218; +35), whole tier re-run because `fixtures.js` changed · `keel-verify` **21 checks: 16 pass, 5 warnings**, test-point rows 17 → **18** (row count confirmed to move, L-038) · `keel-doctor --check` green · lint on both new files **0 errors / 0 warnings** · `docs/api/INDEX.md` **1005**.
- Alternatives rejected (and why): **build the CSP editor over the header Klytos already sends** (product scope inside a fidelity stage, over a live security header, with no store and no validator) · **summarise an integrity score from `IntegrityManager`'s per-file verdicts** (inventing a metric the delivery never defines and entry 34 does not expose) · **drop the Encryption level and Recovery keys cards because the manifest's list omits them** (removing shipped behaviour, and no other entry claims them) · **render the toolbar Save anyway for template consistency** (a control that submits nothing) · **skip the re-auth step because the shipped screen had none** (§6 states it and the product backs it; the shipped flow let a hijacked session remove every second factor) · **ask for the password on TOTP enrolment too** (two confirmations where the stronger one is already being asked for) · **draw each passkey as a switch** (a credential has no off position to restore) · **fix `.k-btn--destructive` by darkening `--color-peligro`** (that token IS delivered — Phase 4 rule 2, and DR-005 already owns it) · **write another comment about `[hidden]` instead of a check** (the comment existed and was forgotten five times) · **leave the `.k-card--secret` floor at 4.5** (a floor that passes without the thing it guards is decoration).
- Bounds: `installer/admin/security.php`, `installer/admin/partials/security-reauth.php` (new), `installer/admin/assets/css/klytos-components.css`, the 20 catalogues (+39 keys each, 1 corrected), `scripts/keel-verify` (check 21), `tests/Unit/KeelVerifyTest.php`, `tests/E2E/security.spec.js` (new), `tests/E2E/fixtures/reset-security.php` (new), `tests/E2E/fixtures.js`, `docs/api/INDEX.md` (1000 → 1005), `docs/BUILD-SPEC.md` §5.4 + §5.9 adaptations 27–31, `docs/05-test-points.md`, `docs/PROGRESS.md`. Supersedes nothing; extends **D-088**, **D-089**, **D-090** and **DR-005**.

## D-092 — Entry 25 (Consent) is built as three cards, the shipped banner is rewritten against §10.4, and the audit was returning nothing on every install since it shipped
- Date / phase: 2026-08-10 / **Phase 4 Step 4, stage 5 of 6 — the form screens, BATCH B, screen 4**
- Decision: build manifest **entry 25 (Consent)** on the `record-form` template as **two of its three cards plus the preview its delta names**, deferring Acceptance stats under D-088's standing answer 1; **rewrite the banner half of `installer/core/assets/consent-manager.js`** against `SPEC/accessibility.md` §10.4, which entry 25's own deltas bind it to; take the audit table's columns and widths from the delivery's prototype and add the missing row to DR-006; and register the delivery's self-contradiction as **DR-008**. All three calls were put to the user as one batch before the first line and all three are theirs.
- **THE SURVEY, RE-RUN PER SCREEN RATHER THAN TAKEN ON D-088's WORD** — the rule D-089 earned, applied a fourth time. Read against `ConsentManager` rather than against a record:
  - **Banner configuration** — backed. `getConfig()` / `saveConfig()` carry `enabled`, `banner_text`, `privacy_url`, `cookie_days`.
  - **Cookie audit** — backed. `getAuditReport()` / `getPluginDeclarations()`.
  - **Acceptance stats (stat row)** — **NOT backed, and deferred.** There is no acceptance data anywhere in the product: Klytos publishes a STATIC site, the visitor's choice is written to a cookie in their own browser, and nothing receives, stores or aggregates it. The prototype's "Accepted everything 62%" is visitor telemetry — a collection surface with its own privacy question, since counting consent choices is itself processing. Carried into `roadmap.md` §0c.
- **THE SLICE'S LARGEST FINDING IS NOT ABOUT THIS SCREEN: `getPluginDeclarations()` HAD BEEN RETURNING NOTHING ON EVERY INSTALL SINCE THE FEATURE SHIPPED.** `StorageInterface::list()` is documented as returning "Array of decrypted RECORDS" and every other manager in core uses it that way; `ConsentManager` alone treated the return as a list of IDs and fed each record back into `read()`, which wants a string. Every record threw, and a bare `catch ( \Throwable ) { continue; }` swallowed every throw. So the cookie audit was empty on every site, the JSON and CSV exports contained nothing, and `klytos_list_consent_declarations` and `klytos_get_consent_audit` returned nothing over MCP. **A GDPR audit trail that answers "no cookies declared" whatever is declared is worse than an absent one**, because it answers confidently. Found by DRIVING — the fixture's files were plainly on disk while the screen said zero — never by reading. Fixed **test-first** (`tests/Integration/ConsentDeclarationsTest.php`, 5 tests), with the skip kept and NARROWED to non-array records rather than deleted: it was doing two jobs and only the hiding was unwanted.
  - **The first red was the WRONG red and was not accepted.** The tests were written in `tests/Unit/` first and failed with `App::getOptionsManager(): Return value must be of type OptionsManager, null returned` — the absent App, not the defect. `ConsentManager::getConfig()` reads through `klytos_get_option()`, so the class is not unit-testable, for the same reason authorization is not. Moved to the integration tier, where the red reads `Failed asserting that actual size 0 matches expected size 1`, which is the defect itself. **Proven by planting the original loop back**: all five went red together, then the file was restored byte-identically (`diff -q` clean).
  - **A second defect rode along in the same class**, surfaced by the same run as a PHP warning: `sanitizeCookies()` wrote `in_array( $cookie['type'] ?? 'cookie', … ) ? $cookie['type'] : 'cookie'`, where the `??` guards the CONDITION and the true branch reads the key raw — so every declaration that omits `type`, which any may, passed the test on the defaulted value and then stored `null`. The type is what tells a compliance reader whether an entry is a cookie or browser storage.
- **THE SHIPPED BANNER MET NONE OF §10.4's FIVE PROPERTIES, and the entry's deltas put it in scope.** §25 binds "the banner preview **and the shipped banner**" to the equal-prominence rule and adds `role="dialog" aria-modal="true"`, focus trapped, `Esc` = reject non-essential. What shipped had: `role="dialog"` with **no** `aria-modal`; an `aria-label` and **no heading**; **no** focus move and **no** trap; **no** Esc handler; and its reject-equivalent ("Solo obligatorias") drawn as `cm-btn-secondary` beside a `cm-btn-primary` "Aceptar todas" — **precisely the colour trick the rule names**. All five built and each driven in a real browser against the real library.
  - **The two choices now carry ONE class, `cm-btn-choice`**, with `min-width` on it: there is no second declaration for one to drift away in, and equal padding around unequal words draws unequal buttons in every one of the 20 catalogues. The preview card is built the same way for the same reason, and both pairs are asserted by reading class list, box, weight and colour **out of the browser**.
  - **§10.4's own two halves are reconciled by Esc, not by weakening the trap.** "focus trapped" and "the site is operable by keyboard before a choice is made" only compose if there is always a keyboard route out; Esc is that route and it records the lawful minimal choice rather than merely dismissing — which is what the manifest's delta says in as many words. Asserted: after Esc, `getConsentState()` is non-null with analytics and marketing false.
  - **Focus is RETURNED before the element is removed, not after.** The banner lingers 400ms for its transition and its trap lingers with it, so a Tab in that window would still be caught by a dialog already answered.
  - **Two further shipped gaps closed in passing, both of them fields that did nothing.** `cookie_days` was editable in the admin and never reached the library, whose 365-day constant won every time — configuring the duration had no effect since the feature shipped. And the banner's own words were hardcoded Spanish on a product whose base language is English with 20 catalogues (D-006); they are now translated server-side and passed through `init({labels})`, because the generated site is static and has no runtime to translate in. The Spanish fallbacks are kept, in the language they shipped in, so an older stored config renders what it always did rather than `undefined`.
- **THE DELIVERY CONTRADICTS ITSELF ON THE RULE IT CALLS STRICTEST → DR-008.** `manifest.md` §25 says the configuration screen "offers **no** option to make reject less prominent; that option does not exist" and `accessibility.md` §10.4 forbids the prominence difference. The PROTOTYPE draws both: a switch labelled "Reject is as prominent as accept", and a preview whose "Aceptar" is `variant="primary"` beside a `variant="secondary"` "Rechazar". Two normative files agree and only the drawing disagrees, so the build follows the SPEC — **the same precedent as D-074**, where `SPEC/navigation.md` beat the prototypes' `navGroups`. The screen therefore renders the rule as a `<p>` and **asserts it is not a control**, so the drawing's version cannot arrive later without failing.
- **THE COOKIE AUDIT IS A FOURTEENTH LIST SURFACE DR-006's SENT TABLE OMITS.** §25 calls the card a list-table and records neither columns nor widths — the shape of 28 Licence and 35 Updates. What makes it different from those twelve is that **nothing contradicts**: DR-006's stated reason for refusing the prototypes was that their tables carry DIFFERENT COLUMNS from the manifest's, and here the manifest has none to differ from. Three sources agree instead — the prototype draws `170px 120px 90px 1fr` over Cookie · Type · Duration · Description, and the shipped screen already renders those four headings. **The user chose to take them**, logged as an adaptation, with the missing row added to DR-006 so Design still records it normatively.
- **ONE CONTRAST DEFECT, and it is the build's own, so it is FIXED rather than registered.** The preview's body text drew `--texto-secundario` on `--fondo-ventana`: **4.46:1 light**, DR-005 gap 2's own token pair on a fourth surface, recomputed independently in Python and agreeing with axe's 4.45. No delivered file states this control's colour — the preview card is entry 25's own delta and the token was this build's choice — so this is **D-090's call exactly**, not a further DR-005 addendum. Now `--texto-primario`: 14.79:1 light / 15.29:1 dark, **proven by planting the original back** and watching the light whole-page scan go red, then restored byte-identically.
- **A SAVE THAT DELETED DATA IT DOES NOT EDIT, fixed and pinned.** `saveConfig()` replaces `categories` wholesale, and the screen this replaces built that array from `$_POST['custom_categories']` — a field its own form never rendered — so it always passed an empty array and **every save silently wiped every custom category**. Categories can only be created over MCP (`klytos_set_consent_config`, which merges and therefore preserves them), so the two surfaces disagreed and the admin one destroyed the other's data. The rewrite carries the stored value through, and a test asserts a custom category survives a save.
- **Two adaptations of shape, both with reasons** (`BUILD-SPEC.md` §5.9, rows 32–35): the per-plugin **Remove lives in an `<h3>` group inside the audit card** rather than in a fifth column, because §4 provides for groups inside a card while the four specified columns are per-COOKIE and a declaration is per-PLUGIN — reusing the `.k-collection` layer (D-089) rather than rebuilding it; and the armed label states what `deletePluginDeclaration()` really does — it removes the AUDIT ENTRY, the plugin stays installed and keeps setting its cookies — where §2's example sentence would be false twice over, exactly as D-089 and D-090 found.
- **The exports and the shipped stat figures both survive.** Export JSON and Export CSV move from the card's header to the footer §1 provides for; the four figures the shipped stat grid drew are backed and now live in the table's `<caption>`, which §2.1 requires to carry the count anyway. Removing shipped behaviour is not a fidelity decision (D-075).
- **One thing checked and found NOT to be a defect, recorded because the check is the point:** `consent.php` has no in-file `klytos_has_permission()` call, which reads as the project's largest known defect class. It is not one — the file is mapped to `site.configure` in `klytos_admin_gate_map()` and enforced centrally by `klytos_enforce_admin_gate()` (S-07's fix). A second in-file check would be a second place for one decision.
- **i18n: 40 new keys × 20 catalogues**, a new `consent.*` domain where none existed (the whole screen was hardcoded English before), spliced in as TEXT rather than re-serialised so the change is **840 insertions, 0 deletions** and reviewable. Every count string number-neutral (D-076).
- **Six new hooks** — filters `admin.consent.audit_rows` / `.declarations`, actions `admin.consent.before_banner` / `.after_banner` / `.before_audit` / `.after_audit` — each with its `docs/api/INDEX.md` row (**1005 → 1011**). The two the shipped screen already fired keep firing where they were. **The INDEX check caught my own mistake here**: both filters were first written into the Actions section, and the count and parity checks failed until they were moved — L-038's rule earning its keep a second time.
- **Tree state (re-measured):** PHP **340 tests / 1643 assertions**, 0 skips (was 335/1629; +5 the new integration tests) · browser tier **275 passing** (was 253; +22), whole tier re-run · `keel-verify` **21 checks: 16 pass, 5 warnings** (unchanged) · `keel-doctor --check` green, 14 rows · lint on `consent.php` **0 errors / 0 warnings**, `build-engine.php` unchanged at 0/5 · `docs/api/INDEX.md` **1011**.
- Alternatives rejected (and why): **build Acceptance stats over a new endpoint** (a collection surface with its own privacy question, invented inside a fidelity stage) · **leave the shipped banner alone and fix only the preview** (the delta binds both, and the preview would then advertise a banner the site does not ship) · **fix only the equal-prominence rule and defer the other four §10.4 properties** (partial compliance on the rule the bundle calls strictest) · **register the section-nav-shaped contrast pair as a DR-005 addendum 3** (the delivery never specified that colour; calling a build-side token choice a delivery gap would inflate what Design owes and leave a real defect shipped) · **leave the audit card DR-006-blocked** (strictly consistent with DR-006's wording, but DR-006's stated obstruction — prototype columns contradicting the manifest's — does not exist here; the user's call) · **add a fifth column for the Remove** (repeats one plugin's control on each of its cookies, and the manifest gives four) · **delete the bare `catch` outright** (trades a silent empty list for a fatal on one bad file) · **keep the tests in `tests/Unit`** (the red named the absent App rather than the defect, which is not a red worth trusting) · **substitute a colour in the shipped banner** (`#0066cc` measures 5.57:1 on white and already passes — a change made on an unverified assumption, reverted the moment it was measured).
- **The generated static site joined `.gitignore` in the same commit.** `BuildEngine::buildAll()` writes the published site into the repository ROOT — `home/`, `about/`, `contact/`, `search/`, `assets/`, `.well-known/`, `llms.txt`, `sitemap.xml`, `search-index.json`, `x402-gate.php` — and this screen's save path calls it, so every E2E run that exercises a save produced ~30 untracked files at the root. None of it was ever tracked; it is build output regenerated from `installer/`, and committing it would put an artifact in history and make each session's diff depend on which screens its tests happened to save. `.htaccess` is deliberately NOT listed: it is tracked source that the build REWRITES, so a change there must stay visible in `git status`. This is D-083's shape — the second time an untracked generated tree has dirtied the working tree, and ignoring it treats the cause rather than the symptom.
- Bounds: `installer/admin/consent.php`, `installer/core/consent-manager.php`, `installer/core/assets/consent-manager.js`, `installer/core/build-engine.php`, the 20 catalogues (+40 keys each), `tests/Integration/ConsentDeclarationsTest.php` (new), `tests/E2E/consent.spec.js` (new), `tests/E2E/fixtures/reset-consent.php` (new), `docs/api/INDEX.md` (1005 → 1011), `docs/BUILD-SPEC.md` §5.9 adaptations 32–35, `docs/05-test-points.md`, `docs/roadmap.md` §0c + §0d, `docs/design/design-requests/DR-008.md` (new), `docs/design/design-requests/DR-006.md` (addendum), `docs/PROGRESS.md`. Supersedes nothing; extends **D-088**, **D-089**, **D-090** and **DR-006**.

## D-093 — DR-007 resolved: the console stream gets a third target-size exception and a measured selected line, and entry 41 owes rework it was not wrong to need
- Date / phase: 2026-08-10 / **Phase 4 Step 4** (re-delivery audit, between stage 5 screens 4 and 5)
- Decision: install the DR-007 re-delivery as a **wholesale swap**, re-gate it, mark **DR-007 resolved**, record the **rework it creates on the already-shipped entry 41**, and delete the staging tree — after, and only after, the export root was accounted for file by file.
- **What the re-delivery is.** 124 files, and `diff -rq` against the archived delivery reports **exactly the five files DR-007 names, 0 added, 0 removed**: `open-questions.md` (items 26 and 27 answered, header still `Status: zero unresolved items`), `accessibility.md` (new §7.1), `template-console-stream.md`, `manifest.md` (**entry 41 only**) and `color-contrast-audit.md` (**purely additive** — `diff` removes no line, so §1c's 72/72 recomputation stands unrepeated, verified rather than assumed). Evidence: `BUILD-SPEC.md` **§1f**.
- **THE ANSWER TO GAP 1 IS A DOCUMENTED THIRD EXCEPTION, NOT A CONCESSION.** `accessibility.md` §7 previously closed with "No other exception exists. If a build produces a smaller target, that is a defect" — which is why the finding was raised rather than absorbed. §7.1 now grants the 19px stream line under 2.5.8's *essential* clause, with the reasoning stated (a 24px line-height costs a quarter of the lines in the same 60vh, and §3 already makes the stream the one permitted horizontal-scroll surface for the same reason) and **four testable conditions**: the line is the only target in its row, it spans the full stream width, `↑`/`↓` + `Enter`/`Space` operate it from the keyboard, and it applies only to verbatim machine output. `.k-hit-24` is **explicitly forbidden** on a stream line — it cannot help and it moves the overlap onto the neighbouring row.
- **THE ANSWER TO GAP 2 IS A MEASUREMENT, AND IT CLOSES MORE THAN IT WAS ASKED.** A selected line paints **all** its text `--texto-primario` with the role colours suppressed, plus a 3px accent left bar so selection is not carried by a 1.16:1 background alone: **12.72:1 light / 9.52:1 dark**. Two corrections rode along, both of them ours to apply: an unselected line's values move off `--texto-secundario` (4.46:1 on `--fondo-ventana`, which **is DR-005 gap 2's own pair — closed here for the stream surface rather than re-raised**), and the highlighted line stops being bare `--color-acento` (4.23:1 light as text) for the tint treatment `ERROR` and `WARN` already use.
- **CONTRAST RECOMPUTED, NOT TRUSTED — and it is the part of the gate that cannot be delegated.** The 9 new **Composed pairs** measure text against a tint *composited over* a surface, which is a different arithmetic from the 72 pairs above them. Recomputed independently in Python from the token hexes, compositing each `rgba()` before taking the ratio: **18 of 18 agree** within rounding (±0.05). That includes the four rows the table marks FAIL and withdraws — **confirmed to fail rather than taken on the delivery's word**, which is the direction a gate is most tempted to skip.
- **The general rule the audit now states is worth more than the nine rows:** *a token that passes on a surface is not thereby passing on a tint over that surface.* That is DR-005's whole shape, stated by the delivery itself, and it should be applied to any future composition before it is specified.
- **ENTRY 41 IS NOW BEHIND ITS OWN SPEC IN EIGHT PLACES, and that is not a defect in what was built.** It was shipped 2026-08-09 (D-085) faithful to the wording it was built against; the wording changed. The eight are enumerated in `roadmap.md` **§0e** with the file and section that mandates each — the per-line copy button **withdrawn**, `Copy line` in the detail header, `Copy all` in the control row honouring filters and truncation, values to `--texto-primario`, the highlighted line to `--sobre-tinte-acento` on `--tinte-acento`, the selected line's full treatment plus its bar, keyboard line navigation promoted from convenience to **condition**, and `.k-hit-24` forbidden. **Not started in this session, deliberately:** it is a slice, the tree was clean and pushed at a known-good point, and doing it inside a re-delivery audit would mix two kinds of work whose evidence is read differently.
- **The tests must be UN-PINNED, and that is the half most easily lost.** `tests/E2E/logs.spec.js` excludes DR-007's two findings by selector and pins their measured ratios as floors, on the standing rule that an open request must not become a licence to regress. The request is closed and the delivery now specifies passing values, so those exclusions and floors must be **removed** — an exclusion that outlives its Design Request is a permanent blind spot wearing the costume of a known issue.
- **FOUR OF THE FIVE OPEN REQUESTS ARE UNTOUCHED, said plainly because a partial re-delivery read as a complete one is how a gate rots.** **DR-004** — `template-shell.md` is byte-identical. **DR-005** — only the stream surface of gap 2 is closed; the badge in a selected row, `--color-peligro` on `--fondo-elevado` and the sidebar's current nav item are all untouched, and both addenda still stand. **DR-006** — `manifest.md`'s diff is entry 41 only, so twelve list surfaces still carry no `grid-template-columns`. **DR-008** — `screens/**` is byte-identical, so the Consent prototype still draws `Aceptar` primary beside `Rechazar` secondary and still draws the switch §25 says does not exist. It could not have changed.
- **THE EXPORT ROOT WAS ACCOUNTED FOR BEFORE THE TREE WAS DELETED**, which is the step D-083 exists to enforce — its predecessor's "verified byte-identical" covered only the `design_handoff_klytos_admin/` subtree and missed nine prototypes. Of the 44 files outside the delivery: nine prototypes differ from the delivery's `screens/` by **exactly one line each** (the ⌘K hint's `top:calc(50% - 5px)` versus `top:50%` — the identical difference D-083 recorded, delivery canonical) and one is identical; **9 of 13 brand SVGs are byte-identical** and the other **4 were each proven byte-identical to the delivery's own `*-text.svg` preservation**, so nothing is lost; the `_ds` bundle and the three support files are byte-identical; and `css/klytos-components.css` was **deliberately not taken** — it is the file the BUILD writes, and taking it is a principle-8 violation (the same refusal D-083 recorded). 124 + 43 + 1 = **168, all accounted for.**
- Alternatives rejected (and why): **apply the entry-41 rework in this session** (a re-delivery audit and a build slice produce different evidence and mixing them makes both harder to read; the tree was clean and pushed) · **leave `logs.spec.js`'s exclusions in place "until someone gets to it"** (an exclusion outliving its request is a blind spot that reads as a known issue) · **merge the new delivery file-by-file** (contract rule 10 exists precisely so the directory stays wholesale-replaceable) · **delete the staging tree first and audit the installed copy** (the root's 43 files exist only in the staging tree; deleting first destroys the only thing that could prove nothing was lost) · **take the root's `css/klytos-components.css`** (principle 8) · **trust the 9 declared composed ratios** (the gate's one non-delegable step, and four of them are FAIL rows whose failure had to be confirmed).
- Bounds: `docs/design/design-handoff/` (swapped, 124 files), `docs/old/design-handoff/DR-007/` (new archive, 124 files), `docs/BUILD-SPEC.md` §1f, `docs/design/design-requests/DR-007.md` (resolved), `docs/roadmap.md` §0e, `docs/PROGRESS.md`, and the deletion of `docs/klytos-cms-redesign/`. **No code changed in this session.** Supersedes nothing; resolves **DR-007** and extends **D-085**.

## D-094 — Entry 41's DR-007 rework is applied, and re-enabling the rule the closed request had disabled found a third failing composition
- Date / phase: 2026-08-10 / **Phase 4 Step 4** (rework, ahead of stage 6 per `roadmap.md` §0e)
- Decision: apply all eight items DR-007's answer owes entry 41, **un-pin** `logs.spec.js`'s now-obsolete DR-007 exclusions and floors, and register the new finding that un-pinning exposed as **DR-009**.
- **What was built, each item traceable to the file that mandates it** (`roadmap.md` §0e):
  1. **The per-line copy button is GONE.** `accessibility.md` §7.1's first condition is that the line is the ONLY target in its row, so a second 19px control there would revoke the grounds for the very exception the axe pass now relies on. Its absence is **asserted**, not merely untested.
  2. **Copy line** in the detail panel's header, `sm` (28px), revealed with the selection, copying the line **as STORED** via a new `data-raw` — not reassembled from the parsed fields, which could drift from the `sprintf()` in `Logger::write()`.
  3. **Copy all** in the control row, naming its count and honouring **filters and truncation**, because §4 says a copy button names what it copies and a control that names a count it does not copy is worse than one that names none. Driven with a level filter applied, asserting every copied line is an ERROR line.
  4–6. The stream's colours: base text `--texto-secundario` → **`--texto-primario`**; a selected line paints **all** its text `--texto-primario` with the role colours suppressed, plus a **3px** accent left bar (the bar is not decoration — `--fila-seleccion` over `--fondo-ventana` is a 1.16:1 background change, so selection carried by it alone is not a second channel). Tinted selected lines take the same treatment, measured at **12.67 / 11.74** and **12.86 / 10.49**.
  7. **`↑`/`↓` between lines and `Enter`/`Space` to select**, bound on the focusable stream group — and it is a **condition** of §7.1's grant rather than a convenience, so it is driven with the real keyboard.
  8. **`.k-hit-24` is asserted absent** from every stream line.
- **THE UN-PINNING WAS NOT HOUSEKEEPING — IT WAS THE SLICE'S SECOND FINDING.** `logs.spec.js` had disabled `color-contrast` on the stream pass while DR-007 was open. Re-enabling it, which the old comment itself said would happen when Design answered, immediately surfaced a **third** composition the blanket exemption had been covering all along: `--texto-sutil` (structure) on a **tint** over `--fondo-ventana` measures **3.88 / 4.18** on `--tinte-peligro` and **3.94 / 3.73** on `--tinte-aviso` — below AA in both tints and both themes. Recomputed independently in Python; axe reports 3.93 for the light/`aviso` case and the arithmetic agrees.
  - **It is the delivery's, so it is registered rather than fixed** (Phase 4 rule 2): §1 specifies `--texto-sutil` for structure AND the tint as the line background, and the build chose neither. **DR-009 drafted, not sent.**
  - **And it is pointed:** the composed-pairs table DR-007 *just added* states the general rule — "a token that passes on a surface is not thereby passing on a tint over that surface" — measures `--texto-sutil` on `--fondo-ventana` (PASS) and on `--fila-seleccion` (FAIL, withdrawn), and never measures it on a **semantic tint** over that surface, which is what every ERROR and WARN line renders.
  - Scoped narrowly: SELECTED tinted lines are unaffected because item 5 makes their text `--texto-primario`, so the exclusion carries `:not([aria-pressed="true"])` and the selected state stays fully checked. All four pairs are **pinned as floors at their measured values**, not at 4.5 — D-091's lesson about a floor that cannot fail.
- **ONE TEST DEFECT OF MY OWN, separated from the product findings, which was again part of the work.** The new "all four conditions" test swept every control outside the stream for a 24px target and reported the Follow **switch** at 38×22. §7's own table answers it: "Switch | 38 × 22 | **38 × 24** | label row is 24px tall; the switch's own label is part of the target." The target is the ROW, and the test was measuring the control. Fixed to measure what the spec names.
- **A CROSS-TIER DEFECT I INTRODUCED LAST SESSION, surfaced only now.** Saving on Consent calls `buildAll()`, which writes the published site into the REPOSITORY ROOT — gitignored in D-092, which fixed the git symptom and not the cause. `AdminGateHttpTest` asserts for **NEW-04** that no build output sits in the repository root, so an E2E run that left it made the **PHP suite fail on code that was fine**. That is **L-025's shape exactly**: two tiers sharing one working tree, each corrupting the other's preconditions. `tests/E2E/fixtures/reset-consent.php` now removes the generated paths after every run — scoped to what `BuildEngine` creates, and never touching `.htaccess`, which is tracked source the build rewrites rather than output it creates.
- **i18n:** `logs.copy_line` **retitled** "Copy this line" → **"Copy line"** in all 20 catalogues (§4's wording changed), and `logs.copy_all` added. The delivery's example is "Copy all 412 lines", which is **not number-neutral** and this mechanism has no plural forms (D-076) — "Copy all 1 lines" would ship. The count is kept, as §4 requires, in a form correct at every value: **"Copy all lines ({count})"**. Logged as an adaptation.
- **Every change proven by planting the defect back:** the stream's base colour reverted to `--texto-secundario` and the per-line copy button restored — **four tests went red together**, including the §7.1 four-conditions test catching the reintroduced second target, which is exactly what it exists for. Both files then restored byte-identically (`diff -q` clean).
- **One transient recorded rather than hidden:** the first whole-tier run reported `content-model.spec.js`'s 320px reflow test failing. It passes in isolation and passed on the immediate re-run of the full tier (284/284), so it is order-dependent flake and not a regression — said here rather than quietly re-run until green.
- **What is NOT built, and it is deliberate:** §1's "the one line that matters", which the answer moves to `--sobre-tinte-acento` on `--tinte-acento`. **Logs has no such concept** — nothing in this screen marks a line as the one that matters — so building the rule would be writing a component with no consumer, which is L-039 exactly. Recorded as absent rather than implemented ahead of a consumer; stage 6's four screens may bring one.
- Alternatives rejected (and why): **leave `color-contrast` disabled on the stream** (it was disabled for two specific pairs that now pass; leaving it off would have kept DR-009 invisible indefinitely — an exclusion outliving its request is a blind spot in costume) · **fix DR-009 by substituting a token** (both halves are the delivery's — Phase 4 rule 2, and DR-005 exists for this exact class) · **re-open DR-007** (it answered what it was asked; a new gap its answer exposed is a new request, and re-opening resolved requests makes the register unreadable) · **keep the per-line copy button and rely on `.k-hit-24`** (§7.1 forbids it by name, and it cannot work: stacked 19px rows leave no undisturbed 24px) · **copy the delivery's "Copy all 412 lines" verbatim** (ships "Copy all 1 lines") · **fix the switch finding in the product** (the product is right and the test was wrong — §7's table says the row is the target) · **gitignore alone for the build output** (that fixed the git symptom while the PHP suite kept failing on it).
- Bounds: `installer/admin/logs.php`, `installer/admin/assets/css/klytos-components.css`, the 20 catalogues (1 retitled + 1 new key each), `tests/E2E/logs.spec.js`, `tests/E2E/fixtures/reset-consent.php`, `docs/design/design-requests/DR-009.md` (new), `docs/BUILD-SPEC.md` §5.9, `docs/05-test-points.md`, `docs/roadmap.md` §0e, `docs/PROGRESS.md`. Supersedes nothing; completes **D-093** and extends **D-085**.

## D-095 — Entry 9's per-screen survey: eleven shipped groups onto five buildable sections, and two items the survey found that nobody had recorded
- Date / phase: 2026-08-10 / **Phase 4 Step 4, stage 5 of 6 — batch B, screen 5 (survey only; the build is NOT started)**
- Decision: run entry 9's per-screen survey against `SiteConfig` and the shipped `settings.php` BEFORE the first line, record its result, and hand the build off with the analysis done rather than begin a seven-section screen this session cannot finish to the standard the rest of the stage was built to.
- **THE SURVEY DISAGREED WITH THE STAGE-WIDE ONE AGAIN — four screens for four.** D-088 recorded entry 9's obstruction as "URLs and Media have no shipped settings". Both of those hold. Reading the shipped screen against the manager found two more things nobody had written down:
  - **The shipped screen has ELEVEN POST groups, not seven sections**: `general`, `social`, `analytics`, `email`, `languages`, `appearance`, `developer`, `encryption_key_backup`, `ai`, `maintenance`, `notices`. They are cards on ONE page; the manifest wants a seven-item section nav where "each section is its own page load". So the work is not "add a nav" — it is a **re-partition of eleven groups onto five sections**, and the manifest gives no mapping. That is D-088 answer 3's licence, and the mapping below is the build's own, logged as an adaptation when it lands.
  - **`encryption_key_backup` is very probably a SECOND surface for something entry 6 now owns.** D-091 built **Recovery keys** on Security. Two screens offering the same operation is the duplication D-090 refused for the Taxonomies card — but the two must be read against each other before either is touched, because they may not be the same thing (a key BACKUP and a recovery KEY are adjacent, not identical). **Whoever builds entry 9 checks this first and does not assume.**
- **THE DR-002 DELTA IS STILL UNBUILT, and entry 9 owns it.** `manifest.md` §9 records "the one existing entry DR-002 changes": search-engine and AI-crawler indexing moves to **Settings → Advanced** as a checkbox + Save with the consequence stated, gated at `site.configure`. The user CONFIRMED it in **D-072 answer 1**. It has not happened: `installer/admin/index.php` still carries the toggle (`$siteConfig['indexing_enabled']`, set at lines 27–40) and `settings.php` contains no indexing control at all. It is not a gap in the design and not a new decision — it is recorded work that no slice has picked up, found only by reading both files. **It also touches entry 44 (Dashboard), which is not built either**, so the two halves should land together or the Dashboard will keep a toggle the manifest has moved.
- **The mapping the build should use** (five sections; URLs and Media omitted until they have content, one line each to restore, per D-088 answer 3):
  - **Site** — `general` (site name, tagline, description) + `social` (six links).
  - **Locale** — `languages` (the code/name list) and the default language, which the shipped screen posts under `general`.
  - **Intelligence** — `ai` (the Gemini key) + `analytics` (the analytics id). The custom head/body scripts posted under `analytics` are NOT intelligence and belong to Advanced.
  - **Email** — `email` entire, including the test-send.
  - **Advanced** — `developer` (developer mode + the seven devbar switches), `appearance` (admin theme, admin bar), `maintenance`, `notices`, the custom head/body scripts, `encryption_key_backup` **if the check above says it is not entry 6's**, and **the indexing checkbox DR-002 moves here**.
- **Nothing is deferred as unbacked on this screen**, which makes it the first stage-5 screen where the survey found no missing product: every one of the eleven groups is backed by `SiteConfig` or by a manager the screen already calls. The obstruction is partitioning and the DR-002 debt, not absent data.
- **Why the build did not start here, said plainly rather than presented as a stopping point:** entry 9 is the largest form surface in the manifest — eleven groups, ~40 controls, a section nav that is the template's optional half rendered for only the second time, and an i18n cost of roughly eighty keys across twenty catalogues on the evidence of entries 39 (85) and 6 (39). Starting it with the context left would have produced a half-built screen and a hand-off describing one, which is worse for the next session than a survey and a clean tree. The analysis above is the part that does not have to be redone.
- Alternatives rejected (and why): **build Site and Locale only and leave the rest** (the section nav is a whole-screen contract — a nav listing five sections where two render is worse than no nav) · **treat the eleven groups as eleven cards on one page and skip the nav** (§9's delta is explicit that each section is its own page load, and the nav is the entry's defining feature) · **move the indexing toggle now as a one-liner** (it is a behaviour change to a shipped screen plus its Dashboard half, and doing it outside the slice that owns it is how the Dashboard ends up with a control the manifest moved) · **assume `encryption_key_backup` duplicates entry 6** (adjacent is not identical; D-090's rule was to check, and it was checked by reading the manager, not by inference).
- Bounds: `docs/decisions.md`, `docs/PROGRESS.md`, `docs/roadmap.md`. **No code changed.** Supersedes nothing; extends **D-088** and carries **D-072 answer 1**'s unbuilt half forward explicitly.

## D-096 — Entry 9 (Settings) is built as five sections of eleven groups, DR-002's two-year-old delta finally lands, and a compliance flag had never once been written
- Date / phase: 2026-08-10 / **Phase 4 Step 4, stage 5 of 6 — batch B, screen 5**
- Decision: build manifest entry 9 as **five section page loads** carrying **one form and one Save each**, apply DR-002's indexing move in **both halves** (Settings gains the control, the Dashboard loses it), and move the encryption-key affordance to **entry 6**, which owns that duty.

### What was built
`settings.php` rewritten against `template-record-form.md`, `SPEC/manifest.md` §9 and `SPEC/accessibility.md`. The eleven shipped POST groups map onto five sections exactly as **D-095** wrote them out; **URLs and Media stay omitted** (no shipped setting behind either — D-088 answer 3), and each is one line in `$sections` to restore. `tests/E2E/settings.spec.js` (**35 tests**), **76 new i18n keys × 20 catalogues**, one new filter and five changed action signatures in `docs/api/INDEX.md` (1011 → **1012**).

### Four decisions inside the build, each with its reason
- **One form per section, not one per card.** The template says the primary Save "lives in the toolbar… and it is the same button on every form screen" — singular. The shipped screen had eleven. A section is now one `<form>` and the toolbar button reaches it by `form=`, which is also what makes Enter in a text field save (§4).
- **§9's heading delta beats the template's generic rule.** "H1 stays *Settings* and the section is `<h2>`" moves the whole hierarchy down one step, so cards are `<h3>`. Logged as an adaptation; the template's "each card `<h2>`" is the default the delta overrides, not a contradiction to resolve.
- **`$pageTitle` carries the SECTION, not the screen.** The shell builds both the H1 and the last breadcrumb crumb from it, and §9 needs them to say different things. The screen emits its own H1 and puts "Settings" in as a middle crumb — the only way to satisfy both halves without touching the shell.
- **The posted section is what gets saved, never the section the GET asked for.** They agree in a browser; they would not for a crafted post, and saving a section whose fields the request never carried would blank it.

### THE FINDING, and it is L-041's shape a second time
**`SiteConfig::set()` had been silently dropping `encryption_key_backed_up` since the feature shipped.** The method copies a value only if its name is in a hard-coded allow-list, returns the merged array either way, and that name was absent — so the caller saw its own value come back and had no way to learn the write never happened. Two shipped surfaces wrote it (`settings.php`, `setup-wizard.php`) and `bootstrap.php` reads it as the condition of an **undismissable system error notice**. So every install carried a permanent red "your encryption key has not been backed up" banner on every admin page that **no control in the product could clear**. Found by reading the manager while answering D-095's open check, not by a failing test. Fixed test-first: `SiteConfigSetTest` was seen failing on the absent key before the line existed, and it pins the other direction too (an unknown key is still not stored).

### D-095's open check, answered by reading rather than assuming
`encryption_key_backup` **is** the same duty as entry 6's **Recovery keys** — §6's card already names `config/.encryption_key` and owns the confirm flag — but the two were **not** the same control: Security had the confirmation, Settings had the only affordance that actually yields the key material. So the material moved to Security rather than being deleted (removing shipped capability is not a fidelity decision — D-075, D-079), §9 names no encryption section, and D-090's "two surfaces for one duty" is closed. **The two flags are now written together**: confirming on Security sets `recovery_keys_confirmed` *and* `encryption_key_backed_up`, so one confirmation clears both warnings. It could not have done so before this slice, because the second write did nothing.

### Five further shipped defects closed in passing, each with a driven test
- **The default-language select offered a hard-coded es/en/ca/fr** whatever the language list said, so a site publishing in German could not select German — and saving rewrote the stored value to one of the four. It now offers what the site actually publishes in, and never hides a stored value it does not recognise.
- **A half-filled language row was dropped without a word.** Typing a code and forgetting the name produced a successful save and a language that had vanished. Now reported per row.
- **`smtp_port` was cast to `int`**, so `abc` became port 0 and was stored as a port nothing can connect to — a value that fails at send time, far from the screen that accepted it.
- **The seven devbar toggles rendered only once developer mode was already on**, so they could not be configured in the same save that enables it, and their absence explained nothing. §2 forbids exactly this ("a disabled control is never hidden"). Now always rendered, `disabled` with the reason beside them.
- **The notices card vanished entirely when empty**, so "no notices" and "this card is broken" looked identical. §2's empty-collection row now states it.

### One spec miss of my own, caught by driving
§4 requires "the `required` attribute **plus the word Required in the hint**". The attribute was there and the word was not — found because two validation tests failed for the *wrong* reason: Chromium's own constraint validation refused the submit, so the server-side path they were written to exercise never ran. Both were repaired rather than relaxed: the word was added in all 20 catalogues, and the tests now clear `noValidate` to reach the guarantee *underneath* the browser's first line, with the reason written into the helper. **A test that cannot reach the code it names is not a passing test.**

### One test that moved rather than being deleted
`AdminGateHttpTest::testPrivilegedPostBranchesRefuseTheViewTierRole` failed, correctly: `index.php` no longer has a privileged POST branch, because the branch moved. Deleting the two rows would have quietly shed the coverage, so it moved into `testIndexingIsRefusedToEveryRoleBelowSiteConfigure()`, which pins **both** halves of a move — the control is gated where it landed, **and** the old home no longer accepts the post. Verifying only the first is how a control ends up existing in two places.

### Evidence
PHP **345 / 1651**, 0 skips (was 340 / 1643) · browser tier **319 tests**, settings alone **35**, axe clean on all five sections × two themes **and** on the error state · `keel-verify` **21 checks: 16 pass, 5 warnings** · lint on the touched files **0 errors**, core+admin baseline **158/423 — DOWN from 191/488**, never up (D-025) · `keel-doctor --check` green, 14 rows · `docs/api/INDEX.md` **1012**, row count confirmed to have risen (L-038).
- Alternatives rejected (and why): **keep a Save per card** (the template says one, and eleven Saves is what made the shipped screen a stack of unrelated forms) · **add `encryption_key_backed_up` to `set()` and leave the Settings card where it was** (fixes the write but keeps two surfaces for one duty, which is what D-090 refused) · **delete the encryption card outright** (§9 names no encryption section, but the card holds the only way to obtain the key — deleting it makes "back up your key" an instruction with nothing behind it) · **relax the two validation tests to assert the browser's native bubble** (that asserts Chromium works; the server-side refusal is the guarantee and is what the spec describes) · **drop the two Dashboard rows from the gate test** (sheds real coverage to make a red go away).
- Bounds: `installer/admin/settings.php`, `installer/admin/index.php`, `installer/admin/security.php`, `installer/core/site-config.php`, `installer/admin/assets/css/klytos-components.css` (two new classes), `installer/core/lang/*.json` (20), `tests/E2E/settings.spec.js` (new), `tests/Unit/SiteConfigSetTest.php` (new), `tests/Integration/AdminGateHttpTest.php`, `docs/api/INDEX.md`, `docs/BUILD-SPEC.md`, `docs/05-test-points.md`, `docs/PROGRESS.md`, `docs/roadmap.md`. Extends **D-095**; discharges **D-072 answer 1**'s unbuilt half; closes D-095's open check.

## D-097 — The confidential-data gate blocked two locale files over a UI label, and the fix is a narrower rule, not a bypass
- Date / phase: 2026-08-10 / **Phase 4 Step 4** (raised by entry 9's commit)
- Decision: narrow `.githooks/pre-commit`'s generic `api_key` branch to require **a digit in the value**, rather than commit entry 9 with `--no-verify`.
- **What happened.** The gate refused the commit: `BLOCKED (content): installer/core/lang/nl.json` and `sv.json`. Neither carries a secret. Both carry the translation value for the Gemini key's own FIELD LABEL — `Gemini-API-sleutel` and `Gemini-API-nyckel` — which is 16+ key-like characters with no spaces, so it satisfied the branch's "something value-shaped after the separator".
- **The rule was already half-fixed, and the half is the interesting part.** D-037 had narrowed this branch precisely so the 20 catalogues would stop tripping it — and it worked, in **English**, where "Gemini API key" contains spaces that break the run. Dutch and Swedish compound the same words. **A pattern tested in one language is tested in one language**, which is the same shape as L-035 (a harness that said "both themes" and had only ever measured one).
- **Why not `--no-verify`.** The hook's own comment argues it: "a pattern that fires on documentation and labels trains people to pass `--no-verify`, which is worse than the narrower rule." Bypassing once for a known-safe file is how a gate becomes ceremony — the next block gets the same treatment, and the one after that is real.
- **The loosening, stated rather than glossed.** Requiring a digit means an all-letter API key of 16+ characters would now pass this branch. That is accepted knowingly and it is small: every well-known credential format has its **own** branch — PEM, `Bearer`, `AKIA`, `sk-`, `ghp_`, `xox` — and none of them is touched. What is given up is the generic catch-all for a shape no issuer actually mints, since generated credentials are base16/62/64 and effectively always carry a digit.
- **Proven in both directions rather than reasoned about**, in an isolated scratch repo: `Gemini-API-sleutel` and `Gemini-API-nyckel` now **pass**, while three real-looking credentials are all still **blocked** — a snake_case `api` + `_key` assignment holding 24 mixed alphanumerics, a camelCase `apiKey` holding 20 hex characters, and an AWS-prefixed 20-character id. One direction is half a test (L-010).
- **The gate then caught THIS ENTRY, and it was right to.** The first draft of the line above quoted those three test values literally, which is exactly the "never create the false positive" rule (`references/assistant-config.md`, and SKILL.md's confidential-data section): what the assistant writes into `docs/` must never itself be a secret-shaped string, or the project's own records become uncommittable and the pressure to bypass returns by the back door. Described instead of quoted. **A gate that blocks the note explaining the gate is a gate that works.**
- Alternatives rejected (and why): **`--no-verify` for this commit** (the gate's own comment forecasts exactly where that leads) · **skip `installer/core/lang/*.json` entirely** (blinds the gate to a secret genuinely pasted into a catalogue, and buys nothing the digit rule does not) · **leave it blocking and hand-edit the two labels** (changing correct Dutch and Swedish so a regex is happy — the tool dictating the product).
- Bounds: `.githooks/pre-commit`. Extends **D-037**, which narrowed the same branch for the same reason and did not go far enough.

## D-098 — Entry 37 (x402 settings) is built as five cards, a list nobody could shorten is fixed, and a live provider secret was in the page source
- Date / phase: 2026-08-10 / **Phase 4 Step 4, stage 5 of 6 — batch B, screen 6**
- Decision: build manifest entry 37 as **five cards** — Provider · Wallet and pricing · Licence · Who pays · Logging and statistics — defer the three unbacked ones, and fix the two shipped defects that make the screen's own controls untrustworthy before building anything on top of them.

### The per-screen survey, run against `X402\Config`, `BotDetector` and the provider registry BEFORE the first line
D-089's rule, applied a sixth time, and it disagreed with the stage-wide survey a sixth time. D-088 recorded entry 37's obstruction as "pricing rules and the 402 response body". Both hold. Reading the manager instead of that record found two more things nobody had written down:
- **"Exempt agents" is not merely unbacked — it is INVERTED.** The product's repeatable list is `custom_bot_user_agents`, which **adds** agents to the charged set. There is no subtractive list anywhere, and `known_bot_user_agents` is a class constant with no removal affordance. An agent that is not on the list already passes free, so an *exempt* list only means anything as an override of the built-ins: new gate semantics, not a card. Deferred; `roadmap.md` §0c.
- **§37's delta "the enable/disable control is a switch" has nothing behind it.** There is no global on/off: `x402_enabled` is per PAGE and `x402_default_enabled` per POST TYPE, each surfaced on its own screen, and the `.htaccess` rules are written unconditionally at `build.after`. In the prototype the switch-shaped controls are the three on the same unbacked "Who pays" card ("Charge API routes too", "Serve a free excerpt", "Log refused requests"), none of which maps to a stored setting. So the delta is deferred **with that card**, and every control here is checkbox + Save — which is what §37's own second sentence asks for. Recorded rather than decided silently, in D-090's shape: it is reversible in one block if Design says otherwise.

### THE FINDING — a list that could be added to by anyone and shortened by nobody
`X402\Config::update()` merged with `array_replace_recursive()`, which merges arrays **index by index**: `['A','B']` updated with `['A']` comes back `['A','B']`. Every scalar setting behaved and every LIST setting was one-way. `custom_bot_user_agents` is the list that decides who meets the paywall — `getBotUserAgents()` feeds the detector and `HtaccessWriter` compiles it into the rewrite rules at every build — so an agent added by mistake (a typo matching a real browser's user agent, a partner's crawler added for a trial) could be added by anyone with `site.configure` and removed **through no surface in the product**, this screen or `klytos_x402_update_config` over MCP. Fixed test-first: the red was observed and names the defect (`BetaBot` surviving the save that removed it), and the two "other direction" tests — a partial scalar write, an associative branch that must still merge — **passed from the start**, which is what makes the red worth trusting.

### A SECOND FINDING, and it is a disclosure
The shipped screen rendered the stored provider setting into the `value=` attribute of its input — including the field the Stripe provider declares as `type => 'password'`, its secret key. **So a live payment credential was written into the admin page's HTML on every load**, readable in view-source, in a proxy log, in any cached copy and in a screenshot of the source. A secret field is now never populated; a blank post therefore has to mean *keep*, or every save would wipe the key, and the hint says in words whether a value is stored. Pinned by a test that reads the RAW HTML, because the field works either way and only the source shows it.

### Four more shipped defects closed in passing, each with a driven test
- **The provider could never actually be changed.** The save validated the newly-selected provider's required fields against a form that had rendered the OLD provider's, so the required-field error fired every time. Switching now saves the choice and asks for that provider's settings next, in a `role="status"` line.
- **`default_price_usd` stored whatever was typed**, so `abc` became the default price of every protected page and failed at payment time, far from the field that accepted it — D-096's `smtp_port` defect on another screen.
- **The built-in agent list was `disabled`**, which takes it out of the tab order and out of a screen reader's browse mode. §2's "read-only vs disabled" says `readonly`, mono, selectable — which it now is.
- **A stored network the active provider no longer supports** was silently rewritten to the first option by the next save. It is now still offered — D-096's default-language defect, avoided rather than repeated.

### One defect of my own, found by driving
A refused save re-rendered the **stored** values, so the screen showed `0.075` beside an error saying the price is not a number: the person's work discarded and the message pointing at a value that is in fact fine. Every posted field is now restored for display on a refusal, not only the one that failed — a refusal on one field must not throw away edits to the others. Proven by planting the defect back and watching its own test go red, then restored byte-identically.

### A check that was blind to a whole directory
`installer/lang/x402/` is a **third catalogue root** (`klytos_register_translations( 'klytos-x402', … )`) and `scripts/keel-verify`'s locale-parity check had only ever looked at `installer/core/lang` and `installer/plugins/*/lang`. Four of its twenty catalogues had been two keys short since the module shipped, and nothing could have said so. The check now walks `installer/lang/*` as well: **120 files across 6 sets → 140 across 7**, the count confirmed to have moved (L-038), and proven to FAIL on a gap planted in the newly covered root before being trusted. The four missing translations were written in the same change. Recorded as **L-043**, because the shape — a check that proves the directories it happens to know about — is L-030's, in a check.

### Two adaptations, both logged in `BUILD-SPEC.md` §5.9 (42 and 43)
- **The wallet stays EDITABLE.** §37 says "read-only mono + copy". Mono and copy are built; read-only is not, because this screen is the only affordance in the product that sets the address, so making it read-only would leave the value unreachable from every surface. Removing shipped capability is not a fidelity decision (D-075, D-079, D-091).
- **Two cards the manifest's list does not name are built anyway** — Licence, and the bot lists. They are shipped product, this screen is their only surface, and no other entry claims them: D-091's call on entry 6, for the same reason.

### Evidence
PHP **350 tests / 1660 assertions**, 0 skips (was 345 / 1651) · whole browser tier **353 passing** (was 319), x402 alone **34**, axe clean on 3 states × 2 themes · `keel-verify` **21 checks: 16 pass, 5 warnings**, test-point rows 21 → **22** (row count confirmed to move, L-038) · `keel-doctor --check` green, 14 rows · lint on the touched files **0 errors**, core+admin baseline **151/423 — DOWN from 158/423**, never up (D-025) · `docs/api/INDEX.md` **1012 → 1024** (3 filters + 9 actions), summary counts re-derived by the check · **45 new i18n keys × 20 catalogues** plus the 2 that closed the pre-existing gap, parity PASS at 87 keys each.
